The daily rotate's `try-restart` of the active `Type=simple` recorder is a clean stop then a new start at 09:00Z (journal 10-01 09:00:23→09:00:33, 10-02 09:00:01→09:00:13: `Stopping`, `Stopped`, `Started`, no failure entry). **New in r7 (read-only, 2026-10-03):** every `deploy/systemd/*.timer` declares `AccuracySec=1min` except `breezy-quote-tape-ingest-frequent.timer` (`30sec`); `systemctl --user show` reports the manager default `DefaultTimerAccuracyUSec=1min` and `AccuracyUSec=1min`, `RandomizedDelayUSec=0` on `breezy-quote-tape-rotate.timer` and `breezy-discovery-pull.timer`, so an unpinned timer may elapse up to 60 s after its calendar instant (§3.10 R-e). Re-counted: 19 of the 22 `deploy/systemd/*.service` files are `Type=oneshot`. `docs/core/PROGRESS.md:52` is the ING-2-AMEND2 row (merge `9730f1e`): its owed proof is a post-rotation ingest with cgroup `memory.peak` ≤ 2G, then removal of `zz-memory-containment-TEMPORARY.conf` plus `daemon-reload`; it names no owner and no deadline. **New in r8 (read-only, 2026-10-03, `journalctl --user -o json` over 7 days and `systemctl --user show`):** every systemd-initiated stop of a `Restart=always` daemon (recorder: the 7 daily rotations and one manual stop at 09-28 15:20Z; NWS ingest 3; supervisor 7) logs, under the stopped invocation's `USER_INVOCATION_ID`, a `MESSAGE_ID=de5b426a63be47a7b6ac3eaac82e2f6f` `Stopping` entry with `JOB_TYPE=stop`, then `9d1aaa27d60140bd96365438aad20286` `Stopped`, and no process-exit entry. The one recorder crash in the period (invocation `819e79d6…`, started by the 09-29 rotate at 09:00:14Z, crashed at 09:00:26Z) logged `98e322203f7a4ed290d09fe03c09fe15` (`Main process exited`, `EXIT_CODE=exited`, `EXIT_STATUS=1`), the unit-failed entry `d9b373ed…` and `5eb03494b6584870a536b337290809b3` (`Scheduled restart job, restart counter is at 1`), and no `Stopping` entry. Every entry carries `_BOOT_ID`. `systemctl --user show -p Result,ExecMainStatus,ExecMainCode` describes only the current invocation (recorder after the 10-02 rotate: the new id `cf7d6049…`, `Result=success`, `ExecMainCode=0`), so how an earlier invocation ended must be read from the journal (§3.9, Y1). `/usr/bin/bwrap` is bubblewrap 0.11.1; `~/.local/share/breezy` is on ext4 (so `O_TMPFILE` is supported) and holds `state/`. **New in r9 (AA1 verify-first, 2026-10-03 06:06–06:07Z, systemd 259.5).** Scratch units `claude-aut6r9-*` were created under `~/.config/systemd/user/`, run, `reset-failed`, removed and `daemon-reload`ed; no `breezy-*` unit was touched, and the journal extract is kept in the session scratchpad. (1) A `Type=oneshot` unit with `TimeoutStartSec=5` and two `ExecStart=` lines, each `sleep 4`, finished `Result=success` after **8.05 s** (stage 1 06:06:07.61→11.62, stage 2 11.63→15.64). (2) The transient form, `systemd-run --user --wait -p Type=oneshot -p TimeoutStartSec=5 -p "ExecStart=/bin/sleep 4" /bin/sleep 4`, also succeeded, with a service runtime of 8.046 s. (3) Negative control, with stage 2 sleeping 7 s: `start operation timed out` came 5.19 s after stage 2 began (06:06:28.40→33.59) and 9.25 s into the unit, giving `Result=timeout`, `code=killed, status=15/TERM`. **So `TimeoutStartSec` re-arms for each start command and never bounds the whole start phase.** (4) A first `ExecStart=-` command killed by `timeout -k 1 2` (exit 124) still let stage 2 run. The unit ended `Result=success`, and **no journal entry records the ignored exit status**: there was only `Starting`, the two stdout lines and `Finished` (`39f53479…`). (5) A user unit's stdout lines carry `_SYSTEMD_INVOCATION_ID` and `_SYSTEMD_USER_UNIT`. (6) Read-only: none of the 22 `deploy/systemd/*.service` files has more than one `ExecStart`/`ExecStartPre`/`ExecStartPost` line, and none uses `ExecStopPost`. The per-command finding therefore changes the bound of no existing unit (§3.10 R-d). (7) Coordinator-verified (CA-1): under bwrap, `os.rename` and `os.link` between two separate `--bind` mounts fail with `EXDEV`. **New in r10 (AC1 verify-first, 2026-10-03 06:16–06:17Z, kernel 7.0.0-34, bubblewrap 0.11.1).** Only the session scratchpad's `aut6r10/` directory (a tmpfs) was used, and it was removed afterwards; no `breezy-*` path or unit was touched. A probe script ran under `bwrap --ro-bind / / --bind <scratch>/reg <scratch>/reg --proc /proc --dev /dev --unshare-pid`, the form the coordinator named, and again with the bind narrowed to `<scratch>/reg/demand`, the production shape. (8) `os.open(<demand dir>, O_TMPFILE|O_WRONLY, 0o600)`, a write and `fsync`, then `os.link("/proc/self/fd/<n>", <demand dir>/final.json)` **succeeded in both forms**. The directory listed nothing before the link and only `final.json` after it. The process was pid 2 in its namespace, so `/proc/self` resolves under `--proc /proc` with `--unshare-pid`. (9) A second link onto the existing name failed with `EEXIST`, so nothing is overwritten. (10) A child that opened an `O_TMPFILE`, wrote to it and was SIGKILLed before the link left **no directory entry**. (11) An inode opened with `O_TMPFILE|O_EXCL` cannot be linked (`ENOENT`), so the writer must not pass `O_EXCL`. (12) A link from the demand bind into a second `--bind` (`<scratch>/alerts`) failed with `EXDEV`, which reproduces CA-1; a link into the read-only root failed with `EROFS`. (13) With the bind narrowed to `reg/demand`, both `O_CREAT` and `O_TMPFILE` under `reg/` outside `demand/` failed with `EROFS`. With the coordinator's wider `reg` bind, both **succeeded**. A bind of `registry/` instead of `registry/demand/` is therefore invisible to r9's probes, and AB9's new negative probe catches exactly that (§3.4.2). Limit: the scratch directory was tmpfs. Production `~/.local/share/breezy` is ext4 (statfs magic 0xEF53), which supports `O_TMPFILE`, and WP5b verify-first repeats (8)–(11) on that filesystem before activation. **New in r11 (read-only, 2026-10-03).** (14) `findmnt -no FSTYPE -T ~/.local/share/breezy` → `ext4` (E-7a's filesystem note, re-read). (15) `PRAGMA journal_mode` of `state/exec_polymarket_us.sqlite`, read through a `mode=ro` URI, is `wal`. At 12:47Z the db was 81 920 B and its `-wal` 4 120 032 B, with `-shm` and the 0-byte `.intent.lock` present. It is the only WAL-mode store among AUT-6's inputs; the C5 registry uses a rollback journal (E-7a rule 3). (16) The `98e32220…` process-exit entry's `COMMAND=` field holds the command type, never the argv. The four newest entries (`breezy-portfolio-roi`, two `run-*` transients and r9's two-line scratch unit) all read `COMMAND=ExecStart`, so `COMMAND=` cannot tell two `ExecStart=` lines apart (§3.4.2, LOW-r11-1). (17) Importing `breezy.adapters.polymarket_us.leg_prices` runs the package `__init__`, which imports the venue HTTP client, data client and credential loaders (`src/breezy/adapters/polymarket_us/__init__.py:153-215`), while `leg_prices.py` itself imports only `decimal` and `typing` (`:26-27`). A pure move to `domain` has a precedent: `src/breezy/domain/instrument_leg.py`, to which the adapter's `symbology` delegates. **New in r12 (AF1 closure measurement, read-only, 2026-10-03; scripts and outputs in the session scratchpad `aut6r12/`; nothing installed, no `uv` or `pip`).** (18) **Method.** Interpreter `/home/jon/breezy/.venv/bin/python`, `PYTHONPATH=/home/jon/breezy/src`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. grimp 3.15 is installed and was run first. It reports 33 `breezy.adapters` modules in the closure of **every** module, `breezy.domain.instrument_leg` included, through the root package's `main()`, whose `from breezy.runtime import cli` is function-local (`src/breezy/__init__.py:13`) and never runs on import. grimp counts function-local imports, so it cannot answer a runtime-closure question in this repo. The measurement therefore uses a static walk of module-level imports: `if TYPE_CHECKING:` blocks and function bodies are skipped, both branches of a top-level `if` or `try` are kept, and every ancestor package `__init__` is added, because Python executes it. **Positive control:** for six modules the walk equals a fresh-process `sys.modules` import. `trial_day_latch` gives 33 adapter modules plus the `breezy.strategy.current_rung_hold` package; `trade_supervisor` gives 33; `instrument_leg`, `runtime.health`, `trade_supervisor_core` and `halt_detector` give 0. ### 0.1 r14 measurements (verify-first, continued from the Code baseline row)

**New in r14 (verify-first, 2026-10-03 16:10–16:15Z, systemd 259.5, bubblewrap 0.11.1).**
- **Method.** The runs used scratch transient units `claude-aut6r14-a` … `-f` and a scratch `OnFailure=` target, which was linked into `~/.config/systemd/user` only for the run. Afterwards every unit was stopped and `reset-failed`, the target was unlinked, and `daemon-reload` was run. No `breezy-*` unit was touched. The sender script and the journal extracts are in the session scratchpad `aut6r14/`.
- **(23) Read-only baseline.** `systemctl --user show breezy-quote-tape.service` reports:
  - `Type=simple`, `Restart=always`, `NotifyAccess=none`;
  - `WatchdogUSec=0`, `TimeoutStartUSec=1min 30s`, `TimeoutAbortUSec=2min`;
  - `LimitCORE=infinity`.
  - Its unit file sets `StartLimitIntervalSec=0` on purpose. The comment at `deploy/systemd/breezy-quote-tape.service:20-45` explains why: on 2026-09-09 the start limit was exhausted, and capture stayed dead for 6.7 h of tape that cannot be re-fetched. The file also sets `RestartSec=30`, `RestartSteps=4` and `RestartMaxDelaySec=480`.
  - Other start limits: `breezy-nws-ingest` declares `StartLimitBurst=3` per 300 s, and the supervisor 5 per 3600 s.
  - No `sd_notify` exists anywhere. No file under `src/`, `scripts/`, `deploy/` or the installed `nautilus_trader` contains `sd_notify`, `NOTIFY_SOCKET` or `WATCHDOG=1`, and the venv has no `sdnotify` or `systemd` package. Positive control: the same grep finds `Restart=always` in the recorder unit.
- **(24) Experiment A: the watchdog restart.**
  - **Setup.** A `Type=notify` Python sender sends one stdlib `AF_UNIX` datagram to `$NOTIFY_SOCKET` for `READY=1`. It then sends `WATCHDOG=1` once a second, but only while its counter advances, and the counter freezes after 4 s. Unit settings: `WatchdogSec=3`, `Restart=on-watchdog`, `RestartSec=1`, `StartLimitBurst=3`, `StartLimitIntervalSec=120`, and `OnFailure=` pointing at a scratch target.
  - **Every invocation logged the same sequence:**
    - `Watchdog timeout (limit 3s)!` about 6.2 s after start;
    - `Killing process … with signal SIGABRT`;
    - the process-exit entry `98e32220…` with `code=dumped, status=6/ABRT`;
    - the unit-failed entry `d9b373ed…` with `UNIT_RESULT=watchdog`;
    - `5eb03494…` `Scheduled restart job, restart counter is at n`, and a new `InvocationID`.
  - **At the start limit.** After the third watchdog failure the journal shows `Start request repeated too quickly` and the job-failed entry `be02cf68…` with `JOB_RESULT=failed`. The unit then stays `ActiveState=failed`, with `Result=watchdog` (not `start-limit-hit`) and `NRestarts=3`.
  - The sender journaled 9 `ping_withheld` lines.
- **(25) `OnFailure=` fires on every watchdog failure, not only at start-limit exhaustion.**
  - In (24) the target ran four times: three times while a restart was pending, and once at exhaustion.
  - **Experiment F** used an `OnFailure=` template that runs `systemctl --user show <unit>`. While a restart was pending it read `ActiveState=activating SubState=auto-restart`, with `NRestarts` 0 and then 1. At exhaustion it read `ActiveState=failed SubState=failed` and `NRestarts=2`.
  - So a notifier can tell the two cases apart from the unit state alone.
- **(26) Negative control B.** The same sender with no stall ran for 44 s. It stayed `active`, with `NRestarts=0` and no watchdog entry.
- **(27) Experiments C and D: bwrap and `NotifyAccess`.**
  - **C.** The sender ran under `bwrap --ro-bind / / --dev /dev --proc /proc --unshare-pid --new-session --die-with-parent --tmpfs ~/.config` with the default `NotifyAccess=main`. It was pid 2 in its namespace and sent `READY=1` with no error. systemd ignored the message, because the main PID is bwrap, and the start failed `Result=timeout` at `TimeoutStartSec=8`.
  - **D.** The same unit with `NotifyAccess=all` reached `active` and ran for 36 s with no watchdog entry. The socket `/run/user/1000/systemd/notify` is reachable through the read-only root bind.
  - **So a wrapped `Type=notify` unit needs `NotifyAccess=all`.**
- **(28) Experiment E: `Restart=always`.** This is the D shape behind `timeout -k 2 600`, with `Restart=always` instead of `on-watchdog`, and the counter frozen after 4 s. systemd sent SIGABRT to every process in the cgroup (`timeout`, both bwrap processes and python), restarted the unit, and hit the start limit after the second failure. **`Restart=always` restarts on a watchdog timeout, just as `on-watchdog` does.**
- **(29) `%n` in a transient unit.** A transient unit's `OnFailure=` value does not expand `%n` (`Invalid unit name`), so the scratch test names the instance literally. Deployed unit files do expand it.

## 1. Goal state

### 1.1 Score-3 criterion (README, verbatim)

> **Score-3 criterion.**
> - Detectors cover every live family for:
>   - data freshness (tape, NBP and observation feeds)
>   - forecast and feature distribution drift
>   - calibration drift on live labels
>   - fill-rate and slippage drift
>   - venue fee and shape drift
>   - permit, process and log liveness
>   - unit health
> - Every detector maps to an action — node-local entry veto (transient conditions, auto-clearing; ARCH C5), halt, demote (through AUT-5), self-heal or alert — with delivery proven.
> - No unit fails as a matter of expected behaviour.
>
> **Live proof:** 7 consecutive days with zero unexplained failed units, plus at least one real or injected drift event per action class that produces its mapped action through the live path.

### 1.2 ARCH Rev 9.2 §10 obligation (verbatim) and where each part is met

> **AUT-6:** the detector catalogue per kind, split `NODE_LOCAL` / `VERDICT`, each with horizon, policy action class and escalation path; the intraday producer and its HEALTH verdicts cited by ATTEST; `deliver_with_proof`, its per-attempt records (P6-6), the node outbox worker and deadline (W9), and the RED test proving G26; the migration of every CRITICAL site to it and the two-day `alerts_undeliverable` read (W13); the canary, its retry cadence, the receiver's absence rule and its live proof; the six failing units (adding `run-p814078`); `TimeoutStartSec` for every oneshot study (G32); the effective-`MemoryMax` check (P6-10); the SELF_HEAL executor (P6-7); `SELF_HEAL_RESTARTABLE_UNITS` membership, the persisted per-unit restart cap (W11) and the argv-only restart call site; the `aut6.health` producer pin and health heartbeat (P6-13); the INTEGRITY-floor demand writer (P6-12); `try-restart`, the trading-day restart window and launch-window deferral (P1-10, P1-12); the `fee_schedule` DRIFT verdict (P7-11); V1, V3, V8, V9, V14, U3, U6, U11, U12 (durable outbox; proof-bearing alerts with `severity`, `attempt_kind`, `drill`; `--no-block`; demand slots; memory sum).

Nautilus 1.231.0 has no `sd_notify` (§0.1 (23)); its `Actor` clock timer is the native host for the periodic ping (AUT-1 r9). | **Reuse systemd.** AUT-6 builds no gate and no sender: AUT-1 r9's `recorder_watchdog.py` holds both (§3.5). AUT-6 owns the SELF_HEAL contract (the directive rules and their config test, the gate contract test, #21 and `watchdog_disarmed`) and the notifier's classification of the unit state. It builds no restart call site, no records and no cap counter. |
| `sd_notify` sender **(r14)** | The venv has no `sdnotify` or `systemd` package, and none may be installed (no `uv` or `pip`). The protocol is one `AF_UNIX` `SOCK_DGRAM` send of `READY=1` or `WATCHDOG=1` to `$NOTIFY_SOCKET` (a leading `@` means the abstract namespace), exactly as the scratch sender did (§0.1 (24)). | **Consume AUT-1 r9's** `recorder_watchdog.sd_notify`. AUT-6 builds none. |
| Detector→action map **(r4)** | ARCH §4.2: the AUT-5 ruling's `autonomy-policy/v1` block. With no verifiable ruling the engine accepts a `no_policy_ruling` FAIL only for DEMOTE or HALT, the class taken from `pins.DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS` (C4 Engine acceptance, P4-10). | **Consume both.** AUT-6 proposes its `DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS` rows (§3.3.1). |
| Restrictive-demand write **(r4)** | ARCH C5 "Second writer, restrictive only" (P6-12, V9, U13): producers in `pins.DEMAND_WRITER_PRODUCER_IDS` (initially `aut6.intraday`) write through the same `demand/v1` writer; reserved slots (`DEMAND_INTEGRITY_RESERVED` ≥ 8); only the engine archives (W16). | **Reuse the AUT-5 writer**; AUT-6 builds only the INTEGRITY-floor call site and its pre-write validation (WP5b), run in a bwrap'd demand stage that binds only `registry/demand/` and `evidence/alerts` (E-7 rule 2; r8), from r11 as the shared wrapper's `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday#demand` row (E-7a, §3.1.1). |
| `fee_schedule` verdict **(r4, P7-11)** | `FeeDriftProbeActor` probes every 2 h (`DEFAULT_FEE_DRIFT_PROBE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `src/breezy/strategy/current_rung_hold/fee_drift_probe.py:177`); `is_fee_verified` is in-node memory with a 4 h staleness bound (`:184,543-584`), which nothing outside the node can read (ARCH G39). ARCH C5 says the verdict is built "from the probe's `DetectorEvent`s" (C1, AUT-1's writer). | **Build one VERDICT detector** over C1 `DetectorEvent(detector=fee_schedule)`; reuse the 4 h staleness value as its freshness rule. |
| Memory sum **(r4, V14)** | ARCH §5.2: `MemoryMax=14G`/`MemoryHigh=12G` for one studies-flock holder; own-lock autonomy units ≤ 4G; ingest counted by its effective limit; sum against `MemAvailable`. `systemctl --user show -p MemoryMax,MemoryHigh,MemoryPeak` gives effective values including drop-ins; `/proc/meminfo` gives `MemAvailable`. | **Reuse the property reads**; one pure check (WP3). |

---

## 3. Design

### 3.1 Placement (import-linter layers, `pyproject.toml:74-101`)

Layers: `app > analysis > strategy > runtime > adapters > ingest > persistence|registry|normalize > settlement > domain`; live packages never import `breezy.analysis`.

| Module | Layer | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/detector_catalog.py` | persistence | `DetectorSpec` (frozen: `id`, `detector_class`, `kind` `NODE_LOCAL`\|`VERDICT`\|`IN_NODE_EXEC_HALT`, `verdict_kind` `DRIFT`\|`HEALTH`, `cause_class`, `proposed_action_class`, `floor_action_class`, `severity` `CRITICAL`\|`WARN`, `halt_exempt`, `horizon_s`, `escalates_to`, `producer_id`, `unknown_streak_max`, `live_proof_class` `natural`\|`drill`\|`gate_only`); `CATALOG` (literal only); `REQUIRED_DETECTOR_CLASSES`; `DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS_PROPOSAL` (literal; the rows AUT-5 copies into `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/pins.py`, §3.3.1). |
| `src/breezy/runtime/alert_delivery.py` | runtime | `DeliveryProof`, `deliver_with_proof`, `DeliveryRecordWriter`, `AlertOutbox` (`write_entry`, `claim`, `reclaim_stale`, `complete`), `drain_outbox(drainer, …)`, `JournalingWebhookAlertSink`, `OffLoopAlertSink`, `resolve_node_alert_sink`, `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS`. |
| `src/breezy/runtime/autonomy_node_detectors.py` | runtime | `PermitLapsedDetector`, `AlertsUndeliverableDetector`. |
| `src/breezy/runtime/unit_health.py` | runtime | `UnitObservation`, `UnitFailureClass`, `observe_units`, `read_failed_invocations`, `classify`, `UNIT_RESULT_CLASS`, `UnitHealthJournal`, `memory_budget_check`. |
| `src/breezy/runtime/monitor_watch.py` | runtime | Meta-detectors #26–#28, `TIMER_MAX_INTERVAL_S`, `TIMER_RETIRED_BY_RULING`, the heartbeat reader and writer. |
| `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/detector_catalog.py` (r14 addition) | persistence | `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS: Final = ("breezy-quote-tape.service",)` and `WATCHDOG_RESTART_SET: Final`. AUT-6 builds no gate or sender: the recorder's are AUT-1 r9's `src/breezy/adapters/polymarket_us/recorder_watchdog.py` (§3.5). |
| `src/breezy/runtime/autonomy_health_cli.py` | runtime | `breezy-autonomy-health`: unit health, meta-detectors, heartbeat and daily rollup. Unit health includes #21, the record of each systemd watchdog restart (r14). **(r14)** It makes no state-changing call. C4 producer `aut6.health`. |
| `src/breezy/runtime/autonomy_canary_cli.py`, `src/breezy/runtime/alert_redeliver_cli.py` | runtime | Canary, outbox drain. (r14: the drill-probe CLI is deleted.) |
| `src/breezy/analysis/nbp_drift.py` | analysis | The moved pure predicates. |
| `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/drift_fq.py` | analysis | FQ `VERDICT` detectors. |
| `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/fill_integrity.py` | analysis | #15: taker-IOC fill selection, leg normalisation through `leg_prices`, the book-at-fill read through `order_book_depth10`. Imports the leg table from `breezy.domain.leg_prices` (r11, E-7a: moved there unchanged by WP6, and the adapter module delegates), so the evaluate stage's closure reaches no `breezy.adapters` module. |
| `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/fee_schedule.py` **(r4)** | analysis | #32 over C1 `DetectorEvent(detector=fee_schedule)`. |
| `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/integrity_floor.py` **(r4: no longer held)** | analysis | Validates and writes the INTEGRITY `demand/v1` file through AUT-5's writer (§3.3.3). **(r8, E-7)** It is the entry point of the bwrap'd demand stage `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday --stage demand` (§3.4.2). Its import closure reaches no `breezy.adapters` module (`test_demand_stage_has_no_import_path_to_venue_adapters`), so it never imports `fill_integrity`. |
| `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/producer_intraday.py`, `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/producer_daily.py` | analysis | C4 producers `aut6.intraday`, `aut6.daily`. |
| `src/breezy/domain/family_halt.py` **(r12, AF1)** | domain | Moved unchanged from `trial_day_latch.py`: `read_family_halt_rows_readonly`, `decode_family_halt_state`, `family_halt_key`, `classify_legacy`, `FamilyHaltReading`, `MalformedHaltValue`, `LEGACY_FAMILY_HALT_KEY`, `FAMILY_HALT_KEY_PREFIX`, `FAMILY_ID_PATTERN`, `LEGACY_HALT_ATTRIBUTED_FAMILY_ID`, `LEGACY_HALT_PINNED_SHA256`, `HALT_CLEARED_MARKER` (§3.1.2). Stdlib only. |
| `src/breezy/domain/leg_prices.py` (r11) | domain | The pure leg table and its inverse, moved unchanged (WP6). |
| `src/breezy/domain/quantile_density.py`, `src/breezy/domain/location_correction.py` **(r12, AF1)** | domain | Moved unchanged from `breezy.strategy.ladder_ev` (WP5). Their imports are stdlib, scipy and each other. |
| `src/breezy/runtime/flock_holders.py` **(r12, AF1)** | runtime | Moved unchanged from `trade_supervisor.py`: `count_lock_holders`, `iter_flock_holders`, `proc_state_char`, `DEFAULT_PROC_LOCKS_PATH`, `FLOCK_LOCK_TYPE`, `NODE_LOG_NAME_RE` (WP6). Stdlib only. |
| `src/breezy/analysis/forecast_calibration_leg.py` **(r12, AF1)** | analysis | `evaluate_calibration_leg` and its same-file dependencies, moved from `scripts/analysis/forecast_conditional_scoring.py` together with `RELIABILITY_BUCKET_EDGES` (WP7). |
| `src/breezy/strategy/forecast_quantile_ladder/plugin.py` (AUT-1 creates; AUT-6 adds one tuple) | strategy | `NODE_PLUGINS[forecast_quantile_ladder].drift_detectors`. |

Before placing any module, grep the containment tests (L-46, L-54): `tests/unit/test_*containment*`, `tests/unit/test_alerts_env_deploy.py`, `tests/unit/test_alert_egress.py`.

**Read-only closure and write authority (r8, E-7 rules 3 and 4; a lint from r11, E-7a rule 4).** From r11 every AUT-6 unit runs under bwrap (§3.1.1), so each unit's write scope is OS-enforced by its `AUTONOMY_BWRAP_TABLE` row. The closure test below is a defence-in-depth lint. It must be non-vacuous, carry its positive controls and be green at build, but its completeness is no longer a READY criterion (E-7a rule 4). Mount directives remain configuration on this host. `tests/unit/test_autonomy_readonly_closure.py::test_aut6_closures_read_only_outside_one_writer_rows` walks the import closure of every AUT-6 entry point (both intraday stages, the daily and health producers, canary, redeliver, check, the node sinks and the NODE_LOCAL detector objects that the watch actor loads). It fails on a write-mode `open`, `sqlite3.connect` without `mode=ro`, `SqliteStateStore(`, `subprocess`, `os.system`, `ctypes` or `importlib` unless that site is in the entry point's row of `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY`. The r9 additions (AA6) are listed after the table. **r10 (AC6, coordinator ruling) turns the whole check into an allowlist**, also stated after the table. That table is a literal in `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/detector_catalog.py`, and its rows go into the programme's single write-authority table when the owner of that table files it. Every SQLite reader also issues `PRAGMA query_only=ON`. The rows:

| Entry point | Writes (paths under `~/.local/share/breezy/`) | Process calls allowed |
|---|---|---|
| intraday, evaluate stage (bwrap row `#evaluate`, r11) | `derived/verdicts/**`, `evidence/alerts/**`; `cache/aut6_intraday_exec_snapshot/` through the E-8 snapshot helper only, judged under AUT-5's table (r11, AE5) | none |
| intraday, demand stage (bwrap) | `registry/demand/<venue>/` through AUT-5's `demand/v1` writer only (r10: the writer's `O_TMPFILE` link and venue `mkdir`, AC1 and AC4, are judged under AUT-5's table; r11, AE2: the fallback and its sweep are removed); `evidence/alerts/**`, including the `stage_status/demand/` retention unlinks (r11, LOW-r11-8) | none |
| daily | `derived/verdicts/**` (incl. `_aut6_daily_skips/`), `evidence/alerts/**`; `cache/aut6_daily_exec_snapshot/` through the E-8 snapshot helper only (r11, AE5) | `systemctl --user show` (read) |
| health | `evidence/unit_health/**`, `derived/verdicts/**`, `evidence/alerts/**`; the `buildside_restart/` marker through `--mark-buildside-restart` only | `systemctl --user show`/`list-units`/`list-timers`, `journalctl` (read). **(r14)** No state-changing call: `restart_unit` and `evidence/selfheal/**` are deleted. |
| canary, redeliver, check, node sinks | `evidence/alerts/**` (canary also `armed.json`) | none |
| bwrap self-probe (shared code, run first by every AUT-6 wrapped entry point; r11, AE3) | only the probe files named in `AUT6_SELF_PROBE_PATHS`, a `Final` literal derived from AUT-6's `AUTONOMY_BWRAP_TABLE` rows (§3.1.1) | none |
| `breezy-autonomy-failed@` notifier, wrapped line and fallback line (r11, E-7a; r14: also the `OnFailure=` target of every `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` member) | `evidence/alerts/**`, incl. the `notify/<YYYY-MM-DD>/` `attempted` and `delivered` markers and, on the wrapped line only, the retention unlinks of `notify/` directories older than 8 days (r12, AF4, LOW-r12-3) | `systemctl --user show` (read) |

A fixture module with a write-mode `open` outside its row fails the test (positive control). `test_aut6_write_authority_table_lists_every_aut6_unit` asserts one row per AUT-6 entry point. Against same-uid processes outside every bwrap row (E-7a rule 5: the node and its sinks, operator CLIs such as `breezy-check-alerts` and `--mark-buildside-restart`, and the notifier's unwrapped fallback line), the one-writer property rests on this lint plus ARCH's stated residual and is never described as enforced (E-7 rule 4).

**Read allowlist, rows and scope (r10, AC6, coordinator ruling; replaces r9's AA6 forbidden-construct denylist).** r9 listed forbidden constructs, and every review round added more. r10 inverts the check: in a non-writer closure only named read-only calls are permitted, and every other call fails.
- **Permitted I/O calls in a non-writer closure.** These are held in the literal `AUT6_READ_ALLOWLIST` in `detector_catalog.py`:
  - `open` with a literal mode of `"r"` or `"rb"`, or with no mode argument, which is read mode (r11, LOW-r11-5);
  - `os.open` whose literal flags are `os.O_RDONLY`, alone or OR-ed with any of `os.O_CLOEXEC`, `os.O_DIRECTORY` and `os.O_NOFOLLOW` (r11, LOW-r11-5: this admits `O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC` and the drill-marker read of §3.4.8), and `fcntl.flock` on such a descriptor (the lock files, §3.1.1);
  - `sqlite3.connect` on a `file:…?mode=ro` URI, followed by `PRAGMA query_only=ON` (E-7 rule 3);
  - `os.stat`, `os.scandir` and `os.listdir`;
  - the shared read helpers, each named in the literal (for example the C5 resolver read, AUT-1's C1 readers and `read_family_halt_rows_readonly`) and each judged under its owner's table.
  - **Read companions (r10 reading, stated for review).** `os.read`, `os.fstat` and `os.close` on a descriptor the closure opened `O_RDONLY`, and the pure `os.path` string functions, are also in the literal. None of them can create, modify or delete a file.
- **Everything else fails.** The test fails on any other call to a builtin, to `os`, `io` or `shutil`, to a `pathlib` write method, to `subprocess` or `multiprocessing`, or to an `asyncio` subprocess or exec function.
  - Builtins that do no I/O are listed in a second literal, `PURE_BUILTINS` (for example `len`, `sorted`, `isinstance`, and `print` to stdout). A builtin missing from both literals fails; it never passes by default.
  - A call that cannot be matched to the allowlist also fails. That covers a non-literal mode or flags argument, `getattr`-style access to these modules, `__import__`, `importlib`, `exec` and `eval`.
- **Writers are limited to their rows.** A writer entry point may make write calls only at the sites its `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY` row names, each with a path constant (next bullet).
  - The argv lists in the "Process calls allowed" column are the only process calls.
  - In the demand stage, writes under `registry/demand/` go only through AUT-5's `demand/v1` writer, which is judged under AUT-5's table. That includes the `O_TMPFILE` link and the venue `mkdir` (AC1, AC4); r11 removes the fallback sweep (AE2).
- **Why.** Every r9 denylist construct still fails, because none is on the allowlist. A call that no list anticipated, such as `os.truncate`, `os.chmod` or `os.utime`, now fails too, so the list stops growing.
- **Programme.** AUT-6 applies this rule to its own closures. AUT-1 (`test_aut1_non_writer_closures_are_read_only`) and AUT-5 adopt it as a build item in their WP briefs (§5). AUT-6 edits neither plan.
- **Rows are path-literal or constant.** Every allowed write site's path argument must resolve in the AST to a string literal, or to a module-level `Final` constant named in that entry point's row. It may be joined with literal segments or with a `verdict_id`, `ts_ns`, date or invocation-id component. A path computed from the environment, `argv` or a file read fails, even in a row module.
- **Scope matches AUT-1's boundary** (`test_aut1_non_writer_closures_are_read_only`).
  - The walk follows the import closure but judges only modules under the AUT-6 globs:
    - `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/detector_catalog.py`;
    - `src/breezy/runtime/` `alert_delivery.py`, `autonomy_node_detectors.py`, `unit_health.py`, `monitor_watch.py`, `autonomy_health_cli.py`, `autonomy_canary_cli.py`, `alert_redeliver_cli.py`, `check_alerts_cli.py`. (r14: `self_heal.py` and `self_heal_probe_cli.py` are deleted.)
    - `src/breezy/analysis/nbp_drift.py` and `src/breezy/analysis/autonomy/*.py`.
  - Each module under those globs must appear in `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY`, as a writer or as a non-writer, so a new module must be classified.
  - A `breezy.*` module owned by another area is checked by its owner's test under its owner's table and never re-judged under AUT-6's. Examples are AUT-5's `demand/v1` writer and `pins.py`, and AUT-1's capture modules.
  - Stdlib and third-party modules are judged only at their AUT-6 call sites.
- **Non-vacuity floor (r11, E-7a rule 4; the review's M2).** The literal `AUT6_LINT_MIN_JUDGED_SITES` holds, per entry point, the number of call sites the lint judged at its first green build. The lint fails if any entry point judges fewer, and an entry point with zero judged sites always fails. The positive controls below stay.
- **Tests:** `::test_aut6_lint_is_non_vacuous_per_entry_point` and `::test_aut6_read_allowlist_accepts_rdonly_cloexec_and_bare_open` (r11), `::test_aut6_closure_forbids_extended_write_and_dynamic_code_constructs` (kept: parametrised over each r9 construct, each a fixture module outside its row, now failing because it is off the allowlist), **`::test_aut6_non_writer_io_calls_are_on_read_allowlist` (r10, AC6)** (positive controls `os.truncate`, `os.chmod`, `os.utime` and `Path.write_text` in a non-writer fixture each fail; every allowlisted read passes), **`::test_aut6_read_allowlist_and_pure_builtins_are_literal` (r10, AC6)**, `::test_aut6_write_rows_are_path_literal_or_constant`, `::test_aut6_closure_scope_is_aut6_globs_and_every_module_classified`.

#### 3.1.1 bwrap rows (r11, E-7a; AE1, AE3, AE5)

Every AUT-6 unit runs under the shared wrapper `deploy/systemd/breezy-autonomy-bwrap` (E-7a rule 1). AUT-5 owns the wrapper, `AUTONOMY_BWRAP_TABLE` and the self-probe code (E-7; E-7a consumption row). AUT-6 supplies the rows below and consumes the rest. `<wrapper>` in unit lines means the wrapper's installed path as AUT-5 files it: a read-only file outside every bind.

Every row has `--ro-bind / /`, `--dev /dev`, `--proc /proc`, `--new-session`, `--die-with-parent`, and the E-7 credentials tmpfs `--tmpfs ~/.config`, with no re-bind under `~/.config/breezy`. The webhook URL arrives through systemd's `EnvironmentFile=`, which is loaded before the wrapper starts. Paths are under `~/.local/share/breezy/` unless shown otherwise.

| Row (`<unit>` or `<unit>#<stage>`) | `--bind` (read-write) | `--unshare-pid` | Extra read-only re-bind | Lock (pre-created; opened `O_RDONLY\|O_CLOEXEC`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday#evaluate` | `derived/verdicts`, `evidence/alerts`, `cache/aut6_intraday_exec_snapshot` | **no** (E-7a rule 2: `/proc/locks` and `/proc/<pid>/stat` for #6) | none | `derived/verdicts/.aut6-intraday.lock` |
| `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday#demand` | `registry/demand`, `evidence/alerts` | yes | none | none (runs after the evaluate stage in the same invocation) |
| `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily` | `derived/verdicts`, `evidence/alerts`, `cache/aut6_daily_exec_snapshot` | yes | `~/.config/systemd/user` (for #24; it holds no credential) | `breezy-studies.lock` (existing, shared with the studies) |
| `breezy-autonomy-health` | `evidence/unit_health`, `derived/verdicts`, `evidence/alerts` (r14: `evidence/selfheal` removed) | **no** (E-7a rule 2: `/proc/<pid>` RSS for the §3.9 add-back) | none | `evidence/unit_health/.health.lock` |
| `breezy-autonomy-alert-redeliver` | `evidence/alerts` | yes | none | `evidence/alerts/.redeliver.lock` |
| `breezy-autonomy-canary` | `evidence/alerts` | yes | none | `evidence/alerts/.canary.lock` |
| `breezy-autonomy-failed@` (the `OnFailure=` target of `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily` and, from r14, of every `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` member) | `evidence/alerts` | yes | none | none |

- **Row key.** The wrapper resolves its row from its first argument and fails closed on an unknown name, with no default row (E-7a rule 1). Each `ExecStart=` passes its own unit name, or `<unit name>#<stage>` for the intraday unit's two stages, whose bind sets differ (stated reading, §11 O-6). `test_every_aut6_unit_execstart_and_onfailure_target_goes_through_wrapper` checks that every AUT-6 `ExecStart=` and the `OnFailure=` target go through the wrapper with a row name that begins with that unit's own name. `test_aut6_bwrap_rows_exact_binds_and_unshare_pid_exceptions` checks each row's bind set exactly, and that only `#evaluate` and `breezy-autonomy-health` lack `--unshare-pid`. `test_aut6_proc_consumers_rows_drop_unshare_pid` is E-7a rule 2's test for AUT-6: a fixture row with `unshare_pid=true` whose closure references `/proc/locks` fails.
- **Command order and locks (E-7a, E-9).** The order is `timeout -k K T` → wrapper → CLI. Each lock is taken in-process with `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on a lock file that was pre-created at activation and is opened `O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC` (E-7a's second lock option). Contention keeps each unit's `SKIPPED lock_held` exit-0 path. A missing lock file prints `<UNIT> INTEGRITY lock_file_missing`, sends a CRITICAL through §3.6 and exits 3, which is a #22 finding (`test_aut6_lock_files_opened_rdonly_and_missing_is_integrity`).
- **Bind sources.** bwrap refuses a missing bind source, so WP activation creates each one, and each `.bwrap_probe/` subdirectory, with `install -d -m 0700`, one path component per argument, parent first. The wrapper validates every bind as absolute, existing and free of `..` (E-7a rule 1).
- **Same-bind rule (CA-1, E-7a).** Every write keeps its temporary file and its final name inside one bind of its row. Verdicts, the heartbeat and the stage status stay in `derived/verdicts`; records, outbox entries, claims and markers in `evidence/alerts`; unit-health records in `evidence/unit_health`; snapshot copies in the row's cache bind. `test_aut6_rows_keep_temp_and_final_in_one_bind_with_exdev_control` checks every row's write sites statically. Its positive control is a cross-bind `os.link` failing `EXDEV` under real bwrap.
- **Self-probe paths come from the table (r11, AE3).** Every AUT-6 row runs the shared self-probe first. Its paths are a pure function of the row:
  - **Negatives.** `state/` and `registry/`, plus the parent of every bind whose parent is not itself bound: for example `derived/` for `derived/verdicts`, `evidence/` for `evidence/alerts` and `cache/` for a cache bind. Each probe is `O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL` on `.aut6_bwrap_probe_<invocation_id>` and must fail with exactly `EROFS`, after an `os.stat` precondition that the directory exists and is owned by this uid. On the demand row the `registry/` negative is r10's AB9 probe. On every other row it shows the row cannot reach the registry.
  - **Positives.** These must succeed. Every `evidence/` or `cache/` bind gets `O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL` under its `.bwrap_probe/` subdirectory, followed by an unlink. `registry/demand/` and `derived/verdicts/` get `O_WRONLY|O_TMPFILE`, which creates no directory entry, so neither the watch actor nor a C4 listing ever sees a probe name (O-5).
  - **Constants.** The resolved paths for every AUT-6 row form the literal `AUT6_SELF_PROBE_PATHS: Final` in `detector_catalog.py`, which is the self-probe's own row in `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY` (§3.1). `test_aut6_self_probe_paths_are_constants_derived_from_bwrap_table` asserts that the literal equals the derivation from `AUTONOMY_BWRAP_TABLE` for every AUT-6 row, so a changed bind cannot leave a stale probe.
  - **Outcomes.** These are r9's and r10's, unchanged. An unexpected success is closed, unlinked and INTEGRITY (`probe_residue=1` if the unlink fails), and any other errno is a probe failure. On failure the unit prints `<UNIT> INTEGRITY bwrap_probe_failed direction=<negative|negative_registry|positive_<bind>>`, sends a CRITICAL `bwrap_self_probe_failed` through §3.6 and exits 3 with no other write. `test_every_aut6_row_self_probe_both_directions_under_real_bwrap` runs each row's probe in the real namespace, and for each row a widened-bind fixture fails it.
- **WAL reads (r11, AE5, E-7a rule 3).** A wrapped AUT-6 unit never opens a live WAL-mode database in place.
  - **How.** Every such read goes through the shared E-8 snapshot helper (AUT-5's `exec_snapshot`) into the row's cache bind. The helper copies the db, `-wal` and `-journal` (never `-shm`) under a fingerprint-stable retry and recovers the copy. AUT-6 reads the copy `mode=ro` with `PRAGMA query_only=ON`, and the copy is deleted at the end of the use.
  - **Call (r12, E-8a).** The evaluate stage calls `exec_snapshot(cache_dir=~/.local/share/breezy/cache/aut6_intraday_exec_snapshot, take_flock=False, lock_path=None)`. The daily producer makes the same call with `cache/aut6_daily_exec_snapshot`. If AUT-5's signature requires a path, AUT-6 passes the exec store's `.intent.lock` path, and in this mode the helper never opens it; AUT-5's per-mode test pins that. `take_flock=True` is reserved for AUT-5's 16:45Z pre-launch pass (E-8a). `test_aut6_exec_snapshot_calls_pass_take_flock_false_and_own_cache_dir` is an AST check: every AUT-6 call site passes the literal `False` and a cache-dir constant from its own row.
  - **Which stores.** Today the only WAL store AUT-6 reads is the exec store (§0 (15)). It is read by the intraday evaluate stage (#19, #30, §3.13) and by the daily producer (#14, #20, §3.13). The C5 registry uses a rollback journal and is unaffected.
  - **Advisory (r12, E-8a; replaces r11's stated reading O-6 (ii)).** These are node-up reads that rely on the fingerprint-stable retry alone, so by E-8a their results are advisory: they never count toward H and never authorise a widening. AUT-6 uses them only in two ways, and neither is a widening:
    - the halted-by-design `PASS reason=family_halted` (§3.13) of ALERT-class detectors (#6, #7, #14, #18, #20). The engine cites those only for ATTEST, which ARCH's CHAMPION → CHAMPION row calls non-widening. A RESUME's "no exec-store halt stands" is decided by the engine's own `take_flock=True` read, never by an AUT-6 verdict;
    - the ALERT findings #19 and #30.
    Each verdict that uses a snapshot carries `metrics.exec_snapshot_advisory=true`.
    - **Bounded to one pass (r13, LOW-r13-1).** A `family_halted` PASS that rests on the advisory snapshot suppresses that detector's finding only for the pass that wrote it. The next pass re-reads the halt from a fresh snapshot and evaluates again, so no later pass inherits the suppression. The verdict's 8 h validity matters only to ATTEST, which is non-widening. `test_snapshot_fed_verdicts_carry_exec_snapshot_advisory` gains a case for this: a snapshot-only `family_halted` PASS followed by a pass whose snapshot shows no halt. The second pass evaluates the detector normally.
  - **Fail closed.** A fingerprint that does not settle within the helper's retries, a missing file or a failed `quick_check` makes the reading detector `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.unknown_reason=input_unreadable` (§3.3.2), never PASS. **(r12, AF3)** An unsettled fingerprint is recorded as `metrics.unknown_detail=exec_snapshot_unsettled`. The escalation, `EXEC_SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN_STREAK_MAX`, and the measured settle-rate floor are in §3.3.2.
  - **Tests.** `test_aut6_wrapped_units_open_no_live_wal_db_in_place` is a lint. It fails on any `sqlite3.connect` in an AUT-6 closure whose path is neither a snapshot copy under the row's cache bind nor a store named in `AUT6_ROLLBACK_JOURNAL_STORES`. `test_aut6_exec_store_snapshot_read_sidecars_present_and_absent_under_bwrap` covers E-7a rule 3's two cases, and `test_exec_snapshot_unstable_fingerprint_is_input_unreadable_unknown` covers the fail-closed path.
- **Import closure (E-7, E-7a).** Network stays on in every row, for delivery only, and no row's import closure reaches a `breezy.adapters` module. `test_demand_stage_has_no_import_path_to_venue_adapters` is kept. `test_evaluate_stage_has_no_import_path_to_venue_adapters` makes the same assertion for the evaluate stage and every other AUT-6 entry point. **(r12, AF1)** The moves of §3.1.2 make it true, and the plan-time measurement shows it (§0 (21)). NO-SEND is unchanged.
- **Notifier and fallback (E-7a rule 1).** `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily` is the only AUT-6 unit that names an `OnFailure=` target. **(r14)** The watchdog daemons (§3.5), which AUT-1 owns, name the same notifier. r10 pointed it at the shared studies notifier `breezy-study-failed@`, which also serves non-autonomy studies. r11 points it at `breezy-autonomy-failed@.service`, which runs the same `breezy-study-failed --unit %i` code under the wrapper, and leaves the studies' notifier unchanged.
  - **Two lines.** First `ExecStart=-/usr/bin/timeout -k 2 25 <wrapper> breezy-autonomy-failed@ breezy-study-failed --unit %i`, then the unwrapped fallback `ExecStart=/usr/bin/timeout -k 2 25 breezy-study-failed --unit %i --fallback`.
  - **Markers (r12, AF4; replaces r11's single marker).** The wrapped line writes two write-once markers (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL`, 0444) in `evidence/alerts/notify/<YYYY-MM-DD>/`, named `<unit>__<InvocationID>.attempted.json` and `….delivered.json`. The order is:
    1. write the failure page's outbox entry (§3.6.3);
    2. write the `attempted` marker;
    3. run `deliver_with_proof`;
    4. after a `delivered=true` record, write the `delivered` marker.

    So `attempted` implies that a durable outbox entry exists. The date directory is the UTC date of the failed invocation's unit-failed entry, and the fallback reads that date and the day before.
  - **Fallback (r12, AF4).** The fallback line reads both markers and never re-delivers when `delivered` is present.
    - **`delivered` present.** It exits 0 and sends nothing.
    - **`attempted` present, `delivered` absent.** The wrapped attempt reached the alert path, and its outbox entry is the retry vehicle, drained by the redeliver unit and the dead-man. The fallback sends nothing, raises no `autonomy_notifier_wrapper_failed`, journals `AUTONOMY_FAILED fallback=outbox_pending` and exits 0. An undelivered entry still reaches #23 (`abandoned` after 24 h) and the canary and #5 paths. **(r13, LOW-r13-2)** The cover for this branch is AUT-5's dead-man, `breezy-autonomy-deadman`, which runs every 30 min. It drains the outbox with `drain_outbox(drainer="deadman", min_age_s=ALERT_OUTBOX_STALE_S)` (§3.11 item 6), independently of the redeliver unit, so a broken redeliver unit cannot strand the failure page.
    - **`attempted` absent.** The wrapped attempt never reached the alert path: a wrapper exit of 126 or 127, a bwrap setup failure, an import crash, or an outbox write failure. The fallback delivers the failure page itself, adding a CRITICAL `autonomy_notifier_wrapper_failed`, which is raised only in this case.
    - **Unreadable.** An unreadable `notify/` directory or marker counts as `attempted` absent: the fallback delivers, which fails toward the page.
    - **Unreadable date or `InvocationID` (r13, LOW-r13-3).** The fallback may be unable to read the failed invocation's unit-failed entry, its UTC date or its `InvocationID`. It then cannot name the markers, so it counts as `attempted` absent: it pages again, adding `autonomy_notifier_wrapper_failed`. A possible duplicate page is the accepted cost, and a silent failure is not.
  - **Retention (r12, LOW-r12-3).** After its own delivery attempt, the wrapped line removes at most one `notify/<YYYY-MM-DD>/` directory older than `STAGE_STATUS_RETENTION_DAYS = 8`, under the same rule as the demand stage's `stage_status/` prune (§3.4.2): a removal error prints `notify_prune_failed errno=<name>`, counts in `journal_write_failures` and never blocks a page. The notifier runs only after a daily failure, so `notify/` holds at most two directories per failure day. The unlinks are a row of the notifier's `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY` entry, limited to that path constant.
  - **Why a marker.** An ignored `ExecStart=-` exit is not journaled (§0 (4)), so the marker, not the exit code, is the signal. Any failure of the wrapped attempt reaches the alert path this way: a wrapper exit of 126 or 127, a bwrap setup failure, or a crash.
  - **Residual and bound.** The fallback line is unwrapped by design and is a stated residual (§8 R-36). The worst case is 1 + 27 + 1 + 27 + 5 = 61 s, and it can run only after the 05:30Z or 13:00Z daily. Test: `test_autonomy_failed_notifier_fallback_delivers_when_wrapper_fails`.
- **Residual (E-7a rule 5).** These stay outside every row: the node, including its outbox writer `OffLoopAlertSink`; the operator CLIs `breezy-check-alerts` and `breezy-autonomy-health --mark-buildside-restart`; the notifier's fallback line; and `breezy-discovery-pull`, which is not autonomy-owned. The evaluate stage and the health unit lack `--unshare-pid`, so they see other same-uid processes in `/proc`. **(r14)** The health unit makes no state-changing call; a watchdog restart is the user manager's own action (§3.5). The recorder is a watchdog daemon but not an autonomy-owned unit, so it is not wrapped. It still declares `NotifyAccess=all`, so that a later prefix cannot silently disarm its watchdog (§0.1 (27)).

#### 3.1.2 Import closure: the halt-decode move and its companions (r12, AF1, coordinator ruling option (a))

**The ruling.** Move the pure halt-mirror decode out of `src/breezy/strategy/current_rung_hold/trial_day_latch.py` into an adapter-free domain module, with delegating shims, following the `instrument_leg.py` and `leg_prices` precedent. Measure the import closure of the evaluate and daily entry points at plan time, and show that no `breezy.adapters.*` module and no `breezy.strategy.current_rung_hold` package `__init__` remains in it. Option (b), narrowing the E-7 closure rule, is rejected, and r12 narrows nothing: E-7a rule 2 is met as written.

**M1. The move (WP6).** The new module is `src/breezy/domain/family_halt.py`, and every symbol moves byte-identically:

| Symbol | Kind | Source today |
|---|---|---|
| `read_family_halt_rows_readonly` | function | `trial_day_latch.py:354` |
| `decode_family_halt_state` | function | `:333` |
| `family_halt_key` | function (used by both) | `:315` |
| `classify_legacy` | function (used by the decode) | `:322` |
| `FamilyHaltReading` | frozen dataclass (the decode's return type) | `:304-308` |
| `MalformedHaltValue` | exception (raised by the reader) | `:311` |
| `LEGACY_FAMILY_HALT_KEY` | `Final[str]` | `:292` |
| `FAMILY_HALT_KEY_PREFIX` | `Final[str]` | `:293` |
| `FAMILY_ID_PATTERN` | `Final[re.Pattern[str]]` | `:301` |
| `LEGACY_HALT_ATTRIBUTED_FAMILY_ID` | `Final[str]` | `:297` |
| `LEGACY_HALT_PINNED_SHA256` | `Final[str]` | `:298-300` |
| `HALT_CLEARED_MARKER` | `Final[bytes]` (today the private `_HALT_CLEARED_MARKER`) | `:296` |

- **Imports.** The module imports only stdlib: `hashlib`, `re`, `sqlite3`, `collections.abc.Callable`, `dataclasses.dataclass`, `pathlib.Path` and `typing.Final` (§0 (20)).
- **One stated reading.** It is the first `breezy.domain` module to perform I/O, through `read_family_halt_rows_readonly`'s read-only SQLite URI. The function keeps its injectable `connect` seam, the ruling names the domain layer, and import-linter's layers contract constrains only `breezy.*` imports. Recorded as observation O-7 (§11).
- **Delegating shims.** `trial_day_latch.py` replaces each definition with `from breezy.domain.family_halt import …` of the same name, so `trial_day_latch.decode_family_halt_state is family_halt.decode_family_halt_state`, and likewise for every symbol. The private name stays bound as `_HALT_CLEARED_MARKER = HALT_CLEARED_MARKER`, the same object.
  - `__all__` is unchanged.
  - Every other user keeps its import, untouched: the clear and set CLIs, `scripts/analysis/decision_funnel_daily_digest.py`, `TrialDayLatch.family_halt_state` / `clear_family_halt`, and the tests.
  - `trade_supervisor_core`'s parallel `continuous_*` decoder is not touched, and `test_runtime_and_strategy_halt_constants_and_decoders_agree_on_the_full_cross_product` stays green unedited.
- **The two named constants, `FILL_KEY_PREFIX` and `REFUSAL_REASONS` (measured, §0 (20)).** Neither is used by the two functions or by anything they use. They are the import edges that put the adapters and the crh package into `trial_day_latch`'s closure:
  - `FILL_KEY_PREFIX` arrives from `breezy.adapters.polymarket_us.exec.client` (`:68-74`);
  - `REFUSAL_REASONS` arrives from `breezy.strategy.current_rung_hold.decision` (`:87`), which runs the crh package `__init__`.

  The domain module therefore neither defines nor imports either one, which is what removes both edges from the AUT-6 closures. Neither constant moves: `REFUSAL_REASONS` lives in `decision.py`, which the code records as untouchable (`trial_day_latch.py:184-185,208-209`), and `FILL_KEY_PREFIX` is the exec client's own key namespace. `test_family_halt_domain_module_imports_stdlib_only` asserts that the module's imports are stdlib only and that neither name is bound in it. "Any others they need" is the table above, derived by a function-level walk (§0 (20)).

**Companion moves the measurement requires (§0 (19)).** The closure claim must hold for the whole entry point, not for the halt decode alone. The measurement found four more dependencies that reach the adapters or the crh package. Each is handled the same way: a move-only, the old module aliasing the moved object, one implementation.
- **M2 (WP6).** `src/breezy/runtime/flock_holders.py` takes these from `trade_supervisor.py`:

  | New name | Old name in `trade_supervisor.py` | Lines |

It is a supervisor code change, so activation needs a supervisor restart (§4 WP6).
- **M3 (WP5).** `src/breezy/domain/quantile_density.py` and `src/breezy/domain/location_correction.py` are moved unchanged from `breezy.strategy.ladder_ev`; their imports are stdlib, scipy and each other. Both strategy modules re-export every public and private module-level name of the moved module by identity. `breezy.analysis.nbp_calibration` repoints its two imports (`:83-92`) to the domain modules. Fourteen `src/` and `scripts/` modules keep importing the strategy path unchanged.
  - **Logger name (r13, LOW-r13-4).** `quantile_density.py:74` binds `_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)`. A pure move would rename the logger from `breezy.strategy.ladder_ev.quantile_density` to `breezy.domain.quantile_density`, and any log filter or grep keyed on the old name would stop matching without an error. The moved module therefore keeps the old name as a literal, `logging.getLogger("breezy.strategy.ladder_ev.quantile_density")`. This is the one line of M3 that is not byte-identical. `location_correction.py` has no logger. Test: `tests/unit/test_ladder_ev_domain_move.py::test_moved_quantile_density_keeps_strategy_logger_name`.

The module identity changes from the bare `forecast_conditional_scoring` to `breezy.analysis.forecast_calibration_leg`, so mypy now checks the code as package code under strict. Before each move, the implementer greps `tests/` for `monkeypatch.setattr` or `patch(` on a moved name. A hit stops that move for review, because patching the old module's alias would no longer reach the moved code. Measured at plan time: none (§R12).

### 3.2 Detector catalogue: kind `forecast_quantile_ladder` (the only `LIVE_GATE_ROUTED_KINDS` member)

Every other kind carries `RefusingPlugin` (ARCH C6). **Proposed action** is AUT-6's proposal for the ruling's `detector_action_map`; the runtime class always comes from the ruling, or from `DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS` when no ruling verifies (§3.3.1). **Sev** is the delivery severity; every alert raised for a detector action goes through `deliver_with_proof` at any severity (ARCH §4.6, V3). **HX** = halted-by-design exemption (§3.13). **LP** = live-proof class: `N` natural, `D` drill (AUT-7b), `G` gate-proven only (ARCH C6: exec-store and INTEGRITY halts would freeze the lineage or the venue).

| # | Detector id | Required class | Kind | Source and rule | Horizon / period | Proposed action | Floor | Sev | HX | Cause | Escalation | LP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `feed_stale` | freshness (NBP, obs) | NODE_LOCAL | AUT-1 | AUT-1 | ENTRY_VETO (code-fixed) | — | WARN | n/a | INFRA | → #7 | N |

**Live-proof class:** HALT is proven live by #8h only (drill); #15 and #17 are gate-proven (RED→GREEN), as ARCH C6 declares for exec-store and INTEGRITY halts. If none happens, it is mechanism-proven by the WP4 verify-first scratch unit, which runs the production gate under the deployed directives (§6, O-9). The drill probe is deleted. ENTRY_VETO and ALERT natural.

### 3.3 Detector → action map, UNKNOWN handling, INTEGRITY floor

#### 3.3.1 Consumption contract and executors (ARCH §4.6, P6-7; C4 Engine acceptance)

- `CATALOG` is the code proposal. The ruling's `detector_action_map` lists **exactly** the VERDICT ids of `CATALOG`, including `DRILL_INJECT`, `DRILL_INJECT_HALT` and `fee_schedule` (`test_policy_detector_map_covers_catalog_exactly`, deploy copy). The H16 skip rule is in WP5.
- **With a verified ruling** the producer stamps `declared_action_class` from the ruling's map via AUT-5's single-read loader and `policy_ruling_sha256` from the ruling.
- **Without one (r4, C4 P4-10):** the ruling is absent, unreadable or sha-mismatched. The producer writes `policy_ruling_sha256=null`, `assumptions:["no_policy_ruling"]` (the closed C4 enum; r3's `policy_unavailable` is withdrawn), and `declared_action_class = pins.DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS[detector]` if the detector has a row there, else `ALERT`. It sends the alert through §3.6 either way. The engine acts on such a FAIL only for DEMOTE or HALT (restrictive fallback); an ALERT-class FAIL is acted on by the producer's own delivery.
- **`DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS` rows AUT-6 proposes** (AUT-5 owns `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/pins.py` and the literal): `aut6.fill_better_than_ask → HALT`; `aut6.forecast_drift_persistent`, `aut6.feature_drift_persistent` (#11p), `aut6.calibration_drift_offline_persistent` (#12p), `aut6.calibration_drift_live → DEMOTE`. The drill ids never appear there: they act only under the drill clause.
- NODE_LOCAL ids are code-fixed `ENTRY_VETO` and never appear in the map. `IN_NODE_EXEC_HALT` (#17) is mirrored by the engine's fixed table.
- **Executors (ARCH §4.6):**
  - ENTRY_VETO: `RegistryWatchActor` (AUT-5), polling #1–#5, and honouring `integrity_floor` demand files as `registry_restrictive_pending`.
  - DEMOTE, HALT: the restrictive-only intraday engine pass (AUT-5).
  - **SELF_HEAL (r14): systemd.**
    - **Executor.** The user manager, acting on each member's own directives: `Type=notify`, `NotifyAccess=all`, `WatchdogSec=`, `Restart=` on watchdog, `StartLimit*` and `OnFailure=` (§3.5).
    - **No autonomy restart.** No autonomy module restarts anything. `test_no_autonomy_module_calls_systemctl_restart` (AST) fails on any `systemctl` argv in an AUT-6 closure that contains `restart`, `try-restart`, `start` or `stop`.
    - **AUT-1's part.** AUT-1's recorder only decides whether to ping, through its own gate (AUT-1 r9's `recorder_watchdog`), under AUT-6's SELF_HEAL contract (§3.5).
    - **Health unit.** It records and pages each watchdog restart (#21).
  - **ALERT: the producing unit, through AUT-6's executor library** (`deliver_with_proof`, durable outbox, drainers). AUT-6 owns the library; AUT-2, AUT-4, AUT-5 and AUT-7 producers raising detector-action or failure-mode alerts call it too.
  - **(r14) No SELF_HEAL mode.** Ruling item 3 deletes, with the executor, the parts r7–r13 built: `self_heal_mode()`, the `ALERT_ONLY`/`SELF_HEAL` switch, its persisted file, and its degraded, lost and corrupt findings.
    - **Arming.** The watchdog is armed from WP4's activation, like the recorder's existing `Restart=always`. It needs no ruling.
    - **Ruling check.** The ruling's map row for #21 is SELF_HEAL. `test_policy_detector_map_covers_catalog_exactly` checks the class as well as the id.
- Every verdict carries `metrics.cause_class`; INFRA never charges `MAX_RECOVERABLE_RESUMES_PER_LINEAGE_14D`; DRILL (#8, #8h) charges only the drill budget and carries `assumptions:["drill"]`.

#### 3.3.2 UNKNOWN fails closed

- **NODE_LOCAL (#4, #5).** Any observation error ⇒ `UNKNOWN`. For at most `WATCH_TICK_STALE_S` (180 s) it vetoes nothing; past that it returns its own veto reason, AUT-1's writer records `DetectorEvent(state=UNKNOWN, detail=persistent)`, and the transition sends a CRITICAL `aut6_detector_unknown_persistent`. It clears on the next good observation.
- **VERDICT detectors (r4).** An unreadable input gives `outcome=INCONCLUSIVE` with `metrics.unknown_reason="input_unreadable"` and `metrics.unknown_streak=k` (a metric, not an `assumptions` tag: that enum is closed). INCONCLUSIVE never counts as PASS. At `k ≥ unknown_streak_max` the producer writes #25 FAIL naming the detector and sends a CRITICAL; the liveness detectors #6, #7, #18, #29 and #30 themselves report FAIL `reason=blind`. A detector with nothing to evaluate writes `INCONCLUSIVE` with `metrics.day_status=NO_INPUT`, never a missing verdict (C4 Invariants, U10).
- **Exec-store snapshot (r12, AF3).** The snapshot-fed reads are the intraday #19 and #30, and the halted-by-design read for #6, #7 and #18 (§3.13). An intraday pass in which the snapshot did not settle within the helper's retries writes those detectors `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.unknown_reason=input_unreadable, metrics.unknown_detail=exec_snapshot_unsettled`.
  - **N = 3.** Such a pass counts toward `EXEC_SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN_STREAK_MAX = 3` consecutive passes (a `Final` in `detector_catalog.py`), in place of the detector's catalogue value of 2. At the third consecutive pass, about 15 min, the producer writes #25 FAIL `detector=exec_snapshot` and sends a CRITICAL `exec_snapshot_unsettled` through §3.6. Any other unreadable cause keeps the catalogue value. The daily producer keeps N = 1: one unsettled daily read is a #25 FAIL, because the next daily slot is 7.5 h or more away.
  - **Floor.** `EXEC_SNAPSHOT_SETTLE_FLOOR = 0.99` is the fraction of snapshot attempts that must settle, measured with the node up (WP6 verify-first (11)). At that floor, a false #25 page needs three consecutive unsettled passes: at most 288 × 0.01³ ≈ 3 × 10⁻⁴ a day, about one a decade. A floor of 0.99 with N = 2 would be about 0.03 a day, roughly one false page a month, which is why N is 3 and not 2. A measured rate below the floor stops WP6, and the design returns to review; neither N nor the floor is loosened to fit. **(r13, AG4)** That arithmetic assumes attempts fail independently. WP6 verify-first (11) therefore also gates on the measured maximum run of consecutive unsettled attempts, which must be below N, so clustered failures cannot hide behind a passing rate.
  - **Reset.** The streak resets on the first settled snapshot. `test_exec_snapshot_unsettled_streak_escalates_to_25_critical_at_3_passes` covers two unsettled passes then a settled one (no page) and three unsettled passes (one #25 CRITICAL). Its positive control is a non-snapshot unreadable input, which still escalates at 2.
- **Unit-health reads.** A non-zero exit, timeout or D-Bus error from `systemctl`/`journalctl` makes the pass `UNKNOWN`, never "zero failures". It increments `passes_unknown_streak`, leaves the heartbeat's `ts_ns` unchanged (§3.11) and feeds #25. A persistently UNKNOWN health unit therefore fails #29 (streak ≥ 3 or age) and the AUT-5 dead-man's ARCH age rule (> 1800 s) within about 30 min; it can never read as healthy.
- **Fold.** An unreadable fold (resolver raises, chain verify fails, export prefix mismatch) or an **empty fold** (the venue chain holds zero rows, impossible after AUT-5a's bootstrap) is never "no subjects". The producer writes a CRITICAL `fold_unreadable` through §3.6, writes `fold_ok=false, fold_reason=<unreadable|empty>` into its heartbeat, and writes no subject verdicts; #26 FAILs on `fold_ok=false` at the next health pass. A non-empty fold with no CHAMPION, HALTED or CHALLENGER (all RETIRED, for example after the KILL) is the legitimate `_host/` case, with `fold_ok=true`.
- **Fold unreadable in the health pass (r5, F4).** The health unit also needs the fold to pick subjects. When its own read fails (same causes), it writes #26 FAIL under `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/_host/` with `metrics.fold_reason`, sends a CRITICAL `fold_unreadable` directly through `deliver_with_proof` (writer `health`; it does not wait for the intraday heartbeat), and records the pass as `pass_result=UNKNOWN`: `passes_unknown_streak` increments and the heartbeat's `ts_ns` stays unchanged (§3.11), so #29 and AUT-5's dead-man also fire if the fold stays unreadable.

#### 3.3.3 INTEGRITY floor (r4: ARCH P6-12, V9, U13; no longer held)

- `floor_action_class=HALT` for every `cause_class=INTEGRITY` row (today #15). It is a code literal and does not depend on the policy file being readable.
- **Two paths, both under ARCH:**
  1. **Verdict path.** An INTEGRITY FAIL is a C4 verdict declaring HALT (from the ruling, or `DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS` with `no_policy_ruling`). The intraday engine writes the registry HALT (INTEGRITY cause, venue frozen per C5) within one engine pass (≤ 150 s after the producer); the watch actor vetoes on its next tick.
  2. **Engine-independent path.** In the same producer invocation, in its second, bwrap'd stage (§3.4.2; r8, E-7), `integrity_floor.write_demand` writes one restrictive-demand file through AUT-5's `demand/v1` writer. The watch actor honours it as `registry_restrictive_pending` on its next tick (≤ 60 s), whether or not the engine runs.
- **The write, exactly as ARCH C5 specifies:**
  - Writer identity: `aut6.intraday`, which must be in `pins.DEMAND_WRITER_PRODUCER_IDS`; the writer refuses otherwise (`test_demand_writer_refuses_unless_producer_in_demand_writer_ids`).
  - Path `~/.local/share/breezy/registry/demand/<venue>/<family_id>_integrity_floor_<verdict_id>_aut6.intraday.json`, atomic (r10, AC1: an unnamed `O_TMPFILE` inode linked into place, §3.4.2; r11, AE2: with no fallback), 0444, schema `demand/v1`, exact-set, ≤ `DEMAND_FILE_MAX_BYTES`, `reason=integrity_floor`, `verdict_id` of the FAIL verdict written beside it.
  - **Validation before the write.** `family_id` must be in the venue fold at `now`, and `reason` must be in `pins.DEMAND_REASONS`. A file failing either check would veto **every** family on the venue (C5), so the producer writes no such file; it sends a CRITICAL `integrity_demand_invalid` and still writes its verdict (the verdict path stands). **(r9, LOW-r9-5) That page is deduplicated on `verdict_id`.** After a `delivered=true` record, the demand stage creates the write-once marker `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/dedupe/integrity_demand_invalid/<verdict_id>.json` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL`, 0444, inside its alerts bind). It sends no second page for that id while the verdict stays valid. An undelivered page writes no marker and is retried on the next pass. An unreadable dedupe directory fails toward paging again.
  - **Write errors (r5, F3).** Any failure of the demand write itself (`ENOSPC`, `EACCES`, `EROFS` or another `OSError`, or the writer's refusal because the reserved INTEGRITY slots are full) sends a CRITICAL `integrity_demand_write_failed` through `deliver_with_proof` (event in `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS`; payload carries the errno name or `slot_full`, no path), increments `integrity_demand_write_failures=<n>` in the demand stage's `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND` summary line (r9, AA3), and is a #23 FAIL. The verdict is still written, so the engine path stands; the failed file is retried on the next pass (idempotent on `verdict_id`).
  - **Slots.** At most one unarchived file per `(family, reason)`; the write is idempotent on `verdict_id`, so a re-run never adds a second file. `integrity_floor` files are not subject to the producer cap `DEMAND_FILES_MAX − DEMAND_INTEGRITY_RESERVED`: they and engine files alone may use the reserved slots (`DEMAND_INTEGRITY_RESERVED` ≥ 8). AUT-6 writes no other reason. r3's `DEMAND_FILES_RESERVED_FOR_INTEGRITY = 1` is withdrawn in favour of ARCH's constant.
  - **Restrictive only.** No AUT-6 code renames, edits or deletes a demand file (`test_producer_demand_write_is_restrictive_only`). Only the engine archives it, by rename into `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/demand/<venue>/`: after the chain shows the family HALTED, RETIRED or not CHAMPION at a later row, or with its `reject_reason` if the engine rejects the verdict (U13). **(r8, E-7)** Only the bwrap'd demand stage holds write authority on `~/.local/share/breezy/registry/demand/`, and its binds are `registry/demand/` and `evidence/alerts` alone (E-7 rule 2). The unit's `ReadWritePaths=` line is configuration, not a control (E-7 rule 1). The evaluate stage has no write path to `registry/`: from r11 its bwrap row binds no registry path, and its self-probe shows `registry/` read-only at every start (E-7a, §3.1.1). The lint (§3.1) is defence in depth. A failed demand-stage self-probe writes no demand file, but the verdict path stands (§3.4.2).
- **Clearing the INTEGRITY freeze** that follows is build-side incident handling (ARCH W15): root cause evidenced under `docs/incident-reports/`, peer-reviewed, then cleared through the halt CLI and the registry CLI on the C5 API. Never an operator decision, never automatic.
- A defect-signature fill is exactly the case that warrants stopping and investigating (L-25).

### 3.4 Producers (C4 writers)

#### 3.4.1 Common rules (r4 conforms the identity and validity rules to C4)

- Output `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/<family_id>/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<verdict_id>.json` (0600 file, 0700 dir), `verdict/v1`, written atomically and never rewritten.
- **Identity (C4, P4-11).** `verdict_id` = sha256 of the canonical body **minus `verdict_id` and `produced_at_ns`**. `valid_until_ns` is **slot-anchored**: the producer's timer slot start (the 5-min or 10-min firing instant, or the daily 05:30Z/13:00Z slot) plus the validity, so a recompute in the same slot dedupes and the store refuses a different body under an existing id (`test_differing_body_same_id_refused`).
- `producer_code_sha` ∈ `pins.PRODUCER_SOURCE_SHA256[producer_id]` for `aut6.intraday`, `aut6.daily` and `aut6.health` (P6-13); each producer computes its closure hash at start and refuses to run if unpinned. **(r9, LOW-r9-4) Per-stage pins.** `aut6.intraday` runs as two stages with two import closures, and each stage hashes its own closure at start. The hashes are pinned in the literal `AUT6_STAGE_SOURCE_SHA256 = {"evaluate": …, "demand": …}` (`detector_catalog.py`), and both values are members of `pins.PRODUCER_SOURCE_SHA256["aut6.intraday"]`. Each stage refuses unless its own hash is pinned under its own stage key, and verdicts carry the evaluate hash as `producer_code_sha`. An unpinned demand stage prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND INTEGRITY demand_stage_unpinned`, sends a CRITICAL through §3.6 and exits 3.
- `assumptions` only from the closed C4 enum: AUT-6 uses `no_policy_ruling` and `drill`, nothing else.
- Subjects: every family the fold names CHAMPION, HALTED or CHALLENGER per venue. Host-wide detectors (#6, #18, #19, #21–#31) take the venue champion as subject; with none (non-empty fold), they write `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/_host/`, which the engine ignores.
- **Validity (W1, Z4):** intraday and health verdicts `valid_until_ns = slot_start_ns + 8 h` (`INTRADAY_VALIDITY_S = 28800` = `ATTEST_VERDICT_VALIDITY_H`); daily `+ 26 h`; the producer refuses above `MAX_VERDICT_VALIDITY_H`.
- **Refresh:** write on outcome change, or when the newest verdict for `(subject, detector)` is older than `INTRADAY_REFRESH_S = 3600` (= `INTRADAY_ATTEST_VERDICT_PERIOD_MIN` 60).
- **ATTEST invariant (ARCH §4.5 W1, in ARCH's names):** `ATTEST_PERIOD_H + L_max + ATTEST_MARGIN_H ≤ ATTEST_VERDICT_VALIDITY_H` with `L_max` = 60 min + 150 s ⇒ 6 + 1.04 + 0.5 ≤ 8. AUT-6's side: at every instant the newest PASS per (subject, intraday HEALTH detector) has ≥ 28800 − 3600 − 300 − 130 = 24770 s of validity (the 130 s is the most a slot start can precede `produced_at_ns`), which is ≥ 21600 + 300 s. Pinned by `test_intraday_refresh_keeps_attest_gap_free`; the schedule row goes to AUT-5's `test_attest_cadence_has_no_expiry_gap`.
- #6 reports `PASS window=closed` outside its window and `PASS reason=family_halted` while halted (§3.13).
- No paths, env values or ids in verdicts (payload hygiene scan).
- AUT-6 verdicts are `DRIFT` and `HEALTH` only: `k_life`, `alpha_k`, `n_min_eff` and `n_cap` are null and no α is charged (ALPHA decision).

#### 3.4.2 `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday` (`aut6.intraday`)

- Inputs (read-only): C1 files for today and yesterday (`DecisionRecord`, `OrderLink`, `LifecycleEvent`, `DetectorEvent`); the C1 payload store `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/capture_payloads/depth10/`; the ingested catalog through `order_book_depth10` (#15 only); the newest `breezy-trade-*.log` (tail ≤ 4 MiB); the exec store through the E-8 snapshot helper into the row's cache bind, with the copy read `mode=ro` and `PRAGMA query_only=ON` (r11, AE5, E-7a rule 3; r8's in-place G6 read is withdrawn), called with `take_flock=False`, and advisory (r12, E-8a; §3.1.1); the recorder live root (stat only); `/proc/locks`; the drill marker (§3.4.8); the C5 fold; the health heartbeat.
- Detectors: #6, #7, #8, #8h, #15, #18, #19, #29, #30, #32, and #25 for its own detectors. Writes: the evaluate stage writes verdicts, its heartbeat, delivery records and outbox entries; the bwrap'd demand stage writes `integrity_floor` demand files and their delivery records (r8, E-7).
- **Lock and bound (r4, ARCH §5.2 table):** `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/.aut6-intraday.lock`, `flock -n` (0 s, inside the table's ≤ 10 s; r11, E-7a: taken in-process with `LOCK_NB` on the pre-created lock file opened `O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC`, §3.1.1); **`TimeoutStartSec=120`** (**r9, AA1:** `TimeoutStartSec` re-arms for each `ExecStart=` line (measured, §0), so with two stages it bounds each command, never the unit. The unit's bound is the two `timeout -k 2` wrappers below, and the 120 s is a per-command backstop; r3 had 240 s, above the table's ≤ 120 s). On contention it prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY SKIPPED lock_held` and exits 0; 3 consecutive skips ⇒ WARN. Never the studies flock. WP6 verify-first measures the pass wall time (p99 ≤ 90 s required).
- **Heartbeat:** after every run, including a failed fold read, it rewrites `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/.aut6-intraday.heartbeat` (single writer, under its lock, `os.replace`) with `{schema, ts_ns, invocation_id, wrote, skipped, fold_ok, fold_reason}`.
- **Reciprocal watch:** #29 reads `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/heartbeat.json`. It is a verdict (and a CRITICAL with proof), not only a log line.
- **Two stages, one invocation (r8, E-7; replaces r7's "Unit sandbox" bullet).** User-unit sandbox directives are not enforced on this host (E-7 evidence: `touch` succeeds under `ReadOnlyPaths=`). r7's `ReadOnlyPaths=` on the exec store, the registry and the catalog, and its `ReadWritePaths=` list, were therefore never controls, and r8 no longer claims them as such. They stay in the unit file as configuration (E-7 rule 1). **From r11 both stages run under the shared wrapper, each with its own row (E-7a rule 1, §3.1.1).** The unit runs two `ExecStart=` lines in order; they always both run (r9, AA2). r8 let a failed first line skip the second. A late evaluate failure, such as an exception after the #15 FAIL verdict was written or the hard kill, would then also have dropped the engine-independent demand write. The evaluate line is therefore an `ExecStart=-` line:
  1. **Evaluate stage, under the wrapper without `--unshare-pid` (r11, E-7a rules 1 and 2; r8–r10 ran it unwrapped).** `ExecStart=-/usr/bin/timeout -k 2 100 <wrapper> breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday#evaluate breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday --stage evaluate` (r9, AA1, AA2). `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday --stage evaluate` runs its row's self-probe (§3.1.1), takes the producer lock, evaluates every detector, and writes verdicts, the heartbeat, delivery records and outbox entries. Its row binds only `derived/verdicts`, `evidence/alerts` and its exec-snapshot cache, so it has no write path to `registry/` or `state/` (OS-enforced; the lint, §3.1, is defence in depth). It reads the exec store only through the E-8 snapshot helper (r11, AE5) and every other file `O_RDONLY`. Its row keeps the host pid namespace, so it reads `/proc/locks` and `/proc/<pid>/stat` for #6's process conjunct; an unreadable `/proc/locks` makes that conjunct UNKNOWN, never "0 holders".
     - **Bounds (r9, AA1).** The in-process deadline is `EVALUATE_STAGE_DEADLINE_S = 95`. The wrapper sends SIGTERM at `EVALUATE_STAGE_BUDGET_S = 100` and SIGKILL 2 s later.
     - **Stage status (r9, AA2).** systemd records nothing for an ignored exit (measured, §0 (4)). So on every exit path the stage controls, it writes `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/.aut6-intraday.stage_status` last, in a `finally` (0600, `os.replace`, `{schema: "intraday_stage_status/v1", invocation_id, evaluate_exit, reason, ts_ns}`). Those paths include `SKIPPED lock_held` and a failed fold read. A killed stage writes no status.
     - **Own journal lines (r10, AC2).** The stage prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY START ts_ns=<n>` as its first statement (r11: from a stdlib-only entry module, as the demand stage's AB8 line, so a missing `START` separates a bwrap launch failure from an import crash). It adds `exit=<code>` to its `PRODUCER_INTRADAY` summary line, which is printed in the same `finally`; the `SKIPPED lock_held` line counts as a summary with `exit=0`. The stage keeps the host pid namespace (no `--unshare-pid`), so both lines carry `_SYSTEMD_INVOCATION_ID` as measured for an unwrapped stage (§0 (5)); from r11 the AB10 test asserts this under the wrapper too. The health pass reads the evaluate outcome from these lines, independently of the demand stage (see the classification below).
  2. **Demand stage, under the shared wrapper's `#demand` row** (E-7 rule 2; r11, E-7a rule 1; AUT-5 owns the wrapper and the self-probe code). The unit line is `ExecStart=/usr/bin/timeout -k 2 15 <wrapper> breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday#demand breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday --stage demand`; the `timeout` prefix is AA1's external hard kill (r9). The row (§3.1.1) is r8–r10's invocation, unchanged. Mounts: `--ro-bind / /`, `--bind` on `~/.local/share/breezy/registry/demand/` and `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts` only, `--dev /dev`, `--proc /proc`. Isolation: `--unshare-pid`, `--new-session`, `--die-with-parent`. Credentials: `--tmpfs ~/.config` with no credential re-bind; the webhook URL arrives in the environment from systemd's `EnvironmentFile=`, which is loaded before the wrapper starts. `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday --stage demand` lists the `aut6.intraday` #15 FAIL verdicts with `valid_until_ns > now` from the C4 store (read through the read-only root). It skips any verdict that already has a demand file, unarchived under `registry/demand/<venue>/` or archived under `evidence/demand/<venue>/`, so an engine-archived demand is never re-raised. It re-validates each remaining verdict (§3.3.3) and writes the missing `integrity_floor` files, idempotent on `verdict_id`. It never reads `/proc/locks`: inside `--unshare-pid` the kernel hides the node's lock, so a count there would read zero (AST-forbidden in this stage). Budget (r9, AA1): the wrapper enforces `DEMAND_STAGE_BUDGET_S = 15`, with SIGTERM at 15 s and SIGKILL at 17 s, and `--die-with-parent` takes the sandboxed process down with bwrap. An in-process deadline, `DEMAND_STAGE_DEADLINE_S = 12`, lets the stage print its summary line first. **Deadline outcome (r10, AC3).** When the deadline is hit, the stage takes no further verdicts and does four things. It prints its summary line with `deadline_hit=1` and `unprocessed=<n>`, the number of eligible verdicts it did not process. It writes a CRITICAL `demand_stage_deadline_hit` entry to the outbox: a local write inside the alerts bind, so no budget is spent on HTTP, and the redeliver unit delivers it. It exits `EXIT_DEMAND_DEADLINE = 5`, which the health pass also pages as a #22 finding. The unprocessed verdicts are retried on the next pass (idempotent on `verdict_id`). `test_stage_deadlines_below_budgets` asserts `EVALUATE_STAGE_DEADLINE_S < EVALUATE_STAGE_BUDGET_S` (95 < 100) and `DEMAND_STAGE_DEADLINE_S < DEMAND_STAGE_BUDGET_S` (12 < 15). WP6 verify-first requires p99(evaluate) ≤ 90 s and p99(demand) ≤ 10 s. The worst-case end is derived in §3.10.
