  - **Self-probe at demand-stage start (E-7, both directions; r11, AE3: these paths are exactly the `#demand` row's derivation, §3.1.1).** Negative: `open(~/.local/share/breezy/state/.aut6_bwrap_probe_<invocation_id>, O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL)` must fail with **exactly** `EROFS` (r9, AA5).
      - **Precondition.** It runs only after `os.stat(~/.local/share/breezy/state)` shows a directory owned by this uid, so the probe can never pass on a missing directory (failure: `direction=negative_precondition`).
      - **Other errnos.** Any other errno (`ENOENT`, `EACCES`, …) is a probe failure: `direction=negative`, with the errno name in the line and no path.
      - **Unexpected success.** If the open succeeds, the stage closes and unlinks the file at once and treats the result as INTEGRITY (`direction=negative_write_succeeded`). An unlink failure adds `probe_residue=1` to the CRITICAL.
      - **Second negative, inside `registry/` (r10, AB9).** The same open on `~/.local/share/breezy/registry/.aut6_bwrap_probe_<invocation_id>` must also fail with exactly `EROFS` (`direction=negative_registry`). It follows the same rules: the precondition (`os.stat(~/.local/share/breezy/registry)` shows a directory owned by this uid), the exact-errno rule and the unlink on unexpected success. The `state/` probe cannot see a bind widened from `registry/demand/` to `registry/`, and this probe can: under a `registry/`-wide bind, the same open succeeded (measured, §0 (13)). The probe file lies outside `demand/`, so it is never a demand file.
      Positive controls, both of which must SUCCEED: the same open under `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/.bwrap_probe/` (the file is then unlinked), and `open(~/.local/share/breezy/registry/demand/, O_WRONLY|O_TMPFILE)`. The demand bind's control uses `O_TMPFILE` deliberately: it creates no directory entry, so the watch actor can never see it (an unexpected named file under `registry/demand/` would veto the venue, C5; observation O-5, §11). **(r11, AE2; replaces r10's AC1 fallback)** If that `O_TMPFILE` open fails with `EOPNOTSUPP`, `EINVAL` or `EISDIR` (the filesystem or kernel lacks `O_TMPFILE`), the control fails like any other errno: `direction=positive_demand`, the errno name in the line, INTEGRITY and a delivered CRITICAL. The data root is ext4 (§0 (14)), so this path means the host changed, and it pages. On any other outcome the stage:
    - prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND INTEGRITY bwrap_probe_failed direction=<negative|negative_registry|positive_alerts|positive_demand>`;
    - sends a CRITICAL `bwrap_self_probe_failed` through `deliver_with_proof` (the event is in `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS`; if the alerts bind itself failed, §3.6.3's outbox-write-failure rule still attempts one delivery);
    - writes no demand file and exits `EXIT_INTEGRITY = 3`.
    Stage 1 has already written the verdicts, so the engine path of §3.3.3 still stands, and the failed invocation is a #22 finding.
  - **Same-bind writes (r9, CA-1).** Under bwrap, `os.rename` and `os.link` between two separate `--bind` mounts fail with `EXDEV` (coordinator-verified, §0 (7)). Every write by the demand stage therefore keeps its temporary file and its final rename or link inside one bind:
    - **Demand files (r10, AC1; replaces r9's `mkstemp` and rename).** AUT-5's `demand/v1` writer, as the demand stage calls it, writes each file with no visible temporary name. This is a requirement on that writer, recorded in §5:
      1. `fd = os.open(<venue dir>, O_TMPFILE|O_WRONLY|O_CLOEXEC, 0o444)`, never with `O_EXCL`, which makes the inode impossible to link (§0 (11));
      2. write the body and `fsync`;
      3. `os.link(f"/proc/self/fd/{fd}", <final path>)`;
      4. `fsync` the venue directory.

      Both ends of the link are inside `registry/demand/`.
      - **No orphan.** A SIGKILL at any point before the link leaves no directory entry (§0 (10)), so a killed stage cannot leave a file that vetoes the venue.
      - **Existing file.** `EEXIST` from the link means the file for that `verdict_id` already exists. It counts as `skipped_existing`, never as an error, and nothing is overwritten (§0 (9)).
      - **Not used.** `mkstemp`, and any named temporary file under `registry/demand/`, are never used (r11, AE2: there is no fallback path).
      - **`O_TMPFILE` unsupported (r11, AE2).** `EOPNOTSUPP`, `EINVAL` or `EISDIR` from the open prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND INTEGRITY o_tmpfile_unsupported errno=<name>`, sends a CRITICAL `o_tmpfile_unsupported` through §3.6, writes no demand file and exits 3. The verdict path of §3.3.3 stands. r10's `DEMAND_TMP_PATTERN`, its orphan sweep and the C5-listing exception are withdrawn, so AUT-6 asks no change of C5 behaviour.
      - **Measured.** The primary path works under the production bwrap shape on this host's kernel (§0 (8)), and the data root is ext4 (§0 (14)). WP5b verify-first repeats the ext4 `O_TMPFILE` check, and the test, on the production tree (AE2).
    - **Alert records, outbox entries, claims and the dedupe marker.** Temporary file and final name both sit under `evidence/alerts`.
    - **No other path.** The stage never renames or links from `registry/demand/` into `evidence/` or back, and never into the read-only root. An `EXDEV` from any write is an `OSError` handled by §3.3.3's write-error rule (CRITICAL, counted), never swallowed.
  - **Listing fails closed (r9, AA4).** Each of these listings is an `os.scandir` whose every `OSError` is caught by name: the C4 verdict store (`derived/verdicts/` and each family's date directories), `registry/demand/<venue>/` and `evidence/demand/<venue>/`.
    - **Empty, not an error.** `ENOENT` of a family's date directory, or of the `evidence/demand/<venue>/` archive (nothing archived yet), is an empty listing. That errs toward writing a demand, which is restrictive.
    - **Missing venue directory (r10, AC4).** `ENOENT` of `registry/demand/<venue>/` is an empty listing only when the bind root `registry/demand/` `os.stat`s as a directory owned by this uid and `os.scandir` of it succeeds. In that case the writer creates the venue directory before the first write, with `os.mkdir(<venue dir>, <mode of registry/demand/>)` inside the bind; `EEXIST` from a concurrent creator counts as success. Any other error from the parent check or from the `mkdir` takes the next bullet (`dir=registry_demand`).
    - **Any other error**, including `ENOENT` of the `derived/verdicts/` root, does all of the following:
      - prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND INTEGRITY demand_listing_unreadable dir=<verdicts|registry_demand|evidence_demand> errno=<name>`;
      - sends a CRITICAL `demand_listing_unreadable` through `deliver_with_proof` (event in `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS`);
      - writes no demand file and exits `EXIT_INTEGRITY = 3`.
    - A blind listing is never read as "nothing to raise".
  - **Own summary line (r9, AA3).** On every path out of its main, the stage prints one line in a `finally`, the probe-failure and listing-failure paths included: `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND wrote=<n> skipped_existing=<k> invalid=<i> integrity_demand_write_failures=<f> journal_write_failures=<j> outbox_write_failures=<o> deadline_hit=<0|1> unprocessed=<n> evaluate_exit=<code|unrecorded> exit=<e>` (r10 adds `deadline_hit` and `unprocessed`; r11 removes r10's `write_mode`, AE2). #23 keys on this line's counters and on the `integrity_demand_write_failed` record (§3.2 #23).
    - **`START` line (r10, AB8).** The stage prints `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND START` as the first statement of its entry module, which imports only `os` and `sys` before printing. The health pass can then tell a bwrap launch failure (no `START` line) from an interpreter or import crash (`START`, but no summary line).
    - **Journal attribution (r10, AB10).** These lines help only if the journal attributes them to the invocation. Under `--unshare-pid` the stage's pid differs from its host pid. `test_demand_stage_stdout_carries_invocation_id_under_real_bwrap` therefore runs a scratch user unit with the production bwrap shape and asserts that both lines carry `_SYSTEMD_INVOCATION_ID` and `_SYSTEMD_USER_UNIT`. If they do not, the stage writes the same two records instead, each write-once and inside the alerts bind under the same-bind rule:
      - `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/stage_status/demand/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<invocation_id>.start.json`;
      - `….summary.json`.

      The health pass then reads those files instead of the journal. The literal `DEMAND_STAGE_SUMMARY_SINK` (`journal` or `file`) is set from the WP5b result. **(r11, E-7a)** The evaluate stage now also runs under the wrapper, without `--unshare-pid`, and the same test asserts its `START` and summary lines carry the unit's `_SYSTEMD_INVOCATION_ID`.
    - **Retention of the stage-status files (r11, LOW-r11-8).** When `DEMAND_STAGE_SUMMARY_SINK = file`, the demand stage prunes after its writes, and only if time remains before its deadline. Each run removes at most one `stage_status/demand/<YYYY-MM-DD>/` directory older than `STAGE_STATUS_RETENTION_DAYS = 8`, one day more than the live-proof window. These unlinks are rows of the demand stage's `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY` entry, limited to that path constant. A removal error prints `stage_status_prune_failed errno=<name>` and counts in `journal_write_failures` (#23); it never blocks a demand write. The health pass reads only the last 2 days. The evaluate stage's `.aut6-intraday.stage_status` is one file, rewritten each run, and needs no retention rule.
  - **Unit result is the OR of both stages (r9, AA2).**
    - **Reading the evaluate result.** After its own work, the demand stage reads the stage-status file. `evaluate_exit` is the recorded code when the file's `invocation_id` equals `$INVOCATION_ID`. Otherwise it is `unrecorded`: the evaluate stage was killed, or the file is unreadable.
    - **Exit code.** The stage exits with its own non-zero code if it has one: `EXIT_INTEGRITY = 3`, else `EXIT_DEMAND_DEADLINE = 5` (r10, AC3). Otherwise it exits `EXIT_EVALUATE_FAILED = 4` when `evaluate_exit` is non-zero or `unrecorded`, else 0.
    - **What #22 can read.** The unit fails if either stage failed. The codes are in the unit-failed entry and the `98e32220…` exit entry under `USER_INVOCATION_ID`, plus the summary line, which carries `_SYSTEMD_INVOCATION_ID` (§0 (5)). **(r11, LOW-r11-1 and LOW-r11-6; r10's AB6 kept as the defensive tie-break)** The reader keeps only the invocation's exit entries whose `COMMAND=` is `ExecStart`. `COMMAND=` holds the command type, not the argv (measured, §0 (16)), so it cannot name the stage. It does not need to. An ignored `ExecStart=-` failure writes no exit entry (§0 (4)), so an invocation's one `ExecStart` exit entry is the demand stage's, unless the demand stage never started, which the table below classifies by the missing `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND START` line. If more than one `ExecStart` exit entry is ever found for one invocation, which is not expected, the reader uses the one with the greatest `__REALTIME_TIMESTAMP`, ties broken by journal order, and the finding carries `metrics.exit_entries=<n>`. `test_intraday_exit_entries_on_real_journal_scratch_unit` checks the single-entry fact on the real journal (WP3).
    - **Classification.** The health pass classifies a failed intraday invocation as follows:

    **Demand row (r10: one row added, the evaluate rows moved to the independent rule below):**

    | Exit status of the last command (AB6) | `PRODUCER_INTRADAY_DEMAND` lines for the invocation | #22 finding (CRITICAL) |
    |---|---|---|
    | 3 | summary present | the stage's own INTEGRITY cause (already paged by the stage) |
    | 5 | summary present, `deadline_hit=1` | `demand_stage_deadline_hit`, with `metrics.unprocessed` (r10, AC3) |
    | 4 | summary present | no demand-row finding; the evaluate rule below reports the cause |
    | 124 or 137 (`timeout` SIGTERM or SIGKILL) | summary absent | `demand_stage_timeout` |
    | any other non-zero | `START` absent, summary absent | **`bwrap_unavailable`** (r9, AA5), with `metrics.cause_hint=bwrap_launch` when a stderr line beginning `bwrap:` is journaled for the invocation, else `cause_hint=unknown` (r10, AB8) |
    | any other non-zero | `START` present, summary absent | `demand_stage_failed`, with `metrics.cause_hint=interpreter_or_import` (r10, AB8) |
    | any other non-zero | summary present | `demand_stage_failed`, with `metrics.exit_status` |

    - **Evaluate outcome, independent of the demand row (r10, AC2).** r9 read the evaluate result only through the demand row, so a demand exit 3, a timeout or a bwrap failure hid it. Now the health pass checks every intraday invocation it sees in its interval, meaning any journal entry under that `USER_INVOCATION_ID`. Whatever the unit result and whatever the demand row, it derives the evaluate outcome from the evaluate stage's own lines (stage 1 above) and cross-checks the demand line's `evaluate_exit`:
      - an evaluate summary with `exit=<n>`, n ≠ 0, gives `evaluate_stage_failed` with `metrics.evaluate_exit`;
      - a `START` line with no summary, where the time from that `START` to the demand stage's `START` (or, without one, to the invocation's last journal entry) is ≥ `EVALUATE_STAGE_BUDGET_S`, gives `evaluate_stage_timeout` (the 100 s kill);
      - anything else that leaves the outcome unknown gives `evaluate_stage_unrecorded`. That covers a missing summary (no `START`, a crash before the `finally`, an external SIGKILL), and a demand line reading `evaluate_exit=unrecorded` or non-zero while the evaluate summary reads `exit=0` or is absent. **(r11, E-7a)** With no evaluate `START` line and a journaled stderr line beginning `bwrap:` for the invocation, the finding carries `metrics.cause_hint=bwrap_launch`.
      - **Only ended invocations are judged (r11, AE4).** The health pass at `*:01/10` overlaps the intraday run that started at `*:00`, which may still be running. An invocation is judged only once one of these holds:
        - (a) it has an exit entry (`98e32220…`), a unit-failed entry or the oneshot's job-completion entry (`39f53479…`, the `Finished` line of §0 (4));
        - (b) its newest journal entry is older than `INTRADAY_INVOCATION_MAX_S = 125` s, the summed stage bounds 102 + 1 + 17 plus `TimeoutStopSec` 5 (§3.10);
        - (c) it is not the unit's current `InvocationID`, or the unit's `ActiveState` is not `activating`.

        An invocation that has not ended gets no finding and no record in this pass. Each pass's intraday window starts `INTRADAY_INVOCATION_MAX_S` before the previous pass's start, so the next pass sees the invocation whole, and the `(unit, USER_INVOCATION_ID, finding)` key stops it paging twice. A `systemctl` read error on (c) leaves (a) and (b) in force and makes the pass UNKNOWN as before. Tests: `test_overlapping_health_pass_does_not_page_in_flight_intraday_invocation` and `test_intraday_invocation_ended_by_exit_entry_age_or_not_activating`.

      Each finding is CRITICAL and keyed `(unit, USER_INVOCATION_ID, finding)` through the commit order. It is therefore reported alongside a demand-row finding, never instead of one. It is raised even if the unit result is success, which the OR rule makes impossible unless the demand stage itself is wrong.
    - **Episode dedupe (r10, AB7; r11, LOW-r11-4 adds `demand_stage_deadline_hit`).** `bwrap_unavailable`, `demand_stage_timeout` and `demand_stage_deadline_hit` repeat every 5 min while their cause stands.
      - **Records.** Each failed invocation still gets its classification record. Only the first invocation in an episode is delivered. Each later one gets an action record citing that delivered page, which explains it (§3.9 "Explained").
      - **State.** The episode state is `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/seen/_intraday_episode_<finding>.json` (`{schema, first_ns, last_paged_ns, last_invocation_id}`). Its single writer is the health pass, under its lock, in commit step 4.
      - **Re-page and end.** The page repeats once `now − last_paged_ns ≥ 86400 s`. **(r12, LOW-r12-1)** Only an invocation that the pass has judged as ended (AE4 (a)–(c)), and found with none of these findings, ends the episode. An invocation still running, or one skipped by the AE4 rule, never ends an episode, so an in-flight run between two failures cannot split one episode into two pages. A new episode pages at once. Test: `test_episode_ends_only_on_a_judged_ended_invocation`.
      - **Delivered only (r11, LOW-r11-2).** `last_paged_ns` is set only from a `delivered=true` record. An undelivered page leaves it unchanged (null in a new episode), so the next invocation in the episode pages again while the outbox retries the first.
      - **The stage's own entry (r11, LOW-r11-4; r12, LOW-r12-2).** The demand stage queues its own `demand_stage_deadline_hit` outbox entry (AC3) only when neither episode record shows a page for that finding within the last 86400 s. The summary line still shows `deadline_hit=1` on every hit.
        - **The stage records its own page (LOW-r12-2).** The health pass's file under `unit_health/seen/` lies outside the demand row's binds (§3.1.1), so the stage cannot write it under bwrap. The stage's half of the episode is therefore its own write-once-per-queue record `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/episodes/demand_stage_deadline_hit.json` (`{schema: "demand_stage_episode/v1", first_ns, last_queued_ns, outbox_entry, invocation_id}`; single writer, the demand stage; temporary file and `os.replace` inside the alerts bind, CA-1). It is rewritten right after the outbox entry is written, naming the entry's basename.
        - **The health pass binds that page to the episode.** In commit step 4, when a `delivered=true` record whose `outbox_entry` equals the recorded basename exists, the health pass sets its episode file's `last_paged_ns` from that record and pages nothing more for the episode. While the entry is undelivered, the health pass treats the episode as `alert_pending`, never as unpaged, so it does not page a second time. The outbox retries the entry, and an entry older than 24 h is #23 `abandoned`.
        - **Episode binding (r13, AG3).** The health pass binds a stage delivery record only if the record's `ts_ns` is at or after its episode file's `first_ns`. A delivered record from an earlier episode therefore never marks a new episode as paged, and the new episode pages at once.
        - **Stage reset (r13, AG3).** The stage resets its record on a clean pass. When a pass's summary shows `deadline_hit=0` and the record is not already reset, the stage rewrites it as `{schema, state: "reset", reset_ns}`, with no `outbox_entry`. The rewrite uses the same temporary file and `os.replace` inside the alerts bind. The next deadline hit therefore starts a new stage episode and queues.
        - **Test (r13, AG3).** `test_stage_delivery_record_binds_only_at_or_after_episode_first_ns_and_resets_on_clean_pass` has three cases:
          - a delivered record older than `first_ns` is not bound, and the episode pages;
          - a record at `first_ns` is bound, and there is no second page;
          - a `deadline_hit=0` pass leaves a reset record, and the next hit queues.
        - **Fail closed.** If either record is unreadable, the stage queues. If the stage record is unreadable, the health pass pages.
        - **Test.** `test_stage_page_recorded_and_bound_to_episode_without_double_page`: one deadline hit, the entry drained by redeliver and one delivered record gives exactly one page in 24 h across both writers; an unreadable stage record pages again.
      - **Fail closed.** An unreadable episode file pages.

    - **Delivery.** The health pass delivers each finding with proof through §3.6, acting as the notifier for a stage that could not speak; each event is in `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS`.
    - **Rejected alternative.** Running stage 2 from `ExecStopPost=` is not taken: the measured `ExecStart=-` behaviour plus the stage-status file gives the same OR with one fewer systemd mechanism.
  - **Residuals, stated (E-7 rule 4; r11, E-7a rule 5).** From r11 bwrap confines every AUT-6 unit and both stages (§3.1.1; r8–r10 confined the demand stage only). Against same-uid processes outside every row, `registry/demand/` and the exec store are protected by the lint and ARCH's stated residual, and are never described as enforced. Those processes are the node, the supervisor, the recorder and ingest, the operator CLIs and the notifier's fallback line.

#### 3.4.3 `aut6.node_liveness` (#6) (unchanged from r3)

- **Window and boot grace.** Evaluated in `[SELF_CHECK_UTC, 01:00Z)` = `[17:05Z, 01:00Z)` (`src/breezy/runtime/trade_supervisor_core.py:39`); before that `PASS window=boot_grace` (16:40Z–17:05Z) or `PASS window=closed`. Permit lines measured about 1 s after boot (16:50:15, 16:50:12, 16:50:40 on 09-30, 10-01, 10-02).
- **Relaunch grace.** Newest node log's name timestamp < 900 s old ⇒ `PASS reason=relaunch_grace`; void when ≥ 3 node logs were created in 3600 s ⇒ `FAIL reason=relaunch_loop`.
- **Conjuncts** (all required, each in `metrics`):
  1. **Process:** exactly one intent-flock holder (`count_intent_lock_holders` port). `/proc/locks` unreadable ⇒ UNKNOWN (r3, G14).
  2. **Log:** newest node-log mtime age ≤ `LOG_MTIME_MAX_S = 1500` (measured max inter-line gap 3 s; FQ funnel flush 900 s).
  3. **Permit:** the newest log holds a permit line with `expires_at_ns > now`.
  4. **Tape:** newest file mtime under the recorder live root `<BREEZY_TRADE_CATALOG_ROOT>/live` ≤ `TAPE_MTIME_MAX_S = max(1500, 2 × observed max gap)` (WP6 verify-first). Ingest output is not the source.
- **Halted-by-design:** §3.13. Unreadable halt state or fold ⇒ UNKNOWN.
- **FAIL** only on 2 consecutive failing passes.

#### 3.4.4 `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily` (`aut6.daily`)

- Inputs: NBP derived store, settlement truth, C2 labels (drill and voided-pair fills included for #14 and #20; excluded for #13), C1 decisions, FQ funnel files, the champion artefact, `systemctl --user show` (for #24, #31), and the exec-store family halt for the halted-by-design check of #14 and #20 (§3.13), read through the E-8 snapshot helper (r11, AE5, §3.1.1) with `take_flock=False`, advisory (r12, E-8a).
- **#16 input (r13, AG1, coordinator ruling; replaces r12's D-1 report file).** The daily does not recompute the shadow parity: `run_live_parity` drives the backtest engine, and its closure holds 33 adapter modules (§0 (20)). #16 consumes AUT-4's existing C4 `HEALTH` verdict `eval_replay_path` on the champion (AUT-4 r6 §3.11, lines 111, 274 and 370). AUT-4's `eval_offline` writes it on every run, alongside its `OFFLINE_CHALLENGER` verdict (AUT-4 r6 line 151). It carries the admitted days, the exclusions by reason, the parity results and the closure sha. AUT-6 reads it read-only through the C4 reader and judges its parity results for Take-set equality and Take numeric mismatches, as r11 judged the moved comparison. It imports nothing from AUT-4. AUT-4 produces no D-1 report file, and AUT-6 requests none.
  - **Selection.** #16 takes the newest `eval_replay_path` verdict for the venue (max `slot_start_ns`, then max `produced_at_ns`, AUT-4 r6's "newest" rule at line 324) whose `produced_at_ns` is within the last 26 h (`AUT4_REPLAY_MAX_AGE_S: Final = 93600`). **(r14, r13-final MEDIUM, SFH)** Among those verdicts, #16 takes the newest one whose replayed tape day equals D-1, with D taken from that verdict's own `slot_start_ns`. It never takes the plain maximum `slot_start_ns` over a backlog of verdicts. A backlog verdict for an older tape day is skipped, never judged stale. If verdicts newer than 26 h exist but none has tape day D-1, the result is `eval_replay_path_tape_day` (next bullet).
  - **Evaluation order (r14, r13-final MEDIUM, SFH).** The no-admitted-day branch of the Judgement bullet is evaluated first, before the tape-day and subject checks. A verdict that admitted no station-day is therefore `NO_INPUT parity_source=no_admitted_day` even when its tape-day metric is absent, so a routine venue-skip day never pages.
  - **Champion swap (r14, r13-final LOW).** For 26 h after a champion swap, `eval_replay_path_subject` and `c4_directory_missing` are exempt; the swap time is the `ts_ns` of the fold's →CHAMPION row. During that window #16 writes `NO_INPUT parity_source=champion_swap` instead, because AUT-4 has not yet replayed the new champion.
  - **Staleness (r13, AG1, SFH M1).** The selected verdict must name the current champion. Its `subject_family_id` must equal the fold's CHAMPION, and its `subject_artefact_sha256` must equal that family's bound artefact sha (§3.4.1). Its replayed tape day must equal D-1, where D is the UTC date of the verdict's `slot_start_ns`, that is, the day of the `eval_offline` run. The 05:30Z daily runs before that day's slot (about 11:00Z, AUT-4 r6 §3.10 K8), so D is the producer's day, not the daily's.
    - If any of these checks fails, #16 writes `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.unknown_reason=input_unreadable`. The same holds when the verdict is `ERROR` or when its parity results or tape day cannot be read. `metrics.unknown_detail` is one of `eval_replay_path_tape_day`, `eval_replay_path_subject` and `eval_replay_path_unparsable`.
    - At the daily's N = 1, that is a #25 FAIL and a CRITICAL.
    - The tape-day and parity-result names are AUT-4's pre-registered `metrics` names. WP7 verify-first (2) records them and pins them as `AUT4_REPLAY_PATH_METRICS: Final` in `detector_catalog.py`. A verdict that lacks a pinned name is `eval_replay_path_unparsable`, so a wrong pin pages on the first day the producer is live and never passes silently. **(r14, r13-final MEDIUM, TBA)** This is an AUT-4 build item: AUT-4 registers these names in `metric_registry` before go-live (§5).
  - **Judgement.** Parity results with a Take-set mismatch or a Take numeric mismatch ⇒ FAIL (CRITICAL, ALERT). Equality ⇒ PASS. A verdict that admitted no station-day on its tape day carries no parity result ⇒ `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.day_status=NO_INPUT, metrics.parity_source=no_admitted_day`. Three such tape days are AUT-4's own `eval_replay_path` FAIL, which AUT-4 alerts, so #16 adds no second page. **(r14, r13-final LOW)** A parity mismatch, by contrast, pages twice by design: once through AUT-4's own ALERT on its producer and once through #16's CRITICAL. The two are independent watchers of one fact. A duplicate page is preferred over a dedupe that would couple AUT-6 to AUT-4's delivery records.
  - **Before AUT-4 is live.** `NO_INPUT` is expected until AUT-4's `eval_offline` producer is live. AUT-4 r6's earliest ETA is 2026-11-23. While no `eval_replay_path` verdict newer than 26 h exists, the producer is not live and today is before `AUT4_REPLAY_EXPECTED_BY`, #16 writes `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.day_status=NO_INPUT, metrics.parity_source=producer_not_live` and sends nothing.
  - **Producer live.** The producer counts as live once the C4 store holds any `eval_offline` verdict, meaning an `OFFLINE_CHALLENGER` or an `eval_replay_path` verdict, since every `eval_offline` run writes both.
    - The daily looks for one in today's and yesterday's date directories of the champion's C4 directory.
    - On the first sighting it writes the marker `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/_aut6_daily_state/aut4_eval_offline_seen.json` (`{schema, first_seen_ns, verdict_id}`). The marker is write-once (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL`, 0444) and has a single writer, the daily. It lies inside the daily's `derived/verdicts` bind and is a row of the daily's `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY` entry.
    - The marker means a later gap in AUT-4's output is still seen as a gap after go-live. An unreadable marker counts as present, which fails toward the page.
    - **(r14, r13-final LOW)** The marker latches on the first `eval_offline` verdict of any outcome, `ERROR` included. An `ERROR` still proves the producer runs, and from then on a gap pages.
  - **Escalation (r13, AG1).** Escalation applies when no `eval_replay_path` verdict newer than 26 h exists and one of two conditions holds: the marker is present, or today is on or after `AUT4_REPLAY_EXPECTED_BY`. That constant is `Final[datetime.date] = 2026-11-30` in `detector_catalog.py`: AUT-4's ETA of 2026-11-23 plus 7 days.
    - #16 then writes `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.day_status=NO_INPUT, metrics.parity_source=missing, metrics.missing_days=k`.
    - `k` counts UTC days since the newest `eval_replay_path` verdict's `produced_at_ns`. If there has never been one, it counts from the later of the marker's `first_seen_ns` and `AUT4_REPLAY_EXPECTED_BY`.
    - For k < 3 the daily sends a WARN `aut6_shadow_parity_input_missing` at most once per UTC day (r14, r13-final LOW): the 13:00Z run does not repeat it. From k = 3 it sends the same event at CRITICAL on every daily run. Both go through `deliver_with_proof` (§3.6), and the event is in `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS`.
    - This path is `NO_INPUT`, not `input_unreadable`, so it pages on the ruled WARN-then-CRITICAL clock rather than through #25 at N = 1.
  - **C4 directory missing (r13, AG1).** This case applies once the marker is present. If the C4 verdict root `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/` or the champion's family directory is missing (`ENOENT`), the daily prints `PRODUCER_DAILY INTEGRITY c4_directory_missing` and sends a CRITICAL through §3.6, as `lock_file_missing` does (§3.1.1). #16 writes `input_unreadable` if it can write at all.
    - Before the marker exists, the same `ENOENT` is the not-live `NO_INPUT` above, or the escalation once `AUT4_REPLAY_EXPECTED_BY` has passed.
    - Any other listing error is `input_unreadable`.
  - **No other route.** No AUT-6 module imports `scripts/analysis/nbp_shadow_parity.py`, `shadow_replay.py` or any AUT-4 module (§3.1.2 closure test).
- **bwrap (r11, E-7a).** The unit runs under its row (§3.1.1). `~/.config/systemd/user` is re-bound read-only for #24, which compares installed units and drop-ins with `deploy/systemd/`, and it declares `OnFailure=breezy-autonomy-failed@%n`.
- Detectors: #9–#14, #16, #20, #24, #31 (and #25 for its own). **#15 moved to intraday (G9).**
- **Studies lock taken by the CLI, not by `flock(1)` (G11).** `ExecStart` runs the Python CLI directly; the CLI opens `~/.local/share/breezy/breezy-studies.lock` with `O_RDONLY|O_CLOEXEC` (r11, E-7a: the file already exists and lies outside the row's binds) and polls `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB)` until a 300 s deadline. On timeout it prints `PRODUCER_DAILY SKIPPED lock_timeout waited_s=300 slot=<05:30|13:00>`, writes one write-once skip record `~/.local/share/breezy/derived/verdicts/_aut6_daily_skips/<YYYY-MM-DD>_<slot>.json` `{ts_ns, slot, reason}` (single writer: this unit), and **exits 0**. A `flock -w` wrapper would exit 1 on timeout and make the unit fail as expected behaviour, which the score-3 criterion forbids.
- The health pass's #27 reads the skip records: `metrics.skips_since_last_verdict`, `metrics.last_skip_reason`. The first skip of a day sends a WARN; two skipped slots age the newest daily verdict past 30 h (prior 05:30Z + 31.5 h at the 13:00Z skip), so #27 FAILs CRITICAL with the reason attached.
- An unreadable or empty fold (§3.3.2) also writes a skip record with `reason=fold_unreadable` plus the CRITICAL.
- **Two slots:** 05:30Z and 13:00Z; the 13:00Z run is `PRODUCER_DAILY SKIPPED already_produced` (exit 0, no skip record) when every (subject, daily detector) has a verdict produced that UTC day.
- **Measured peak before enabling (ARCH §5.2, U6).** WP7 records the unit's `MemoryPeak` from one capped run before enabling its timer; its `MemoryMax=3G` is a studies-flock holder value well inside 14G.

#### 3.4.5 Z12 SLO (unchanged)

The producer runs at `*:00/5` and AUT-5's intraday engine at `*:02:30/5` (150 s offset). No `.path` unit. Both timers declare `AccuracySec=1s` (§3.10 R-e, r7 U5), so the 150 s offset holds to within 1 s. Worst case `DetectorEvent → entry veto` ≤ 11 min (`test_demotion_latency_slo`, producer half), inside ARCH's 15 min.

#### 3.4.6 `aut6.fill_better_than_ask` (#15) in full

- **Population.** Every C1 `LifecycleEvent(event=FILLED)` in the 36 h lookback whose `client_order_id` joins (C1 invariant ii) to an `OrderLink` with `time_in_force == IOC` and to an entry `DecisionRecord(kind=TrySubmit)`. **Excluded:** resting orders (`time_in_force ∈ {GTC, GTD, DAY}` or anything ≠ IOC: a resting bid legitimately fills below the current ask), exit orders (client ids carrying an `src/breezy/persistence/exit_tags.py` prefix), `source=canary`. Drill and voided-pair fills are included (W12). An unjoinable fill is not judged here; it is AUT-1's join-gap `HEALTH` FAIL.
- **Leg normalisation.** The leg comes from the instrument id (`leg_of`, composite NO-leg ids, G24). The C1 fill `px` is the Nautilus instrument price (the exec client books NO fills via `instrument_price_for_leg`). If a record instead carries the venue's representation of a NO buy, `(ORDER_SIDE_SELL, ORDER_INTENT_BUY_SHORT)` per `VENUE_SIDE_FOR_LEG`/`VENUE_INTENT_FOR_LEG`, its wire price is converted with `instrument_price_for_leg("no", px)`. If the price space cannot be decided from the record (memory note `venue-represents-no-buy-as-sell-buy-short`), the fill is `INCONCLUSIVE reason=leg_price_space_undecidable`, never FAIL.
- **Book at the fill, not the decision ask.** `ref_ask = min(decision ask from the C1 `depth_ref` Depth10 payload, every bought-leg best ask in catalog Depth10 frames with ts in [eval_ns, fill ts_ns])`, read with one native `order_book_depth10(instrument_ids=[id], start=eval_ns − 60 s, end=fill_ts + 5 s)` call. For a NO leg without its own depth rows, the NO ask is `1 − best YES bid` of the paired YES instrument via `instrument_price_for_leg` (WP6 verify-first records which form the catalog holds). Same-timestamp book updates are applied before the quote derived from them (L-25 rule).
- **Rule.** `FAIL` iff `fill_px < ref_ask − 0.001`. A legitimate ask drop between decision and fill lowers `ref_ask`, so the fill matches a displayed level and PASSes, with `metrics.ask_drop_between_decision_and_fill=true`.
- **Missing book.** No Depth10 frame covering `[eval_ns, fill_ts]` (ingest lag or gap) ⇒ that fill is `pending_depth`; the verdict is INCONCLUSIVE only if a pending fill is older than 6 h (`unknown_streak_max = 72` passes), then #25 CRITICAL. Pending fills are re-evaluated every pass inside the 36 h lookback.
- **Frame gap (r5, F9).** If `max_frame_gap_ns`, the widest interval between consecutive Depth10 frames for the bought leg inside [eval_ns, fill_ts], exceeds `FILL_FRAME_GAP_MAX_NS = 2_000_000_000` (2 s), the book in that interval is not known well enough to call a fill better than every displayed ask: the fill is `INCONCLUSIVE reason=frame_gap` and never FAIL, so it can never HALT the venue. Such fills count toward `metrics.inconclusive_frame_gap`; they are not pending (no later ingest can close a recorded gap) and do not feed the #25 streak, but a day with any is listed in the WP9 report for review.
- **Latency (r4).** Fill → depth in the catalog (ingest every 15 min; measured catalog staleness up to 1769 s on a healthy host, r2) → next producer pass (≤ 5 min), which writes the verdict **and** the `integrity_floor` demand file → watch-actor tick (≤ 60 s): **≈ 36 min worst case on a healthy ingest**, independent of the engine. The engine HALT row follows within 150 s of the producer. A stalled ingest extends detection until #25 fires at 6 h; the repeated-defect exposure in that time is bounded by `rung_net_position_held`, the per-position cap and the venue daily budget (operator-owned values, never read).
- **Remaining false-positive risk, stated.** (i) A venue fill at a price inside the spread that never appeared in a Depth10 frame (hidden or very short-lived liquidity between two 10-level snapshots) would FAIL; no such case is known on this venue, and the action is a HALT on an INTEGRITY freeze, which fails closed. (ii) A recorder-vs-node clock skew > 5 s could exclude the matching frame; the window pads −60 s/+5 s and records `metrics.max_frame_gap_ns`, and a gap over 2 s is INCONCLUSIVE (r5, F9), which also narrows risk (i) to sub-2 s liquidity. (iii) A mis-joined `client_order_id` is impossible by C1 invariant (ii) but would surface as AUT-1's join gap first. **Clearing:** the INTEGRITY HALT freezes the venue; it clears only through build-side incident handling (ARCH W15): root cause evidenced under `docs/incident-reports/`, peer-reviewed, then cleared through the halt CLI and the registry CLI on the C5 API. Never an operator decision, never automatic.
- **Memory.** ≤ 5 fills a day, one bounded single-instrument query each; WP6 verify-first measures the ΔRSS of one query on the largest instrument-day in a fresh child (L-49 amendment) and requires it inside the 512M unit cap with 2× margin.

#### 3.4.7 `fee_schedule` (#32) in full (r4, P7-11)

- **Purpose.** ARCH C5 makes rollback selection, the AUT-7 drill start gate and ROOT_ADMIT read "the newest accepted AUT-6 `DRIFT` verdict for `fee_schedule` … a PASS within `ATTEST_VERDICT_VALIDITY_H`"; `is_fee_verified` is node memory and unreadable from the engine (G39).
- **Input.** C1 `DetectorEvent(detector="fee_schedule", state ∈ {AGREE, DISAGREE, UNKNOWN})` written by AUT-1's in-node writer at each probe outcome (the probe fires at node start and every 2 h, `src/breezy/strategy/current_rung_hold/fee_drift_probe.py:177,330-331`). The id `fee_schedule` is the C6 id ARCH names; AUT-1 emits it (§5).
- **Rule.** Over today's and yesterday's C1 files: PASS iff the newest event is `AGREE`, `now − ts_ns ≤ 14400 s` (the probe's own `_FEE_VERIFIED_STALENESS_NS`, `:184`) and no later `DISAGREE` or `UNKNOWN` exists. `DISAGREE` ⇒ FAIL. No event in 14400 s, `UNKNOWN`, or an unreadable file ⇒ `INCONCLUSIVE` (`metrics.unknown_reason`), never PASS.
- **Timing.** The node is down from STOP (16:40Z) to LAUNCH (16:50Z). The pass at 16:35Z sees an AGREE from at most 4 h earlier and writes (or refreshes) a PASS valid 8 h from its slot, which the 16:45Z pre-launch pass can cite. A DISAGREE also trips #17's exec-store halt; #32's FAIL adds no second action (ALERT).
- **Validity and refresh** as §3.4.1 (intraday, 8 h, hourly refresh). It is a `DRIFT` verdict and is not in `attest_required_detectors`.

#### 3.4.8 Drill-marker read (#8, #8h) (r4, ARCH C6 U7, V12)

- Open `~/.local/share/breezy/registry/drill/` with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat`-check it (a directory, owned by this uid, mode 0700), then `openat` the marker `marker.json` with `O_NOFOLLOW`; read once, ≤ 4096 bytes, parse the exact-set body.
- Outcomes per detector id: marker `ENOENT` ⇒ PASS; marker names this id ⇒ FAIL; marker names the other drill id ⇒ PASS for this id (one action class per detector, C6); directory `ENOENT`, any other `OSError`, a symlink, oversize, a parse error or an unknown `detector` value ⇒ `ERROR`, which never satisfies a RESUME (`test_drill_marker_read_error_never_resumes`, producer half).
- Drill verdicts carry `assumptions:["drill"]` and `metrics.cause_class="DRILL"`.

### 3.5 Self-heal, native (r14: the systemd watchdog replaces r4–r13's executor and drill probe)

- **What heals.** systemd does.
  - **The mechanism.** A member unit declares `WatchdogSec=`, and its process sends `WATCHDOG=1` only while its capture counters advance. If the pings stop for `WatchdogSec`, systemd sends `WatchdogSignal`, records `Result=watchdog` and restarts the unit under `Restart=`. This was measured on this host, both plain and under bwrap (§0.1 (24)–(28)).
  - **What AUT-6 does not build.** No restart call site, no selfheal record, no cap counter, no probe, and no gate or sender of its own.
- **Ownership (r14, reconciled with AUT-1 r9).** AUT-1 r9 was being drafted at the same time; its module map is read at `AUT-1-data-capture_plan_r9.md:208-218`.
  - **AUT-1 r9 owns the recorder's side:**
    - the gate and the stdlib sender (`src/breezy/adapters/polymarket_us/recorder_watchdog.py`: `classify_recorder_sample`, `sd_notify`) and their call site in `sample_feed_health`;
    - the recorder unit's watchdog lines;
    - the `ExecStopPost=` stop hook, which delivers the per-kill CRITICAL through AUT-6's `deliver_with_proof` and writes `recorder_watchdog/v1`.
  - **AUT-6 owns the SELF_HEAL contract that side must meet:**
    - `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` and the directive rules below, both checked by AUT-6's config test;
    - the gate requirements below, checked by AUT-6's contract test against AUT-1's pure classifier;
    - #21 (the HEALTH verdict, with a fallback page);
    - `watchdog_disarmed` and the Y1 watchdog exception (§3.9);
    - the notifier's start-limit branch.
  - **One implementation.** AUT-6 builds no second gate, sender or per-kill page (DRY).
- **Members.** `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS: Final = ("breezy-quote-tape.service",)`, a literal in `detector_catalog.py`.
  - A unit joins only if it is a long-running daemon whose liveness is a capture counter, and its owner has filed a gate for it.
  - Never a member: a oneshot, `breezy-trade*`, the supervisor, `breezy-autonomy-engine*` or the dead-man (ARCH §4.5). `test_watchdog_units_are_literal_and_exclude_trade_supervisor_engine` carries this rule.
  - `breezy-nws-ingest` is not a member.
- **Directive rules for every member.** AUT-6's contract; AUT-1 writes the recorder's lines.
  - **`Type=notify` (ruling item 1).** `READY=1` is sent on the first ping the gate allows, so systemd arms the watchdog only once capture is flowing. AUT-1 r9's unit row lists no `Type=` change: **cross-plan item X-2.**
  - **`NotifyAccess=all` (ruling item 5).** With `main`, any process started ahead of Python (a `timeout`, a wrapper or bwrap) makes systemd ignore the child's messages. In that case the start fails with `Result=timeout` (§0.1 (27)). The recorder runs Python directly today, so `main` works there now; `all` stops a later prefix from silently disarming the watchdog. AUT-1 r9's unit row says `NotifyAccess=main`: **cross-plan item X-1.**
  - **`WatchdogSec` within [120 s, 600 s].** AUT-1 r9 chose 600 s. The floor sits above the 60 s Nautilus idle-timeout reconnect. WP4 verify-first V-W2 measures the longest legitimate gap between counter advances over 7 days, and a gap above W/2 is a finding for AUT-1.
  - **`Restart=` in `WATCHDOG_RESTART_SET`.** The set is `{"always", "on-failure", "on-abnormal", "on-watchdog"}`: the systemd.service(5) values that restart on a watchdog timeout. The recorder keeps `Restart=always`, which restarts on a watchdog timeout (§0.1 (28)). Narrowing it to `on-watchdog` would stop the crash restarts that the 2026-09-09 fix relies on.
  - **`WatchdogSignal`.** It must be `SIGTERM`, or `SIGABRT` together with `LimitCORE=0`. The default SIGABRT ends `code=dumped` (§0.1 (24)), and the recorder has `LimitCORE=infinity` (§0.1 (23)), so SIGABRT alone would write a core of about 1–2 GB on each kill. AUT-1 r9 chose SIGTERM, which meets the rule.
  - **`TimeoutStartSec` covering start to `READY=1`.** It is at least 90 s, and at least twice the p99 start-to-first-frame time from V-W3.
  - **No `RuntimeMaxSec`** (R-c).
  - **Start limit, which is the cap. Stated deviation O-8 (§11).**
    - **What the ruling says.** The cap goes in `StartLimitBurst`/`StartLimitIntervalSec`.
    - **What the recorder keeps.** `StartLimitIntervalSec=0`.
    - **Why.** Its unit file records that a start limit made the 2026-09-09 outage permanent and lost 6.7 h of tape that cannot be re-fetched (§0.1 (23)). systemd counts every start against the limit, crash restarts included (§0.1 (24), (28)), so any finite limit would bring that failure back.
    - **The recorder's bound instead.** The existing backoff, `RestartSteps=4`/`RestartMaxDelaySec=480`. Every kill is still paged (AUT-1's stop hook, with #21 as the fallback), and AUT-1's `recorder_stale` storm veto applies at 3 kills in a trading day.
    - **Other members.** A member with a finite start limit must have `StartLimitBurst ≤ 3` and `OnFailure=breezy-autonomy-failed@%n.service`, and exhaustion pages through that notifier (below).
    - **If the literal ruling stands.** The coordinator and the reviewers rule on O-8. If they keep the ruling, the recorder gets `StartLimitBurst=3`/`StartLimitIntervalSec=86400` plus `OnFailure=`, and R-43 records the hazard this re-opens.
- **Gate requirements (AUT-6's contract on `classify_recorder_sample`).**
  - **Withhold on a stall.** It returns no-ping when the capture counters have not advanced (frames or on-disk bytes, per AUT-1) while at least one instrument is subscribed.
  - **Empty listing.** It pings for a legitimately empty listing while discovery reloads stay fresh: at most twice the unit's 15 min `POLYMARKET_US_DISCOVERY_RELOAD_INTERVAL_MINS` old.
  - **Launch-window deferral (ruling item 4, ARCH V7).** It pings while `[now, now + WatchdogSec + TimeoutStopSec + TimeoutStartSec]` meets `[16:30Z, 17:10Z)`. It journals the deferral once per stall and withholds again from 17:10Z. AUT-1 r9's module map does not show this rule: **cross-plan item X-3.**
  - **One thread.** It runs on the node's own timer, never on a second thread. A second pinging thread would defeat the watchdog.
  - **Limit.** Deferral covers a recorder whose event loop runs but whose counters stall. A hung loop sends no ping in or out of the window, so systemd restarts it at once, just as it restarts a crash today.
  - **The sender.** It is a no-op returning `False` when `$NOTIFY_SOCKET` is unset. On a send error it logs and never raises.
- **#21 and paging.**
  - **Per-kill page.** AUT-1's stop hook pages each kill.
  - **#21 verdict.** #21 writes the HEALTH verdict for every member `UNIT_RESULT=watchdog` ending (§3.2, §3.9).
  - **Fallback page.** #21 pages CRITICAL itself only when that invocation has no `delivered=true` stop-hook record by the health pass after next. A missing hook therefore fails toward the page, and a working hook is never paged twice.
- **The notifier on a watchdog failure.** `OnFailure=` fires on **every** watchdog failure, including those followed by a restart (§0.1 (25)). So `breezy-autonomy-failed@` first reads `ActiveState,SubState,NRestarts,Result` of the failed unit:
  - **`SubState=auto-restart` (a restart is pending).** It journals `AUTONOMY_FAILED unit=<u> result=watchdog restarting=1 nrestarts=<n>`, sends nothing and exits 0.
  - **`ActiveState=failed` (the start limit is exhausted).** It pages CRITICAL `watchdog_start_limit_exhausted`, keyed `(unit, InvocationID)`, through the r12 marker path. The health pass's #22 treats a delivered marker as the page.
  - **Unreadable state.** It pages, failing toward the page.
  - **Coalescing (a stated reading, not measured).** Two failures inside one notifier run may be coalesced by systemd. #21 and the `NRestarts` rule still count both.
- **No oneshot is a member.** A failed or timed-out oneshot is classified, alerted with delivery proof (#22) and retried at its next timer elapse (unchanged from r6, S1).
- **Memory (L-49).** systemd restarts a member whatever the cause. A watchdog ending with `MemorySwapPeak > 0` or `MemoryPeak ≥ 0.9 × MemoryHigh` carries `metrics.memory_ceiling_suspect=true` on #21 and is paged CRITICAL by #21 itself, whatever the stop hook did. A memory-starved recorder is therefore never restarted in a quiet loop (L-49 triage line).
- **Deleted (ruling items 1–3).**
  - The executor: `self_heal.py::restart_unit`, `restarts_in_trading_day` and `trading_date_for`.
  - The membership and cap: `SELF_HEAL_RESTARTABLE_UNITS` and `SELF_HEAL_MAX_RESTARTS_PER_UNIT_PER_DAY`.
  - The records: `evidence/selfheal/**` and their `.outcome.json` companions.
  - The checks and windows: the per-InvocationID idempotency check, the trading-day restart window, the executor's launch-window deferral, and V-4 and V-5.
  - The HUNG→restart path and the HUNG class.
  - The drill probe: `breezy-autonomy-selfheal-probe.{service,timer}`, `self_heal_probe_cli.py` and `evidence/selfheal_probe/state.json`.
  - The `ALERT_ONLY`/`SELF_HEAL` mode, with `self_heal_mode_degraded`, `self_heal_mode_state_lost` and `self_heal_mode_state_corrupt`.
  - r14's own first draft of `src/breezy/runtime/sd_watchdog.py`, withdrawn in favour of AUT-1 r9's module.

### 3.6 Delivery proof and the ALERT executor (r4: ARCH §4.6, V1, V3, U3, R9.2-Z4, erratum E-1)

#### 3.6.1 API

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class DeliveryProof:
    delivered: bool
    status_class: Literal["2xx", "3xx", "4xx", "5xx", "transport", "not_configured", "outbox_overflow"]
    event: str
    ts_ns: int
    recorded: bool

def deliver_with_proof(sink: AlertSink, payload: AlertPayload, *, writer: str,
                       records: DeliveryRecordWriter,
                       attempt_kind: Literal["alert", "retry", "canary", "drain"],
                       drill: bool = False,
                       now_ns: Callable[[], int] = time.time_ns) -> DeliveryProof: ...
```

- Calls `emit` **directly on the webhook branch** (via `TeeAlertSink.sinks`), never through `emit_alert` or the tee, because both swallow failure (G25); every other branch receives the payload through `emit_alert` first. Proof is an HTTP 2xx. `httpx.HTTPStatusError` maps to `status_code // 100` (3xx included); other `httpx`/`ssl`/`OSError` errors map to `transport`. Only the exception type is logged. Per-attempt hard timeout `ALERT_DELIVERY_TIMEOUT_S = 5` (ceiling ≤ 10).
- **`attempt_kind` (r4, ARCH enum):** `alert` (the first attempt by the originating process, including `breezy-check-alerts`), `retry` (a later attempt by the same claimant), `canary`, `drain` (an attempt on an entry claimed from the outbox by a drainer). r3's `inline`, `redeliver` and `check` map to `alert`, `drain` and `alert` (writer `check`).

#### 3.6.2 Per-attempt records (ARCH §4.6, P6-6, V3)

- One write-once file per attempt: `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<ts_ns>_<writer>_<d|f>.json` (`d` delivered, `f` failed), 0600 in 0700 directories, written `mkstemp` then `os.link` (never replaces an existing name; a same-name collision retries at `ts_ns + 1`), file and directory fsynced.
- **Fields:** ARCH's `event`, `ts_ns`, `delivered`, `status_class`, `severity`, `attempt_kind`, `drill`, plus `schema: "alert_delivery/v1"` (common rule) and two diagnostic fields, `site` and `outbox_entry` (the entry's basename, no path). No URL, message, detail or exception text. The C5 reader uses only ARCH's fields.
- **Writer ids** (one per process type; L-50): `node`, `intraday`, `daily`, `health`, `redeliver`, `canary`, `check`, `deadman`, `engine`, `probe`, and `legacy-<component>` for the migrated oneshot sites.
- **Record write failure:** never blocks delivery; logs `alert_delivery_journal_unwritable` at ERROR (type only), increments the process counter printed in the unit summary line as `journal_write_failures=<n>`, and returns `recorded=False`. #23 reads both. Because #5 counts only records, an unwritable record root also ends in the node veto after 26 h: fail closed.

#### 3.6.3 Durable outbox, claim and reclaim (r4: ARCH V1, U3, R9.2-Z4; **erratum E-1**)

- **Scope (V3).** Every proof-bearing alert: every CRITICAL from any site, and every alert of any severity emitted for a C6 detector action or a named failure mode, from any area. `PROOF_BEARING_EVENTS` (literal, in `src/breezy/runtime/alert_delivery.py`) holds the detector-action and failure-mode event names; the gate test derives the detector half from `CATALOG` so a new detector cannot be missed (`test_detector_and_failure_mode_alerts_use_delivery_proof`). Other WARNING and INFO lines keep `emit_alert`.
- **Entry.** Before any HTTP attempt the originating process writes `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/<ts_ns>_<event>.json` (0600, schema `alert_outbox/v1`, the `AlertPayload` fields plus `severity`, `drill`, `writer`), via `mkstemp`, file `fsync`, `os.link` to the final name (no replace; a collision retries at `ts_ns + 1`), then directory `fsync`. Write-once.
- **Claim (E-1 order, binding).** A drainer claims an entry in two steps: **first** `os.utime(entry)` stamps the claim time on the entry; **then** `os.rename(entry, ~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/claimed/<drainer>/<name>)` claims it, and the directory is fsynced. The losing renamer gets `ENOENT` and never sends. **Never rename then utime:** a crash between those two would leave a claimed entry carrying its old mtime, which a second drainer would reclaim at once while the first might still send.
  - A crash after the `utime` and before the `rename` leaves the entry unclaimed with a fresh mtime. It stays drainable, because **entry age is read from the filename `ts_ns`, never from the mtime**; only claim age reads the mtime.
- **Attempt and completion.** The claimant attempts delivery under `ALERT_DELIVERY_TIMEOUT_S`, writes the delivery record, and only then removes the claimed file and fsyncs the directory. Before each retry it re-stamps its claimed path with `os.utime`; `ENOENT` there means another drainer reclaimed it, so it stops without sending. Delivery is at-least-once: a duplicate page is possible, a lost CRITICAL is not.
- **Reclaim.** A drainer may reclaim any `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/claimed/<other>/<name>` whose mtime is older than `ALERT_CLAIM_STALE_S = 60` (ceiling ≥ 3 × `ALERT_DELIVERY_TIMEOUT_S` = 15), with the same order: `utime`, then `rename` into its own `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/claimed/<drainer>/`. Each reclaim increments `metrics.reclaims` in the drain summary; more than 3 for one entry is a #23 FAIL.
- **Drainers.**
  - The originating oneshot claims its own entry immediately (drainer id = its writer id) and attempts it once (`attempt_kind=alert`).
  - The node worker (§3.6.4) claims node entries, retries every 20 s while it holds the claim (`retry`), and drains leftovers at boot (`drain`).
  - `breezy-autonomy-alert-redeliver` (AUT-6, every 5 min) claims unclaimed entries whose filename age is ≥ `REDELIVER_MIN_AGE_S = 60` (an originator that died before claiming) and stale claims (`drain`).
  - AUT-5's dead-man drains entries older than `ALERT_OUTBOX_STALE_S = 300` (ceiling ≤ 300) by the same `drain_outbox(drainer="deadman")` call (ARCH §4.6, §5.2 dead-man row).
- **Bound.** `ALERT_OUTBOX_MAX = 128` files in `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/` plus `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/claimed/` (ceiling ≤ 256). **16 of those slots are reserved for `severity=CRITICAL` (r5, F5):** `ALERT_OUTBOX_CRITICAL_RESERVED = 16` (a `Final` in `src/breezy/runtime/alert_delivery.py`, asserted `< ALERT_OUTBOX_MAX`), so a non-CRITICAL entry is refused once the count reaches `ALERT_OUTBOX_MAX − 16 = 112`, and a CRITICAL is refused only at 128. A WARN storm therefore cannot crowd out a CRITICAL. Past the bound for its severity, the payload is not queued; a `delivered=false`, `status_class=outbox_overflow` record is written and #23 FAILs.
- **Abandonment.** An entry whose filename age exceeds 24 h is still attempted, and #23 FAILs naming it (`abandoned`).
- **One-writer row** (`test_autonomy_files_have_one_writer`, U3): `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/outbox/*.json` → write-once (no lock) → creators {node, intraday, daily, health, canary, check, deadman, engine, probe, legacy-*}; rename claim → {each originator for its own entry, node worker, redeliver, deadman}; remove → the current claimant only.
- **Write-on-change is not consumed by a failed send:** a producer's transition alert counts as sent only on `delivered=true`; otherwise the outbox carries it and the next verdict keeps `metrics.alert_pending=true`.
- **Outbox write failure:** logs `alert_outbox_unwritable`, writes a record with `outbox_write_failed=true` when records are writable, increments `outbox_write_failures=<n>`, and still attempts the delivery once; #23 FAILs.

#### 3.6.4 Node delivery off the event loop (W9)

- `OffLoopAlertSink(inner_webhook, *, close_deadline_s=10.0)`, built by `resolve_node_alert_sink()`.
  - The local log branch stays inline.
  - For a proof-bearing payload the calling thread writes the outbox entry (one small local file with two fsyncs, no network) and enqueues its name; enqueue never blocks. One dedicated worker thread, touching no exec-store state, claims (§3.6.3, E-1 order) and attempts it.
  - Past the bound for its severity (§3.6.3: 112 for non-CRITICAL, 128 for CRITICAL; r5, F5) nothing is queued and the `outbox_overflow` record is written.
  - `close()` drains within `close_deadline_s`, then leaves the rest in the outbox, unclaimed or with claims that go stale and are reclaimed.
- WP1b verify-first measures the p99 of the two fsyncs on `~/.local/share/breezy` against the `try_submit` budget (`test_try_submit_latency_independent_of_webhook_latency` stays the gate).
- **Wiring:** AUT-5a swaps the four node sites (`src/breezy/app/trade.py:474,533,1148`, `src/breezy/runtime/node_config.py:475`) to `resolve_node_alert_sink()`. Oneshots use the synchronous path.

#### 3.6.5 Legacy sites, G26 fix, egress, sandboxing

- `resolve_alert_sink` builds `TeeAlertSink(LoggingAlertSink(), JournalingWebhookAlertSink(url, records=…, outbox=…))`. `JournalingWebhookAlertSink(WebhookAlertSink).emit`, for a proof-bearing payload, writes the outbox entry, claims it, runs `deliver_with_proof` on the parent branch, and **re-raises** `AlertNotDeliveredError` so `emit_alert` contains it and logs as today. Existing tee-containment tests stay green unedited. This migrates every CRITICAL site without touching the 48 call sites (Z13); `test_critical_alerts_use_delivery_proof` pins it.
- **G26:** `check_alerts_cli.check_alerts` uses `deliver_with_proof(attempt_kind="alert", writer="check")` and exits `EXIT_DELIVERY_FAILED` iff not delivered.
- **Egress unchanged:** the only URL source is `BREEZY_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (G27); `test_autonomy_alert_egress_not_widened` and the AST scan pin it.
- **Sandbox configuration (r8, E-7: configuration, not a control).** Every unit that loads `alerts.env` and declares `ProtectHome=` or `ProtectSystem=strict` also declares `ReadWritePaths=%h/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts`, pinned by `test_every_alerting_sandboxed_unit_config_lists_the_delivery_journal`, so the configuration stays coherent if a later host enforces it. The binding guarantee that records are writable is #23's `os.access(W_OK)` and free-space check, not this line. **(r11, E-7a)** It is now also the bwrap row: every AUT-6 unit that writes records binds `evidence/alerts` (§3.1.1), and its self-probe shows that bind writable at every start.

### 3.7 Canary and off-host absence (r4: ARCH §4.6, W13, U12, §5.2 table)

- **`breezy-autonomy-canary`** (oneshot, own lock `-n`, `MemoryMax=128M`, `TimeoutStartSec=55` with `TimeoutStopSec=5` (r10, E-9; r11 corrects this line, which r10 left at 60, to match §3.10), under its bwrap row (r11, E-7a, §3.1.1)), `OnCalendar=*-*-* *:45:00 UTC`, `*-*-* 16:30:00 UTC` and `*-*-* 17:10:00 UTC`, `Persistent=false`.
- **Scheduled slots (always send):** 15:45Z (primary, ahead of the receiver's 16:15Z absence check), 16:30Z and 16:45Z, the two firings ARCH §5.2 lists inside `[16:30Z, 17:10Z)`. Each sends `AlertPayload(severity="INFO", event="autonomy_canary", site="global", detail="canary_ok")` via `deliver_with_proof(attempt_kind="canary")`.
- **Retry gate (every other `:45` firing and the 17:10Z firing):** sends iff (a) the newest canary attempt failed and no canary `delivered=true` record exists since it, or (b) today's or yesterday's record directory is absent or unreadable (the gate fails toward sending). Otherwise `AUTONOMY_CANARY skipped=not_due`, exit 0. Period `CANARY_RETRY_PERIOD_MIN` = 60 (ceiling ≤ 60).
- **Launch-window suppression (U12):** no retry runs in `[16:30Z, 17:10Z)`; a failed 16:30Z or 16:45Z canary is recorded and its retry is deferred to the 17:10Z firing, journaled `AUTONOMY_CANARY retry_deferred until=17:10Z`. #23 judges a failed 16:30Z or 16:45Z slot only once the 17:10:00Z firing's canary record (`d` or `f`, `slot=1710`) exists, or from the 17:21Z health pass on (r6, S2). The 17:10Z firing may run until 17:11:01Z (its 60 s bound, `TimeoutStartSec=55` plus `TimeoutStopSec=5` under E-9 (r10), plus `AccuracySec=1s`, r7 U5), so the 17:11Z pass can start before its record is written; r5 F13's 17:11Z rule is replaced. Until then a pass reports `metrics.canary_slot_pending`, never FAIL. ARCH's 17:00Z dead-man reports the failed 16:45Z record (R9.2-Z6, read under §4.6 per erratum E-4).
- Prints `AUTONOMY_CANARY delivered=<0|1> status_class=<c> slot=<1545|1630|1645|retry|1710>` and exits 0 either way; a failure queues CRITICAL `autonomy_canary_undelivered` (proof-bearing) and #23 reports it as a `HEALTH` FAIL.
- **Veto rule (#5, ARCH C5):** only `delivered=true` records with `attempt_kind=canary` or `severity=CRITICAL` reset the 26 h clock.

#### 3.7.1 Arming marker (r5, F1)

- `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/alerts/armed.json` (0444, schema `alerts_armed/v1`, `{ts_ns, record}` where `record` is the basename of the first delivered canary record). Single writer: the canary unit, `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` after `mkstemp` + `os.link`, file and directory fsynced; `EEXIST` is success. Never rewritten, never deleted by any code (one-writer row in `test_autonomy_files_have_one_writer`).
- Written after the first `delivered=true` canary record. If the write fails, the canary counts `journal_write_failures` (#23) and retries the write after its next delivered canary while the marker is absent. **(r6, S5)** If the marker is still absent when the oldest delivered canary record in the two scanned directories is older than 24 h, #23 FAILs `armed_marker_missing` (CRITICAL), so #5 can never stay unarmed silently.
- #5 reads the marker on each listing refresh (600 s cache). Present ⇒ armed for ever: both dated directories absent or unreadable ⇒ veto, which closes the r4 gap where a 3-day outage would have left no record to show the detector was ever armed. `ENOENT` ⇒ unarmed (ARCH §5.1: enabled only after the first delivered canary). Any other error ⇒ UNKNOWN, vetoing after `WATCH_TICK_STALE_S` (§3.3.2).
- **Receiver absence rule (INFERRED; WP2 verify-first STOP):** Path A configures it receiver-side; Path B adds one host-pinned `BREEZY_ALERT_HEARTBEAT_URL` read only by the canary CLI, a reviewed alert-egress change re-pinning `test_autonomy_alert_egress_not_widened` in the same commit (ARCH §4.6).
- **Suppression drill and its veto arithmetic (r4 recomputed):**
  - `--suppress-drill` is honoured only on dates in the literal `CANARY_SUPPRESSION_DRILL_DATES`, only for the 15:45Z slot, and only if the previous day's 16:45Z canary was delivered (else it journals `drill_skipped precondition` and sends normally). It records `attempt_kind=canary, delivered=false, status_class=not_configured, drill=true` and sends nothing. The 16:30Z and 16:45Z slots run normally.
  - **Arithmetic.** The veto lands at `last delivered canary or CRITICAL + 26 h`. If drill day D's 16:30Z or 16:45Z slot delivers, the clock resets and the drill costs zero trading minutes. If both and every retry also fail (an unrelated broken path), the newest delivered record is D−1 16:45Z and the veto lands at **D 18:45Z**, inside D's trading window `[17:10Z, 01:00Z)`. That veto fires only after 26 h with no delivered canary or CRITICAL, which is the fail-closed outcome the veto exists for; the precondition guarantees the drill never moves it earlier than D 18:45Z.
  - Evidence: the receiver's absence-page timestamp (a text attestation and a C4 `HEALTH` input hash) and no 15:45Z `delivered=true` canary that day.

### 3.8 The six failing units (ARCH §10) and wrapper exits (unchanged from r3)

`systemctl --user list-units --failed` at about 04:15Z (r2, re-verified for the ARCH list):

| Unit | Fact | Root cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| `breezy-portfolio-roi.service` | exit 1, `PORTFOLIO ROI SKIPPED -- no score-live-trials success marker` (`deploy/systemd/portfolio-roi-run.sh:93-98`) | `score-live-trials` SKIPs FQ by design (`deploy/systemd/score-live-trials-run.sh:177-179`) and writes no marker | sibling skip marker `<success-marker>.skipped` (`composition_kind_has_no_scorer`); portfolio-roi prints `PORTFOLIO ROI NO_INPUT -- upstream skipped: <reason>`, exit 0; a missing marker stays exit 1 (WP3) |

L-49 signature, L-53 largest-item rule. | **Redesign, never raise the ceiling (WP3b, §3.8.1)** |
