Wrapper exits that would fail as expected behaviour (RED cases for `test_every_wrapper_skip_path_exits_success`): `deploy/systemd/asos-refresh-run.sh` `SKIPPED-INFRA ... exit 75` (`:26,31,32`) with no `SuccessExitStatus=75`; the `deploy/systemd/replay-daily-run.sh` exit-75 disposition (r2). Each becomes exit 0 with its SKIPPED line, or the unit declares `SuccessExitStatus=75` with a test. `jetbrains-remote-dev.service` and `pressure-*` are foreign (§3.9).

#### 3.8.1 Discovery-pull redesign (G12)

1. **Verify-first (L-49 amendment, L-53):** measure the current job's working set as `MemoryPeak + MemorySwapPeak` of the 10-02 run (4.47 GB, already measured) and the largest single work item (`du` of today's node log). Working set > 1 GB ⇒ redesign branch (taken).
2. **Scope the read to today's spawn:** glob only node logs whose filename stamp is ≥ `_today_window_ns` start minus one launch; seed any older file's cursor at its current size (it is never read). The pull needs only today's initial discovery summary (`find_initial_trigger(since_ns)`).
3. **Stream, do not slurp:** replace `fh.read()` with bounded 1 MiB chunk reads split on newlines, and keep only records whose component ends with `DataClient-POLYMARKET_US` (the only consumer filter, `scripts/analysis/discovery_venue_pull.py:211`). The trigger-file replay `_replay_node_active_slugs` re-reads that file whole through `iter_node_log_records` (`scripts/analysis/discovery_set_equality.py:149-164`, `read_text`); that function is changed to iterate lines from the open file, same signature and return type, so the offline analysis keeps one parser (the module's own rule).
4. **Atomic outputs:** `<day>.json` and `<day>_pages.json` via `mkstemp` + `os.replace`.
5. **Size from the new working set:** re-measure ΔRSS over a post-import baseline in a fresh child (`ru_maxrss`) plus `MemorySwapPeak` of one capped run; set `MemoryHigh` = 1.5× and `MemoryMax` = 2× that working set. If the redesigned working set still exceeds 1 GB, chunk further; never raise the ceiling past 512M.
6. `TimeoutStartSec=900`; timer 16:52Z → **17:12Z** (out of `[16:30Z, 17:10Z)`); not a watchdog member (oneshot; r6 S1, r14): a failure goes to #22 and the next day's 17:12Z run.

### 3.9 Unit health: sources, ordering, scope, ownership, timers, "unexplained"

- **Failure source of truth: the journal.** Each pass reads `journalctl --user -o json --after-cursor=<cursor> MESSAGE_ID=d9b373ed55a64feb8242e02dbe79a49c` (argv list) and takes `USER_UNIT`, `USER_INVOCATION_ID`, `UNIT_RESULT`, `__REALTIME_TIMESTAMP` and `__CURSOR`.
- **Result map (G5):** `UNIT_RESULT_CLASS = {"exit-code": EXIT_CODE, "signal": SIGNAL, "core-dump": SIGNAL, "watchdog": WATCHDOG, "timeout": TIMEOUT (then the §3.5 memory classifier), "oom-kill": OOM, "start-limit-hit": UNHEALABLE, "resources": UNHEALABLE}`; any other value is `UNHEALABLE` plus a WARN naming it. **(r14)** `WATCHDOG` on a `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` member is #21. On any other unit it is handled as `SIGNAL` (#22). Fixtures are built from the four results measured since 09-28 (exit-code from `breezy-portfolio-roi`, signal from `breezy-exit-window-study` 09-28, oom-kill from `breezy-parity-fq` 10-01, timeout from `breezy-discovery-pull` 10-02), with ids replaced.
- **Commit order (G5).** For each entry, in journal order:
  1. **Classification record** `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<unit>__<USER_INVOCATION_ID>__class.json`, created `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` (0444). If it exists, it is reused: dedupe is on `USER_INVOCATION_ID`, never on time.
  2. **Action**, unless `<unit>__<USER_INVOCATION_ID>__action.json` already exists: ALERT through §3.6. **(r14)** For #21 systemd has already restarted the unit, so the action is the page. The health unit never restarts anything.
  3. **Action record** `…__action.json` (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL`) citing the delivery record.
  4. Only after every entry of the batch has both records: rewrite `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/seen/<unit>.json` (one file per unit, single writer under the health lock), then the cursor `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/cursor.json` (`os.replace`), **cursor last**.
  - A crash anywhere before step 4 replays the same entries next pass with no double count and no double alert.
  - A missing cursor (first run or journal rotation) falls back to `--since=<last rollup day start>` and records `cursor_reset=true`.
- **Errors are UNKNOWN.** A non-zero exit, timeout or `Failed to connect to bus` from `systemctl` or `journalctl` makes the pass `UNKNOWN`: `passes_unknown` and `passes_unknown_streak` increment, the cursor is not advanced, and the pass never reports zero failures.
- **Failed-unit reconciliation (r5, F2).** Each pass runs `systemctl --user list-units --failed --all --plain --no-legend` (argv list) **before** its journal read, so any failure it lists happened before the read began. For every in-scope failed unit it reads `InvocationID` (`systemctl --user show -p InvocationID,Result`) and checks for a classification record for `(unit, InvocationID)`, from this pass or any earlier one. If none exists, it runs one targeted, unbounded lookup `journalctl --user -o json MESSAGE_ID=d9b373ed55a64feb8242e02dbe79a49c USER_INVOCATION_ID=<id>`; an entry found there (older than the cursor, for example after a `cursor_reset`) goes through the normal commit order. If the unit-failed entry still cannot be found, or the unit's `InvocationID` is empty, the pass is `pass_result=UNKNOWN` (streak, heartbeat and cursor rules above) with a `journal_blind` finding naming the unit: it feeds #25 and sends a CRITICAL `unit_health_journal_blind` through §3.6, so a journal that silently stopped logging failures can never read as healthy. WP3 verify-first records that `InvocationID` survives on a failed unit on systemd 259 (else the key falls back to `(unit, InactiveEnterTimestamp)`).
- **Pass budget (r5, F6).** Every `systemctl`/`journalctl` call carries a `subprocess` timeout, and the pass holds a monotonic deadline `HEALTH_PASS_BUDGET_S = 90` inside `TimeoutStartSec=115` (r10, E-9; r9 read 120). A pass that reaches the deadline stops, commits nothing past its last completed entry and ends `UNKNOWN`, so it never runs into systemd's timeout and fails as expected behaviour. WP3 verify-first measures the p99 wall time of a full pass on the host (≤ 90 s required).
- **Scope and ownership (G13).**
  - In scope: `^breezy-[a-z0-9@._-]+\.service$`, plus any `run-*.service` whose `Description` or `ExecStart` contains the **repo path prefix `/home/jon/breezy/`** (this covers the shared interpreter `/home/jon/breezy/.venv/`) or the path of any worktree listed by `git -C /home/jon/breezy worktree list --porcelain` (read-only).
  - Every pass lists **all** failed `run-*.service` units, not only regex matches.
  - Foreign units (e.g. `jetbrains-remote-dev`, `pressure-*`, other projects' transients) are recorded as `foreign_failed` and never enter `unexplained_failed_units`; `FOREIGN_UNIT_PREFIXES` is literal.
  - Breezy-owned transients (`Transient=yes`) classify `TRANSIENT_ADHOC`.
- **Timer liveness #28 (G6).**
  - `TIMER_MAX_INTERVAL_S` has one key per `deploy/systemd/*.timer` file; a **template** key (`breezy-family-tally@.timer`) is expanded each pass to its enabled instances from `systemctl --user list-units --all --type=timer --plain 'breezy-family-tally@*'` (today `@pm_us_crh_v2`, `@pm_us_crh_v4`). `TIMER_RETIRED_BY_RULING = {"breezy-live-tally.timer": "RULING_R5_prereg_v1_tally_2026-09-24"}`.
  - `test_timer_interval_table_covers_every_deployed_timer` asserts `set(deploy timers) == keys(TIMER_MAX_INTERVAL_S) ∪ keys(TIMER_RETIRED_BY_RULING)`, disjoint, and that each retired file starts with a `# RETIRED` header naming an existing ruling file under `docs/evidence/`.
  - Each pass reads, for every key (and expanded instance), `UnitFileState`, `ActiveState`, `ActiveEnterTimestamp`, `TimersCalendar`, `TimersMonotonic`, `LastTriggerUSec`, `NextElapseUSecRealtime`, `NextElapseUSecMonotonic`. FAIL if: not `enabled`; not `active`; no future next elapse (the **monotonic** field for a timer with a non-empty `TimersMonotonic`, e.g. `OnUnitActiveSec`; the realtime field otherwise; today every deploy timer is calendar-only); `LastTriggerUSec` empty or 0 once `ActiveEnterTimestamp + interval + 900 s` has passed (grace); `LastTriggerUSec` older than `interval + 900 s`; a template with zero enabled instances; a retired timer that is loaded and enabled. **(r14)** r7's `TIMER_GATED_BY_SELF_HEAL_MODE` is deleted along with the probe timer.
  - **Assumed accuracy (r8, Y7).** Each `TIMER_MAX_INTERVAL_S` value is a pair `(interval_s, assumed_accuracy_s)` stating the `AccuracySec` its grace assumes. The value is `60` (1 min) for every non-autonomy timer, which declare `AccuracySec=1min` (§0); `30` for `breezy-quote-tape-ingest-frequent.timer`; and `1` for every `breezy-autonomy-*.timer` (R-e). The `interval + 900 s` grace absorbs that lateness. `test_timer_interval_table_states_assumed_accuracy_matching_unit_files` parses each timer's `AccuracySec` (the manager default `1min` when absent) and fails on any mismatch. A timer later loosened beyond what the table states therefore fails the test instead of silently consuming the grace.
- **Classes:** `TIMEOUT`, `MEMORY_CEILING_SUSPECT`, `OOM`, `NOFILE_EXHAUSTED`, `EXIT_CODE`, `SIGNAL`, `UNHEALABLE`, `TRANSIENT_ADHOC`, `EXPECTED_FAILURE_SUSPECT`, and **`WATCHDOG`** (r14). `WATCHDOG` is a member's `UNIT_RESULT=watchdog`, and it is the only class #21 reports (§3.5). r4–r13's `HUNG` class, its drill-probe exception (O-3) and `SELF_HEAL_STOPPED` are deleted with the executor. r4's `STALLED_ACTIVATING` stays withdrawn (r6, S1).
- **Watchdog arming, checked every pass (r14; replaces r6's member-liveness slot rule).**
  - **What is read.** Each pass reads `Type`, `NotifyAccess`, `WatchdogUSec`, `Restart`, `ActiveState`, `NRestarts` and `InvocationID` for every `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` member.
  - **Finding.** The live unit raises the #22 finding `watchdog_disarmed`, CRITICAL, keyed `(unit, InvocationID)`, if any of these holds: `Type≠notify`, `NotifyAccess≠all`, `WatchdogUSec=0`, or `Restart ∉ WATCHDOG_RESTART_SET`.
  - **Why every pass.** A drop-in or a hand edit that disarmed the watchdog would otherwise remove SELF_HEAL silently, and #24 sees file drift only once a day.
  - **Unreadable.** An unreadable property makes the pass UNKNOWN.
- **Auto-restarted daemons (r5, F7).** A `Restart=always` daemon that crashes is restarted by systemd and may never show as failed. Each pass reads `NRestarts`, `Restart` and `InvocationID` of every in-scope unit whose effective `Restart=always` (today `breezy-quote-tape`, `breezy-nws-ingest`, `breezy-trade-supervisor`) and stores the last value in `seen/<unit>.json`. Growth since the previous pass is a #22 finding `daemon_auto_restarted` with `metrics.nrestarts_delta`, CRITICAL for data-path units, keyed `(unit, InvocationID)` through the commit order and explained by its delivered alert. A drop in `NRestarts` (the counter resets on an explicit start, such as the 09:00Z rotate's `try-restart`) re-baselines with no finding. An unreadable `NRestarts` makes the pass UNKNOWN. **(r14)** Growth in which every ended invocation is a member's `UNIT_RESULT=watchdog` ending is #21, not `daemon_auto_restarted`, so one restart pages once.
- **Unexplained `InvocationID` change (r6, S4).** `NRestarts` resets on an explicit start, so a crash followed by a reset (for example a crash, then the 09:00Z rotate's `try-restart`) can hide the growth. Each pass therefore also compares the stored `InvocationID` of every `Restart=always` daemon with the current one. A change is explained only by (i) **(r14)** a member's watchdog ending (Y1 exception below), or (ii) a rotate run: for `breezy-quote-tape` only, a `breezy-quote-tape-rotate.service` invocation with `ExecMainStartTimestamp` inside (previous pass, this pass], **later than the stored invocation's `ActiveEnterTimestamp`** (kept in `seen/<unit>.json` beside the stored id; r7 LOW-1), and `Result=success` (read with `systemctl --user show`; no new marker file). A rotate that started before the stored invocation began explains nothing. Any other change is a #22 finding `daemon_invocation_changed_unexplained`, with the old and new ids in the classification record (not in the payload), severity as `daemon_auto_restarted`, keyed `(unit, new InvocationID)` and explained by its delivered alert. An unreadable `InvocationID` makes the pass UNKNOWN.
- **How each replaced invocation ended (r8, Y1).** Before (i), (ii) or a build-side marker may explain a change, the pass reads how **every invocation that ended in (previous pass, this pass]** ended. That is the stored invocation plus any intermediate one: an id seen in a `MESSAGE_ID=39f53479d3a045ac8e11786248231fbf` `Started` entry in the interval that is not the current id. **(r9, LOW-r9-1)** The set is every `USER_INVOCATION_ID` of the unit, other than the current id, that appears in the interval in any of three entries: `Started` (`39f53479…`), process-exit (`98e32220…`) or `Stopping` (`de5b426a…`). An invocation whose `Started` entry fell before the previous pass, but which ended inside the interval, is caught by its exit or `Stopping` entry. `systemctl --user show -p Result,ExecMainStatus,ExecMainCode` describes only the current invocation (§0, measured), so the source is the journal: `journalctl --user -o json USER_UNIT=<unit> USER_INVOCATION_ID=<id>` (argv list, inside the pass budget).
  - **Stopped by systemd** means the invocation's entries hold a `MESSAGE_ID=de5b426a63be47a7b6ac3eaac82e2f6f` `Stopping` entry with `JOB_TYPE=stop`, and none of the following:
    - a `MESSAGE_ID=5eb03494b6584870a536b337290809b3` `Scheduled restart job` entry;
    - a unit-failed entry `d9b373ed55a64feb8242e02dbe79a49c`;
    - a `MESSAGE_ID=98e322203f7a4ed290d09fe03c09fe15` process-exit entry dated before the `Stopping` entry;
    - a process-exit entry after it with anything other than `EXIT_CODE=killed EXIT_STATUS=15` or `EXIT_CODE=exited EXIT_STATUS ∈ {0, 143}` (SIGTERM from systemd, default or handled).
  - **Any other ending is `daemon_crashed`.** A self-exit, including a clean exit 0 that `Restart=always` silently restarts and that logs no unit-failed entry, a crash, a signal not sent by systemd, or an auto-restart is a #22 finding `daemon_crashed`. It carries `metrics.exit_code` and `metrics.exit_status` from the journal, takes the severity of `daemon_auto_restarted`, and is keyed `(unit, ended InvocationID)` through the commit order. **It is reported regardless of any rotate run or build-side marker.** **(r14) One exception:** an ended invocation of a `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` member whose unit-failed entry carries `UNIT_RESULT=watchdog` is `watchdog_restarted`. Its journal also shows the measured `EXIT_CODE=dumped EXIT_STATUS=6` exit and a `Scheduled restart job` entry (§0.1 (24)). #21 reports it, and it is never `daemon_crashed`. A member's crash with any other `UNIT_RESULT` stays `daemon_crashed`.
  - **One explanation per ended invocation (r9, LOW-r9-1).** Each ended invocation must be matched to its own explanation. A rotate run or a marker explains at most one ended invocation: the one whose `Stopping` entry it most closely precedes. An ended invocation left without its own explanation is `daemon_invocation_changed_unexplained`.
  - **When explanations apply.** (i), (ii) and the marker apply only when every invocation that ended in the interval was stopped by systemd. Checking intermediate invocations covers both orders: crash, auto-restart, then rotate; and rotate, then crash.
  - **Fail closed.** A journal read error makes the pass UNKNOWN. An ended invocation with no journal entries at all (vacuumed or never logged) cannot be proven, so the change stays `daemon_invocation_changed_unexplained`.
  - **Measured (§0).** Each of the last 7 days' recorder rotations shows `Stopping` and `Stopped` with no exit entry. The 09-29 crash, 12 s after that day's rotate had started a new invocation, shows the exit, unit-failed and scheduled-restart entries and no `Stopping` entry. This is exactly the pattern the rule separates; r7's (ii) would have explained the stored invocation's rotate stop and left the crash to `NRestarts` alone.
- **Build-side restarts (r7, LOW-2).** A deliberate build-side restart (for example the supervisor restart after a merge) is announced by a named, write-once marker `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/buildside_restart/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<ts_ns>_<unit>.json` (0444, schema `buildside_restart/v1`, `{ts_ns, unit, reason, commit}`), written by `breezy-autonomy-health --mark-buildside-restart <unit> --reason <text> --commit <sha>` immediately before the restart command. No autonomy code path writes it; one-writer row: creator = that CLI subcommand, run by the build-side implementer; never rewritten or deleted. An `InvocationID` change whose new invocation's `ActiveEnterTimestamp` falls in [marker `ts_ns`, marker `ts_ns` + 300 s] and after the stored invocation's `ActiveEnterTimestamp` is the finding `daemon_restarted_buildside`: **WARN** for every unit, delivered with proof through §3.6 and explained by that delivered alert. A marker never silences the page and never downgrades a finding already raised; a marker that matches no change is ignored; an unreadable marker directory counts as no marker, so the CRITICAL stands (fail closed). Without a marker the change stays CRITICAL for data-path units, as in r6. **(r8, Y1)** A marker explains only invocations stopped by systemd; a crash or self-exit inside the marker window is `daemon_crashed`, never WARN.
  - **The marker is a checklist step, not a memory (r8, Y5).** The marker command `breezy-autonomy-health --mark-buildside-restart <unit> --reason <text> --commit <sha>` goes immediately before the `systemctl --user restart` line in two places:
    - the WP activation rule (§4);
    - every supervisor, recorder or NWS-ingest restart step of `docs/plans/R8_OPERATOR_RUNBOOK.md` (today lines 318, 421, 477 and 510).
    WP3 makes that runbook edit, and `test_runbook_daemon_restart_steps_mark_buildside_first` pins it. A forgotten marker fails toward the CRITICAL, never toward silence.
- **Self-heal mode (r8–r11): deleted in r14 (ruling item 3).** With no mode, there is nothing to persist. Removed: `seen/_self_heal_mode.json`; the `self_heal_mode_degraded`, `self_heal_mode_state_lost` and `self_heal_mode_state_corrupt` findings; the `degraded` flag; the pre-AUT-5b re-baseline; and the #28 `gated_mode_degraded` interaction. A disarmed watchdog is now `watchdog_disarmed` (above).
- **`MemAvailable` samples (r5, F10).** Each completed pass appends one line `{ts_ns, mem_available_kib, mem_available_free_kib}` to `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/memavail_<YYYY-MM-DD>.jsonl` (single writer, under the health lock, `fsync`), where `mem_available_free` is the §3.10.1 item 6 add-back computed at that instant. The daily #31 uses the minimum over the trailing 24 h; fewer than 72 samples in 24 h ⇒ `INCONCLUSIVE metrics.unknown_reason=memavail_samples_insufficient`, never PASS.
- **Daily rollup** `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/day_<YYYY-MM-DD>.json`, by the first pass after 00:00Z: `unexplained_failed_units` (count and names), `foreign_failed`, `passes_completed`, `passes_unknown`, `max_passes_unknown_streak`, `produced_at_ns`, `cursor_reset`.
- **Explained** means each failed `(unit, USER_INVOCATION_ID)` that day has a classification record and an action record with proof (an ALERT `delivered=true`; for #21, the delivered page for a restart that systemd already made, r14). `TRANSIENT_ADHOC` is explained by its classification plus one delivered WARN. `EXPECTED_FAILURE_SUSPECT` (same `(Result, ExecMainStatus)` on two distinct invocations on consecutive days) is never explained.
- **Failed invocations (r4; r6, S1; r14).** A failed or timed-out oneshot invocation is explained by its classification record plus a delivered #22 alert; its recovery is the next timer elapse. A member's watchdog ending is explained by its classification record plus the delivered #21 page; systemd made the restart.

### 3.10 Slot table and runtime bounds (r4: ARCH §5.2, G32, programme rules)

Rules:
- **R-a (G32):** every `Type=oneshot` unit, autonomy or existing, declares `TimeoutStartSec` and never `RuntimeMaxSec` (a no-op on oneshot under host systemd 259). Measured at `4b8347a6`: 19 of the 22 `deploy/systemd/*.service` files are oneshots, all with `TimeoutStartSec`, none with `RuntimeMaxSec`. Pinned by ARCH's `test_oneshot_units_use_timeout_start_sec_not_runtime_max_sec`, which AUT-6 implements over every `deploy/systemd/*.service` with a positive control (a fixture oneshot setting `RuntimeMaxSec` fails). **(r7, LOW-3)** The test derives the oneshot set by parsing the `Type=` line of every unit file and never hard-codes a count (neither 19 nor 20); adding a oneshot file grows the checked set without editing the test.
- **R-b:** a non-daemon `simple`/`exec` unit declares `RuntimeMaxSec`; `Type=notify` units add `WatchdogSec` (AUT-3's). R-c: the daemon allowlist (node, supervisor, recorder) is exempt from R-b. **(r14)** A watchdog daemon (`WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS`) is `Type=notify` with `WatchdogSec`. Under R-c it never declares `RuntimeMaxSec`, which would stop a healthy recorder (§ERRATA-REQUEST ER-4).
- **R-d (P6-3):** outside ARCH's §5.2 launch-window table, no unit's `[start, start + flock wait + TimeoutStartSec]` meets `[16:30Z, 17:10Z)` for any firing or retry, `start` being the latest elapse (slot + the timer's `AccuracySec`) (ARCH's `test_no_unit_overlaps_launch_window`; AUT-6 contributes its rows and the existing studies). r3's blanket "data-path units exempt" clause is withdrawn: only the table's launch-path rows run inside (observation O-1, §11).
- **R-e (r7, U5): slot-critical timers fire on their slot.** systemd's default timer accuracy is 1 min (§0, measured), so an unpinned timer may elapse up to 60 s late: an intraday producer firing at 16:30:60 with its 120 s bound would end at 16:33:00, after its engine pass at 16:32:30. Every slot-critical autonomy timer therefore declares **`AccuracySec=1s`** and no `RandomizedDelaySec` (or 0): AUT-6's `breezy-autonomy-producer-intraday`, `breezy-autonomy-health`, `breezy-autonomy-alert-redeliver`, `breezy-autonomy-canary`, and `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily` timers (r14: the probe timer is deleted), and every other area's `breezy-autonomy-*.timer` (AUT-5's engine and dead-man rows, §5). Pinned by `tests/unit/test_systemd_unit_contracts.py::test_slot_critical_autonomy_timers_pin_accuracy_sec_1s` (WP1), which globs every `deploy/systemd/breezy-autonomy-*.timer`, requires `AccuracySec=1s` and `RandomizedDelaySec` absent or 0, and fails on a fixture timer left at the default (positive control). Existing non-autonomy timers keep `1min`; R-d is computed for them with that lateness.
- **R-d per command (r9, AA1).** `TimeoutStartSec` re-arms for each start command (measured, §0). A unit with several start-phase commands is therefore bounded by the sum, over its `ExecStartPre=`, `ExecStart=` and `ExecStartPost=` lines, of each command's bound. That bound is `T + K` for a line wrapped in `timeout -k K T`, else `TimeoutStartSec`. ARCH's `test_no_unit_overlaps_launch_window` computes that sum. AUT-6 contributes the rule and `test_runtime_bound_sums_per_command_timeouts`, whose fixture of two unwrapped 120 s lines must count 240 s. Today only the intraday producer has two start commands (none of the 22 deploy units has more than one, §0), so no other row moves.
- **R-d with the stop term (r10, E-9).** Erratum E-9 adds `TimeoutStopSec` to that sum: after a start timeout, systemd waits up to `TimeoutStopSec` before it kills.
  - **Declared value.** Every AUT-6 oneshot declares `TimeoutStopSec=5` explicitly, never the 90 s manager default, and every worst-case end below includes it.
  - **Single-command units.** To keep every row inside its ARCH §5.2 "Ends by" column, these units lower `TimeoutStartSec` by the same 5 s: health 120 → 115 s, canary and redeliver 60 → 55 s. Their in-process budgets still fit: the 90 s health pass; the redeliver's 40 s claim stop plus one 5 s attempt; the canary's 5 s attempts.
  - **Tests.** `test_runtime_bound_sums_per_command_timeouts` gains the term (its fixture now counts 240 + 5 = 245 s), and `test_aut6_oneshot_worst_end_includes_timeout_stop_sec` checks each AUT-6 row.
- **E-10 readings (r11).**
  - **(a) A wrapper bound below `TimeoutStartSec`.** E-10 (a) reads E-6's bootstrap unit, with the literal `TimeoutStartSec=60` and a 46 s `timeout -k` wrapper, as bounded by the wrapper. That is R-d's rule already: a wrapped line counts `T + K`, and an unwrapped line counts `TimeoutStartSec`. `test_runtime_bound_sums_per_command_timeouts` gains that fixture: one line wrapped to 46 s under `TimeoutStartSec=60` counts 46 + 5 s, never 60 + 5 s.
  - **(b) The 16:47:30 engine pass.** E-10 (b) moves the 16:47:30 intraday engine pass's lock-timeout end from 16:47:50 to 16:48:01, with a worst case of ≤ 16:49:50. No AUT-6 row moves:
    - the 16:45 producer run ends ≤ 16:47:06 (slot + 126 s), before that pass starts and therefore before it reads the verdicts it writes demand files for;
    - the 16:50 producer slot starts after 16:49:50;
    - the 16:41 health pass ends ≤ 16:43:01, and the next starts at 16:51:00;
    - the canary's 16:45 firing ends ≤ 16:46:01;
    - the 16:48 redeliver run ends ≤ 16:49:01 and takes no engine lock.

    The Z12 SLO (§3.4.5) and the §3.4.1 ATTEST arithmetic are unchanged.
- **Wrapper setup (r11, E-7a rule 5).** The wrapper adds about 10–50 ms inside each command it prefixes, and each command's bound (`timeout -k` or `TimeoutStartSec`) already contains it. The sums below are therefore re-checked, not re-derived, and every row's end is unchanged.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily` | oneshot | 05:30, 13:00 | studies, CLI-acquired, 300 s deadline | 1500 s, `TimeoutStopSec=5` (r10, E-9) | 3G | none | lock timeout ⇒ SKIPPED, exit 0; `OnFailure=breezy-study-failed@%n`; `LimitNOFILE=524288`; ends ≤ 06:00Z and ≤ 13:30Z |

**Slot arithmetic with `AccuracySec=1s` (r7, U5).** Latest start = slot + 1 s; the own `-n` locks wait 0 s.
- Intraday producer **(r9, AA1: derived from the worst case)**:
  - **Sum.** The latest start is slot + 1 s. The evaluate stage runs ≤ 100 + 2 s (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL), the inter-stage spawn ≤ 1 s (measured 0.02 s, §0), and the demand stage ≤ 15 + 2 s.
  - **End.** It ends ≤ slot + 1 + 102 + 1 + 17 = **slot + 121 s**, before its engine pass at slot + 150 s (ARCH §5.2 row: "start + 130 s", "its engine pass (+150 s)"); margin 29 s.
  - **E-9 term (r10).** Adding `TimeoutStopSec=5` gives slot + 126 s. That is start + 125 s against ARCH's start + 130 s, with a 24 s margin to the engine pass. The term is reached only if systemd's 120 s per-command backstop fires, which the wrappers pre-empt. It is counted because E-9 requires it.
  - **Correction to r8.** r8 also stated slot + 121 s, but derived it from a whole-unit `TimeoutStartSec` bound that does not exist. Without the wrappers, the worst case was slot + 241 s.
- Health: the 16:31, 16:41, 16:51 and 17:01 passes end ≤ start + 121 s (slot + 1 + 115 + `TimeoutStopSec` 5; r10, E-9) (16:33:01 … 17:03:01Z); the 17:21Z pass that judges a deferred canary slot (§3.7) starts at 17:21:00–17:21:01Z.
- Canary: the 16:30 and 16:45 firings end ≤ 16:31:01 and 16:46:01Z (1 + 55 + 5; r10, E-9); the 17:10 firing ends ≤ 17:11:01Z, which is why #23 waits for its record or the 17:21Z pass (r6, S2, unchanged).
- Redeliver: each `*:03/5` run ends ≤ slot + 61 s (1 + 55 + 5; r10, E-9).
- Daily producer: the 300 s lock deadline sits inside `TimeoutStartSec=1500`, so the runs end ≤ 05:55:06Z and ≤ 13:25:06Z (r10, E-9: plus `TimeoutStopSec=5`), inside "ends ≤ 06:00Z and ≤ 13:30Z".
- The Z12 SLO (§3.4.5) stays ≤ 11 min.

Own-lock AUT-6 units: 512M + 256M + 128M + 128M = 1024 MiB (r14: the probe's 64M is gone), inside ARCH's 4G for all own-lock autonomy units; #31 checks the effective sum daily.

#### 3.10.1 The memory check (#31, r4: ARCH §5.2 V14, U6)

**Studies-flock holders (r5, F11)** are identified by a static scan, not a text grep: for each `deploy/systemd/*.service`, the `ExecStart=` argv and every `deploy/systemd/*.sh` it names are parsed with comments stripped, and a unit is a holder iff a non-comment command takes `breezy-studies.lock` (`flock`, `fcntl`). In-process takers (today `breezy-autonomy-producer-daily`, §3.4.4) are listed in the literal `IN_PROCESS_STUDIES_LOCK_UNITS`, kept honest by an AST test that every `src/` module opening `breezy-studies.lock` maps to a listed unit. **`MemoryMax=infinity`** (or an absent limit, which `systemctl show` reports as `infinity`) is parsed as +∞, never skipped: it FAILs condition 1 for a holder, condition 4 for an own-lock unit and condition 6 for the sum.

Each run reads the **effective** `MemoryMax`, `MemoryHigh` and `MemoryPeak` (`systemctl --user show`, drop-ins included) of every `deploy/systemd/` unit and `MemTotal`, `MemAvailable` from `/proc/meminfo`. FAIL, each condition named in `metrics.violations`:
1. a studies-flock holder with `MemoryMax` > 14G or `MemoryHigh` > 12G, unless it carries a named raised cap;
2. a named raised cap > 16G (`tests/unit/test_analysis_units_memory_capped.py:138`), or one whose timer slot falls inside `[16:30Z, 01:15Z)` or shares its slot with another heavy (> 4G) unit;
3. a scheduled study whose last `MemoryPeak` reached its `MemoryHigh` (ARCH: no silent OOM-kill; it is FAILed by name);
4. own-lock autonomy units (every area's) with a summed `MemoryMax` > 4G;
5. quote-tape-ingest, named while its temporary drop-in stands (ARCH counts its effective limit; today 12G/14G per §5.2, 14G found 10-03);
6. **the memory sum** `max(studies-holder MemoryMax) + Σ own-lock MemoryMax + ingest MemoryMax + measured node RSS + measured recorder RSS` > `MemAvailable_free`, where `MemAvailable_free = MemAvailable + Σ MemoryCurrent of those same units + node RSS + recorder RSS` (memory already resident in the counted processes is added back, so it is not counted twice). This implements ARCH's "sum of effective limits plus measured node and recorder RSS against `MemAvailable`" (`test_health_memory_sum_within_memavailable`); the add-back is AUT-6's reading of an ARCH sentence that does not say whether the node is running when `MemAvailable` is read (observation O-2, §11). **The `MemAvailable` side is the 24 h minimum of the health pass samples (§3.9, r5 F10)**, so a single daily read cannot miss the night's tightest point.
7. a heavy (> 4G) unit with a timer firing in `[01:00Z, 04:30Z)`.

### 3.11 Monitor the monitors and the dead-man (r4: ARCH §4.6, P6-5, P6-13)

The dead-man is AUT-5's (`breezy-autonomy-deadman`, every 30 min). It raises CRITICAL past `ENGINE_HEARTBEAT_STALE_S`, past H, and **on a health heartbeat older than 1800 s** (ARCH's only health condition). AUT-6 builds no second dead-man and supplies:

1. **The health heartbeat** `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/unit_health/heartbeat.json` (0600, `os.replace`, single writer under the health lock), rewritten by every pass: `{schema: "health_heartbeat/v1", ts_ns, last_attempt_ns, invocation_id, pass_result, passes_unknown_streak}`.
   - **`ts_ns` is the end of the last completed pass** (`pass_result ∈ {OK, FINDINGS}`). An UNKNOWN pass rewrites the file with `ts_ns` unchanged, `last_attempt_ns` = now and the streak incremented. So ARCH's age-only dead-man rule also fires on a health unit that runs but cannot read systemd, after about 1800 s (three UNKNOWN passes), with no change to ARCH (r3's request C-13 that AUT-5 add a streak condition is withdrawn). The health-pass UNKNOWN causes are: a `systemctl`/`journalctl` error, a `journal_blind` finding (F2), the 90 s pass deadline (F6) and an unreadable fold (F4) (r5).
   - AUT-6 supplies the fixtures for AUT-5's dead-man test: fresh, aged, and "fresh `last_attempt_ns`, aged `ts_ns`, streak 3".
2. **#29 `aut6.health_monitor_stale`** in the intraday producer: `ts_ns` age > 1800 s or `passes_unknown_streak ≥ 3`, as a C4 verdict and a proof-bearing CRITICAL. A blind health unit therefore fails two independent watchers within about 30 min.
3. **#26 `aut6.producer_stale`** in the health unit: intraday heartbeat age, `fold_ok=false`, and in-window absence of a valid intraday verdict.
4. **#27 daily-verdict absence**, with skip reasons.
5. **Unit-health coverage** of `breezy-autonomy-deadman.service` and every AUT-6 unit (#22, #28).
6. **The CRITICAL path and the drain library** for the dead-man: `deliver_with_proof` with writer `deadman`, and `drain_outbox(drainer="deadman", min_age_s=ALERT_OUTBOX_STALE_S)` (§3.6.3). Each watcher (health, intraday producer, dead-man) is watched by at least one other; the off-host canary covers whole-host death.

### 3.12 Contract use (C1–C6)

- **C1:** consumes `DetectorEvent` (including `fee_schedule`), `DecisionRecord` (`TrySubmit`, `ask_px`, `depth_ref`, `eval_ns`, `p_hat`), `OrderLink` (`client_order_id`, `time_in_force`, `side`, `instrument_id`), `LifecycleEvent` (`FILLED`, `px`, `ts_ns`), the Depth10 payload store, `source`, `drill`. Canary records are excluded everywhere. Provides the #4 and #5 observation objects.
- **C2:** consumes `admissible`, `excluded_reason`, `slippage`, `fill_px`, `entry_ask`, `p_at_decision`, `settled_outcome`, `drill` (#13, #14).
- **C3:** the champion artefact for #12.
- **C4:** produces `DRIFT`/`HEALTH` for every VERDICT id from three pinned producers (`aut6.intraday`, `aut6.daily`, `aut6.health`), including `DRILL_INJECT`, `DRILL_INJECT_HALT` and `fee_schedule`, with C4 identity, validity and the closed `assumptions` enum (§3.4.1).
- **C4.1:** #9 and #12 read forward days only (≥ 2026-10-02); no AUT-6 detector reads an outcome row inside the frozen holdout `[2026-07-01, 2026-10-02)`.
- **C5:** consumes the fold, the bound sha, the drill marker and halt-relevant state. **Writes** `integrity_floor` `demand/v1` files through AUT-5's writer as a pinned producer (§3.3.3); nothing else in C5.
- **C6:** provides `DriftDetectors` for `forecast_quantile_ladder` and the required-class gate test.

### 3.13 Halted-by-design is not a failure (unchanged from r3)

- Before evaluating a `halt_exempt=True` detector (#6, #7, #14, #18, #20) the producer reads the C5 fold and the champion's exec-store family halt (`read_family_halt_rows_readonly` + `decode_family_halt_state`, moved by r12 AF1 to `src/breezy/domain/family_halt.py` (§3.1.2; today `trial_day_latch.py:333,354`), `mode=ro` on the E-8a snapshot copy, advisory).
- No CHAMPION on the venue ⇒ `PASS reason=family_not_champion`. Champion HALTED in the fold or exec-store halted ⇒ `PASS reason=family_halted`, `metrics.halt_detail` from the closed enum. Unreadable ⇒ UNKNOWN, never PASS.
- **#30 is deliberately not exempt (G7):** a halt that nobody clears for 24 h is itself the finding (a RECOVERABLE halt with no RESUME, or an INTEGRITY freeze awaiting build-side incident handling). `halted_since_ns` is the `ts_ns` of the fold row that made the family HALTED; for an exec-store halt not yet mirrored, the halt row's own timestamp if `decode_family_halt_state` exposes one (WP6 verify-first), else `metrics.halted_first_observed_ns` carried from the previous #30 verdict.
- Safe because a halted family cannot send (`family_halt_submit_veto`), a dead node while halted is still caught by #22, B1 and AUT-1's capture detectors, and the halt was alerted by its writer.
- Clears on the first verified fold naming the family CHAMPION with no exec-store halt (W5).
- `ROLLBACK_FAILED` halts (ROLLBACK-FAILURE decision) are HALTED in the fold, so the exempt detectors PASS `family_halted` and #30 measures them like any other halt.

---

## 4. Work packages

**Gate commands, every WP, in the WP's own worktree:**
1. `PYTHONPATH=<tree>/src scripts/ci/run_tests_no_egress.sh` with the exact interpreter `/home/jon/breezy/.venv/bin/python`; never `uv` or `pip` (L-51). Unit-launched gates take `-p LimitNOFILE=524288`, basetemp on `~/.cache`.
2. `cd <tree> && lint-imports`, demanding "N kept, 0 broken".
3. The mypy ratchet (`tests/unit/test_mypy_ratchet.py`).
4. Read the gate EXIT before any push; the full gate after **every** merge (L-43).

Every injectable seam gets one production-default test (L-55). Every operator-visible line is asserted, then checked in the journal after the first live run (L-52). **Activation:** immediately on merge unless stated. A unit-file change means `daemon-reload` plus `enable --now <timer>` by the implementer. **(r11, E-7a)** Before the first enable, the unit's bind sources, `.bwrap_probe/` subdirectories and lock file must exist (`install -d -m 0700`, one component per argument; the lock file created empty, mode 0600), and AUT-5's wrapper and table must have merged; node code takes effect at the next 16:50Z LAUNCH. **(r8, Y5)** Any activation that restarts a `Restart=always` daemon (`breezy-trade-supervisor`, `breezy-quote-tape`, `breezy-nws-ingest`) first runs `breezy-autonomy-health --mark-buildside-restart <unit> --reason <WP id> --commit <sha>`, once WP3 has landed. New or renamed tests in r4 are marked **(r4)**, and in r5 **(r5)**; tests ARCH names keep ARCH's names.

A clean intraday invocation still `activating` at the pass does not end the episode; the same invocation judged ended at the next pass does. ### AUT-6.WP4 — Native self-heal: the SELF_HEAL contract on the systemd watchdog (r14; replaces the executor and the drill probe). Wave 1, after WP3, the `breezy-autonomy-failed@` notifier unit and AUT-1 r9's recorder watchdog work package

- **Files:**
  - `src/breezy/persistence/autonomy/detector_catalog.py`: `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS`, `WATCHDOG_RESTART_SET` and the `aut6.unit_health` row.
  - `src/breezy/runtime/study_failure_notifier.py`: the unit-state read and the two watchdog branches.
  - `src/breezy/runtime/unit_health.py`: #21 with its fallback page, `watchdog_disarmed` and the Y1 watchdog exception.
  - `deploy/systemd/breezy-autonomy-failed@.service`: WP7's file, which WP4 lands if it merges first.
- **What WP4 does not touch.** It edits neither `deploy/systemd/breezy-quote-tape.service` nor any recorder module; those are AUT-1 r9's. AUT-1's edits must pass AUT-6's config and contract tests, and AUT-6's reviewer reviews them.
- **Verify-first STOP.** Results go in `docs/evidence/aut6/WP4_verify_first_<date>.md`.
  - **V-W1, the mechanism.** It was done at plan time (§0.1 (24)–(29)) and is repeated on the WP4 tree.
    - **Setup.** A scratch transient unit runs AUT-1's `classify_recorder_sample` and `sd_notify`, fed a fake sample that freezes after a few seconds. Its name is `systemd-run --user --unit=aut6test-wd-<ts>`, never a `breezy-*` name, and it is stopped, `reset-failed` and removed afterwards. It uses the directive set parsed from the recorder unit file, with `WatchdogSec` scaled to 3 s and a scratch start limit.
    - **It must show:** `Result=watchdog`; a new `InvocationID`; `NRestarts` growth; the `OnFailure=` target reading `SubState=auto-restart`; and, at the scratch limit, `ActiveState=failed`.
    - **Negative control.** A sample that never freezes runs for at least 30 s with `NRestarts=0`.
    - **Repeats.** The run is repeated behind `timeout` and the shared bwrap wrapper with `NotifyAccess=all`. It is repeated again with `NotifyAccess=main` as the negative control, which must time out.
    - Any other outcome stops WP4, and the design returns to review.
  - **V-W2, the counter gap.** Measure the longest gap between counter advances over 7 days, split into "subscribed ≥ 1" and "listing empty". A subscribed gap above W/2 is a STOP and a finding for AUT-1.
  - **V-W3, start to READY.** Measure the recorder's p99 time from the start job to the first frame over its last 14 starts. `TimeoutStartSec` must be at least max(90 s, 2 × p99).
  - **V-W4, the rotate.** `breezy-quote-tape-rotate.service` runs a blocking `systemctl --user try-restart`. Under `Type=notify` that job completes only at `READY=1`. The rotate's bound must exceed the recorder's `TimeoutStopSec` (120 s) plus its `TimeoutStartSec`; if not, this is a finding for AUT-1, which owns that file.
- **RED tests first:**
  - **Unit-file config, bwrap included:** `tests/unit/test_systemd_unit_contracts.py::test_watchdog_unit_config_declares_notify_watchdog_restart_startlimit_and_notifyaccess_all`.
    - **What it asserts.** For every `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS` member it parses the deploy file and asserts the §3.5 directive rules:
      - `Type=notify` and `NotifyAccess=all`;
      - a `WatchdogSec` within [120 s, 600 s];
      - `Restart ∈ WATCHDOG_RESTART_SET`;
      - `WatchdogSignal=SIGTERM`, or `SIGABRT` with `LimitCORE=0`;
      - no `RuntimeMaxSec`;
      - either the O-8 recorded `StartLimitIntervalSec=0`, or a finite limit with `StartLimitBurst ≤ 3` and `OnFailure=breezy-autonomy-failed@%n.service`.
    - **ExecStart shapes.** It is parametrised over `plain`, `timeout-prefixed` and `bwrap-wrapped`, and `NotifyAccess=all` is mandatory for all three.
    - **Positive controls.** Each of these fixtures fails: `NotifyAccess=main` behind bwrap, `Type=simple`, no `WatchdogSec`, `Restart=no`, `Restart=on-success`, and `SIGABRT` with no `LimitCORE=0`.
    - **Companion tests.** `::test_every_unit_with_watchdogsec_is_a_listed_member`; `::test_watchdog_units_are_literal_and_exclude_trade_supervisor_engine` (carries ARCH's `test_self_heal_unit_allowlist_is_literal_and_excludes_trade` forward under the name in §ERRATA-REQUEST); `::test_watchdog_member_execstart_reaches_a_notify_sender` (AST, R-40: a member's ExecStart entry module must reach `recorder_watchdog.sd_notify` before its unit may declare `Type=notify`).
  - **Ping withheld on a stall:** `tests/unit/test_aut6_watchdog_contract.py::test_ping_withheld_when_counters_stall`. It is AUT-6's contract test on AUT-1's pure `classify_recorder_sample`:
    - frames and bytes unchanged with at least one instrument subscribed ⇒ no ping;
    - positive control: frames advanced ⇒ ping.
    - **Same file:** `::test_empty_listing_pings_only_while_discovery_reload_fresh`; `::test_launch_window_defers_by_pinging_until_1710_then_withholds` (X-3); `::test_ready_sent_once_on_first_allowed_ping`; `::test_sd_notify_without_notify_socket_is_noop_false`.
  - **systemd restarts a silent notifier, on real systemd:** `tests/integration/test_aut6_watchdog_scratch_unit.py::test_systemd_restarts_silent_notifier_on_watchdog` is V-W1 written as a test.
    - **Skip and safety.** It skips with `AUT6_EXPECTED_SKIP:no_user_systemd` only when `systemd-run --user` is unavailable, and it never touches a `breezy-*` unit.
    - **Companions.** `::test_notifyaccess_main_behind_bwrap_times_out` and `::test_onfailure_target_sees_auto_restart_then_failed`.
  - **Notifier:**
    - `tests/unit/test_study_failure_notifier.py::test_watchdog_failure_with_restart_pending_sends_nothing`;
    - `::test_watchdog_start_limit_exhausted_pages_critical_via_markers`;
    - `::test_unreadable_unit_state_pages`.
  - **Health pass:**
    - `tests/unit/test_unit_health.py::test_member_watchdog_ending_is_21_not_daemon_crashed`. It uses the §0.1 (24) journal pattern with the ids replaced. Positive control: the same member ending `exit-code` stays `daemon_crashed`.
    - `::test_watchdog_restart_paged_once_via_stop_hook_record_else_fallback_critical`.
    - `::test_watchdog_restart_counted_once_not_also_daemon_auto_restarted`.
    - `::test_watchdog_disarmed_on_live_type_notifyaccess_watchdogusec_or_restart`, parametrised over each property.
    - `::test_watchdog_memory_ceiling_suspect_is_critical_at_once`.
    - `tests/unit/test_detector_catalog.py::test_unit_health_21_maps_to_self_heal_and_is_only_self_heal_detector`.
    - `tests/unit/test_autonomy_readonly_closure.py::test_no_autonomy_module_calls_systemctl_restart` (AST).
- **Activation.** WP4's code takes effect at merge.
  - **Arming.** The watchdog is armed by AUT-1's activation of its unit lines, which must pass this WP's config test first. AUT-1 also writes the `--mark-buildside-restart` marker before the recorder restart (§4 preamble).
  - **Checks after AUT-1's activation.**
    - `systemctl --user show breezy-quote-tape -p Type,NotifyAccess,WatchdogUSec,WatchdogTimestamp` shows `notify`, `all`, W, and a `WatchdogTimestamp` that advances between two reads.
    - The next health pass reports no `watchdog_disarmed`.

        - `::test_bwrap_self_probe_detects_registry_wide_bind`: a fixture that binds `registry/` instead of `registry/demand/` fails the probe with `direction=negative_registry` and exits 3.
      - **AB10:** `::test_demand_stage_stdout_carries_invocation_id_under_real_bwrap` runs the production bwrap shape with `--unshare-pid` in a scratch user unit (`claude-aut6-*`, removed afterwards; no `breezy-*` unit is touched). The stage prints both demand lines, and the test asserts that each line's `_SYSTEMD_INVOCATION_ID` equals the unit's `InvocationID`. If it fails, `DEMAND_STAGE_SUMMARY_SINK` is set to `file`, and `::test_demand_stage_summary_written_to_stage_status_file_when_journal_unattributed` must pass instead.
  - The `--stage demand` entry point and its `AUT6_WRITE_AUTHORITY` row join the WP1 closure test.

### AUT-6.WP6 — Intraday producer (including `fee_schedule` and the marker read). Wave 1, after WP5, WP5b and AUT-5a's resolver stub and bootstrap rows

- **Verify-first STOP:** (1) recorder live-root max `ts_init` gap over 7 in-window periods → `TAPE_MTIME_MAX_S`; (2) which instrument ids carry Depth10 for NO legs (own rows or the YES-bid complement); (3) ΔRSS of one `order_book_depth10` single-instrument query on the largest instrument-day, fresh child; (4) whether `decode_family_halt_state` exposes a halt timestamp; (5) `/proc/locks` and `/proc/<pid>/stat` readable, with the node's intent flock listed, inside the `#evaluate` row's real bwrap without `--unshare-pid` (r11, E-7a rule 2). As a negative control recorded for E-7, the same read with `--unshare-pid` must **not** list the node's intent flock (r8; the demand stage never reads it); **(6, r4; restated r9) the p99 wall time of the evaluate stage ≤ 90 s, inside its 95 s deadline**; **(7, r4) that AUT-1's writer emits `DetectorEvent(detector=fee_schedule)` on every probe outcome** (else WP6 stops and the gap goes to AUT-1, §5); **(8, r8, E-7)** that the C4 verdict listing and both demand directories are readable inside the bwrap'd demand stage, and that `p99(evaluate) ≤ 90 s and p99(demand) ≤ 10 s (r9, AA1: each stage has its own hard bound); **(9, r9 LOW-r9-3)** that, inside the production bwrap invocation, the webhook host resolves and the CA bundle the production client loads is readable. The resolution check is `getaddrinfo` only, with no request, and its result is recorded as yes or no, never as the host; **(10, r11, E-7a; r12, AF1: the method is replaced)** The plan-time closure measurement (§0 (18)–(21)) is repeated on the WP6 tree after M1, M2 and the `leg_prices` move, with the module-level static walk and the fresh-process `sys.modules` cross-check, never grimp, which counts function-local imports (§0 (18)). Both intraday stages must show no `breezy.adapters` module and no `breezy.strategy.current_rung_hold` package; any hit stops WP6 for review. **(11, r12, AF3)** The snapshot settle rate with the node up. AUT-5's `exec_snapshot`, called with `take_flock=False` as the evaluate stage calls it, copies into a scratch cache directory (never the production cache bind) at the 5-min cadence across at least two trading windows `[17:10Z, 01:00Z)` with the node running, at least 150 attempts. Recorded: attempts, settled attempts, retries used, and the p99 settle time. The settle rate must be ≥ `EXEC_SNAPSHOT_SETTLE_FLOOR = 0.99`; below it, WP6 stops and the design returns to review (§3.3.2). **(r13, AG4)** The run also records the maximum run of consecutive unsettled attempts and gates on it: the maximum must be below N = `EXEC_SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN_STREAK_MAX` = 3, because a run of N is itself a false #25 page. A run of 3 or more stops WP6 and returns the design to review, just as a rate below the floor does. Every copy is deleted after it is used, and no exec-store file is opened for write. Results in `docs/evidence/aut6/WP6_verify_first_<date>.md`.

## 5. Association

| Contract / interface | Direction | Exact item |
|---|---|---|
| C1 (AUT-1) | consume | `DetectorEvent` (incl. `detector=fee_schedule` at every probe outcome, **required by #32**); `DecisionRecord.kind ∈ {TrySubmit, Take}`, `ask_px`, `depth_ref`, `eval_ns`, `p_hat`; `OrderLink.time_in_force`, `side`, `instrument_id`, `client_order_id`; `LifecycleEvent(FILLED)` `px`, `ts_ns`; the Depth10 payload store; `source`, `drill`; `decisions/capture_<family_id>_<date>.jsonl` |
| C1 (AUT-1) | provide | #4 and #5 emit `DetectorEvent(AGREE/DISAGREE/UNKNOWN)` through AUT-1's writer |
| AUT-1 (r9) | consume / require **(r14)** | The recorder's watchdog gate and sender (`recorder_watchdog.classify_recorder_sample`, `sd_notify`) with their call site; the recorder unit's watchdog lines; and the `ExecStopPost=` stop hook that pages each kill through `deliver_with_proof`. All of these follow AUT-6's §3.5 SELF_HEAL contract, which AUT-6's config and contract tests check. **Cross-plan items for AUT-1 r9 as drafted:** X-1 `NotifyAccess=main` → `all`; X-2 add `Type=notify` and send `READY=1` on the first allowed ping; X-3 put the launch-window deferral in `classify_recorder_sample`. Also: a gate for any other daemon before it joins `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS`. AUT-1 restarts nothing. |
| C2 (AUT-2) | consume | `admissible`, `excluded_reason`, `slippage`, `fill_px`, `entry_ask`, `p_at_decision`, `settled_outcome`, `role`, `drill` (#13, #14) |
| C2 (AUT-2) | boundary | AUT-6 owns portfolio-roi's exit semantics; AUT-2 owns the scorer (P6-9) |
| C4 (AUT-5) | provide | `DRIFT`/`HEALTH` for every VERDICT id from `aut6.intraday`, `aut6.daily`, `aut6.health`; `metrics.cause_class`; slot-anchored validity (8 h intraday and health, 26 h daily); hourly refresh; `assumptions ⊆ {no_policy_ruling, drill}` |
| ATTEST (AUT-5) | provide | the intraday schedule row and the §3.4.1 invariant |
| C5 (AUT-5) | consume | the fold (subjects, bound sha, CHAMPION/HALTED, HALTED row `ts_ns`, `halt_cause_class`); the drill marker (directory-descriptor read, §3.4.8); the resolver CLI (WP8) |

## 6. Live-proof protocol

- **Artefact:** `~/.local/share/breezy/evidence/aut6/live_proof_<from>_<to>.json` (WP9), citing the rollups, delivery records, C1, the C4 ids, the C5 export and node-log lines.
- **Window:** 7 consecutive qualifying days (ARCH §5.3), ≥ 5 real fills.
  - Starts no earlier than the day after the AUT-5b ruling is filed.
  - **Named prerequisite (r6, S3; r7, U4):** the ING-2-AMEND2 (`docs/core/PROGRESS.md:52`) removal of the quote-tape-ingest drop-in has landed, so #31 can PASS; until then the §7 "#31 PASS" check cannot be met and the window cannot close.
  - **A day fails** if its rollup is missing or produced before the day ended; `passes_completed < 130` of 144; `passes_unknown > 6`; `max_passes_unknown_streak ≥ 3` without a delivered #29 CRITICAL record that day; or `unexplained_failed_units > 0`.
- **Per action class, one live-path event:**

| Class | Event | Natural or injected | Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|
| ENTRY_VETO | `permit_lapsed` at the daily permit expiry (permit `ttl_s=36000`; 10-02: issued 16:50:40Z, expires 10-03 02:50:40Z). Proof: a C1 `EntryVeto(reason=permit_lapsed)` within 120 s after the boot log's `expires_at_ns`, and its clear at the next boot. | natural | daily, about 02:50Z |
| ALERT | the 15:45Z, 16:30Z or 16:45Z canary `*_canary_d.json`, plus any detector ALERT with its `d` record | natural | three times a day |
| SELF_HEAL **(r14)** | **Natural path:** a real recorder watchdog restart. The chain is: journal unit-failed `UNIT_RESULT=watchdog` on `breezy-quote-tape` → `Scheduled restart job` → a new `InvocationID` → `WatchdogTimestamp` advancing → the #21 verdict, with a delivered CRITICAL from the stop hook (or from #21's fallback). **If none occurs in the window:** the WP4 V-W1 record (the production gate under the deployed directives, on a scratch unit) plus `test_systemd_restarts_silent_notifier_on_watchdog` green at the window sha (O-9). | natural, else mechanism-proven | whenever the recorder stalls |
| DEMOTE | AUT-7b marker naming `DRILL_INJECT` → `aut6.intraday` FAIL verdict → engine DEMOTE (DRILL) → watch-actor `registry_not_champion` veto | injected, live path | once (AUT-7b, D+1) |
| HALT | AUT-7b marker naming `DRILL_INJECT_HALT` → `aut6.intraday` FAIL verdict → engine registry HALT (DRILL; no exec-store write, no freeze) → watch-actor `registry_halted` veto (ARCH C6, §5.3) | injected, live path | once (the same AUT-7b run, D+3) |

- **HALT and gate-proven HALTs.** One AUT-7b run (§5.3 timeline D…D+4) gives AUT-5 and AUT-6 their HALT proofs. The exec-store HALT writers (#17 fee probe, `duplicate_fill`, `ambiguous_exit`) and the INTEGRITY HALT of #15 (with its demand file) are **gate-proven only** (RED→GREEN), because a live injection would freeze the lineage or the venue. AUT-6 claims 3 on the drill HALT plus those gate proofs.
- **Evidence class:** "machinery proven, edge unproven".
- **ETA** (about 5 fills a day; zero-fill days extend): ARCH-0 ≈ 10-08; AUT-6 Wave 1 merged ≈ 10-16; AUT-5b ruling ≈ 10-28, window from ≥ 10-29; AUT-7b DEMOTE and HALT ≈ 11-03 → 11-06 (≥ 4 trading days). **Full live proof ≈ 2026-11-05 to 11-10**, about 11 weeks before the 2027-01-25 KILL, **provided the ING-2-AMEND2 (`docs/core/PROGRESS.md:52`) drop-in removal has landed by the window start** (r6, S3; r7, U4: no ING-2 date exists, so this is a condition, not a deadline); a later removal moves the window start to its landing date + 1.

## 8. Risks and failure modes

- **R-1 Alert storm.** Write-on-change plus hourly refresh, per-detector dedupe, ≤ 6 drain attempts per redeliver run, at-least-once delivery (a duplicate page after a SIGKILL or a stale-claim reclaim), accepted over losing one.
- **R-2 Memory, the 31 GB (30.67 GiB) host, and the ingest drop-in.** Own-lock AUT-6 units total 1088 MiB; daily producer 3G. The uncommitted quote-tape-ingest drop-in (`zz-memory-containment-TEMPORARY.conf`, 14G measured 10-03) breaks ARCH §5.2 while it stands; #31 and #24 name it daily, WARN until 2026-10-16 and CRITICAL from then (r6, S3). Upstream: ING-2-AMEND2 (`docs/core/PROGRESS.md:52`): the drop-in is removed after the ≤ 2G `memory.peak` proof (or committed through review, per ARCH §5.2), a build-side decision, never the operator's. No owner or deadline is recorded there and AUT-6 invents none; 2026-10-16 is only AUT-6's escalation constant (r7, U4). The live proof waits on it (§6). The nightly study stops for the node, never the reverse.
- **R-3 Shared venv and concurrent agents.** Exact interpreter, no `uv`, no `git stash`, per-agent scratchpads, `PYTHONPATH` per worktree, worktrees fast-forwarded first. Tests use a fake `systemctl`/`journalctl` on PATH; the only real `systemd-run` calls are WP4's V-W1 and its integration test, on `aut6test-*` scratch units (r14). Other agents' `run-*` transients are counted only if they reference the repo or a registered worktree path; then they are Breezy jobs (`TRANSIENT_ADHOC`).
- **R-4 Self-heal harm (r14).** systemd restarts only `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS`. That set is literal and never includes trade, the supervisor, the engine or the dead-man. A restart happens only after the gate has withheld pings for W. While the event loop runs, it never happens inside the launch window, because the gate defers it. Every restart is paged (#21). A false stall restarts a healthy recorder; that risk is bounded by V-W2's W and the empty-listing rule, the restarts back off from 30 s to 480 s, and each kill is paged (AUT-1's stop hook, with #21 as the fallback).
- **R-5 Self-heal reach (r14).** Only the recorder is a member, until AUT-1 files a counter for another daemon. A failed or timed-out oneshot waits for its next timer elapse, with a delivered #22 alert (r6, S1, unchanged). A recorder whose event loop hangs inside the launch window is restarted at once, because there is no ping thread to defer it. A crash is handled the same way today.
- **R-6 False DEMOTE.** Persistent variants only; UNDERPOWERED never acts; INFRA never charges the model budget; ARCH Z20 accepted.
- **R-7 Dirty shadow-parity baseline.** #16 pages CRITICAL on a parity mismatch in AUT-4's `eval_replay_path` verdict once AUT-4's `eval_offline` producer is live (r13, AG1; R-39); a finding for AUT-4 and AUT-2.
- **R-8 Statistical capacity.** #13 and #14 stay UNDERPOWERED for weeks; #9 and #12 are `INCONCLUSIVE NO_INPUT` until 14 forward FINAL days exist (about 2026-10-17 plus the CLI lag). The live proof depends on none of them.
- **R-9 No receiver absence rule.** Path B, one pinned egress key, reviewed.
- **R-10 Records on sandboxed units.** The `ReadWritePaths` test checks configuration only (E-7; r8). #23, which catches an unwritable or full record root, is the binding check.
- **R-11 Off-loop worker.** Entries are on disk and fsynced before enqueue; a wedged or killed worker loses nothing, because the claim goes stale and the redeliver unit or the dead-man reclaims it; the canary and #5 catch a dead path.
- **R-12 Outbox fsync on the loop thread.** Two small local fsyncs per proof-bearing alert; WP1b measures the p99 and `test_try_submit_latency_independent_of_webhook_latency` gates it.
- **R-13 KILL 2027-01-25.** If TERMINAL: no sender; #6 reports `family_not_champion`; the fold is non-empty, so `fold_ok=true` and verdicts go to `_host/`; unit health and the canary continue.
- **R-14 Log-line dependence.** The permit line and `FeeDriftProbeActor: RUNNING` verified in the 10-02 logs; the BREEZY-NWS subscribe error whitelisted.
- **R-15 INTEGRITY demand lifted by an engine rejection (r4).** Under ARCH U13 the engine archives a producer demand whose verdict it rejects (for example `unpinned_producer` or `subject_unbound`), which lifts that veto. The CRITICAL has already been delivered with proof, and the rejection is itself a defect alert; an engine-accepted INTEGRITY FAIL HALTs and freezes the venue.
- **R-16 Journal cursor loss.** Vacuum past the cursor ⇒ day-start fallback with `cursor_reset=true`; retention covers ≥ 7 days today. The cursor never advances on an UNKNOWN pass or before records are written.
- **R-17 Node log volume.** One node log reached 1.16 GB (10-01). AUT-6 bounds its own reads (4 MiB tail, WP3b streaming); the volume itself is a finding for AUT-1 and AUT-5a (L-29), not silenced.
- **R-18 #15 false positives.** Hidden or sub-snapshot liquidity and clock skew are the residual causes (§3.4.6); each would HALT with an INTEGRITY freeze cleared only by build-side incident handling (W15). Fail-closed over availability, consistent with ARCH Z20.
- **R-19 Ingest stall blinds #15.** Pending fills become #25 CRITICAL at 6 h; AUT-1's `capture_gap` and #7 page earlier.
- **R-20 `fee_schedule` input gap (r4).** If AUT-1 does not emit the probe's `DetectorEvent`s, #32 is INCONCLUSIVE and rollback, the drill start and ROOT_ADMIT refuse: fail closed, caught by WP6 verify-first (7) before activation.
- **R-21 CRITICAL reservation (r5, F5).** 16 reserved slots mean a WARN storm can fill only 112 entries; a CRITICAL storm past 128 still overflows, which #23 reports. Accepted: 128 undelivered CRITICALs already means the webhook is down, which the canary and #5 catch.
- **R-22 Frame-gap INCONCLUSIVE (r5, F9).** A real defect fill inside a > 2 s frame gap is not halted by #15. Accepted: availability over a HALT on an unknown book; such days are listed in the WP9 report, and a repeated defect would surface in a later fill with a dense book.
- **R-24 Rotate explains a same-interval crash (r6, S4): closed in r8 (Y1).** Every invocation that ended in the interval must have been stopped by systemd before a rotate run may explain anything, so a crash in the rotate's interval, in either order, is `daemon_crashed`. Residual: a journal vacuumed past an ended invocation leaves the change unexplained (a CRITICAL), which fails toward the page.
- **R-25 Build-side marker misuse (r7, LOW-2; narrowed in r8, Y1).** A marker can no longer downgrade a crash (Y1); a mistaken marker can downgrade only an unrelated systemd-initiated stop and start within 300 s of it from CRITICAL to WARN. The page is still delivered with proof, the marker is a named file with its commit, and AUT-1's `recorder_stale`/`capture_gap` still see a real outage.
- **R-26 (r7): withdrawn in r14.** There is no probe timer, and SELF_HEAL waits on no ruling: the watchdog is armed from WP4's activation. The live-proof change is O-9.
- **R-27 bwrap scope (r8, E-7 rule 4; r11, E-7a).** From r11 every AUT-6 unit and both intraday stages run under bwrap, each confined to its row's binds (§3.1.1). Residual (E-7a rule 5): the node and its outbox writer, the operator CLIs, the notifier's fallback line and `breezy-discovery-pull` run unconfined, and the evaluate stage and the health unit share the host pid namespace. A bwrap regression in either direction is caught at the next start of any wrapped unit by its self-probe, which pages CRITICAL; the intraday verdict path still stands when only the demand stage fails.
- **R-28 Journal dependence of Y1 and Y3 (r8).** Explaining an `InvocationID` change, and telling a member's watchdog ending from a crash (r14), both need the ended invocation's journal entries. When they are missing, the change pages CRITICAL. That fails toward the page, never toward a silent pass.
- **R-29 Per-command `TimeoutStartSec` (r9, AA1).** systemd alone would let the two-stage intraday unit run 240 s. The bound rests on coreutils `timeout -k 2` around each stage, and systemd's 120 s is a per-command backstop only. A wrapper that failed to kill would show up as a late engine pass, #26 heartbeat age and a #22 `timeout` result. It would fail loud, never silently.
- **R-30 Stage-status race (r9, AA2).** A manual evaluate run that overlaps a timer run can overwrite `.aut6-intraday.stage_status` with another invocation's id. The demand stage then reads `unrecorded` and exits 4, which is a #22 page. This fails toward the page.
- **R-31 `O_TMPFILE` dependence (r10, AC1; r11, AE2).** The demand write needs `O_TMPFILE` on the filesystem under `registry/demand/`. The data root is ext4 (§0 (14)), and the link was measured under bwrap (§0 (8)). r11 removes the fallback. If a future filesystem or kernel lacks `O_TMPFILE`, the self-probe and the open fail INTEGRITY with a delivered CRITICAL and no demand file is written, while the verdict and engine path of §3.3.3 still stands. WP5b verify-first repeats the check on the production tree.
- **R-32 Allowlist false failures (r10, AC6).** A legitimate new read call fails the gate until it is added to `AUT6_READ_ALLOWLIST` through review. That fails loud at build time and never silently at run time. From r11 this is a lint (E-7a rule 4): write scope is enforced by the bwrap rows, and the allowlist's completeness is not a READY criterion.
- **R-33 (r10): withdrawn in r14**, together with the self-heal mode file.
- **R-34 Snapshot reads (r11, AE5; r12, AF3, E-8a).** Each intraday pass copies the exec store (about 4 MB, §0 (15)) into its cache bind, with `take_flock=False`. A copy that cannot settle while the node writes makes the reading detectors INCONCLUSIVE for that pass. Three consecutive unsettled passes (`EXEC_SNAPSHOT_UNKNOWN_STREAK_MAX`, about 15 min) give a #25 CRITICAL. WP6 verify-first proves a settle rate ≥ 0.99 with the node up, so false pages run at about 3 × 10⁻⁴ a day. A result never reads as PASS, never counts toward H and never authorises a widening (E-8a), and the copy is deleted after each use.
- **R-35 Health inside bwrap (r11, E-7a).** The health unit's systemd and journal reads go through the D-Bus socket and journal files under the read-only root. If V-6 (WP3) fails, WP3 stops. If a later change breaks them, the reads error and the pass is UNKNOWN, which #29 and the dead-man see within about 30 min.
- **R-36 The notifier's fallback runs unwrapped (r11, E-7a).** The fallback line exists so that a broken wrapper cannot silence the daily's failure page. It runs only the notifier closure, which is under the lint. **(r12, AF4)** It never re-delivers when a `delivered` marker exists. When only `attempted` exists, it leaves the page to the durable outbox and journals `fallback=outbox_pending`. When neither exists, it pages and adds `autonomy_notifier_wrapper_failed`, so a broken wrapper is never silent and a webhook outage never produces a false `wrapper_failed`.
- **R-37 Missing lock file (r11, E-7a).** Lock files are pre-created at activation. A deleted lock file makes every run of that unit fail INTEGRITY with a CRITICAL until it is recreated: loud, never silent.
- **R-38 Moves of live code (r12, AF1).** M1, M2 and M3 move code that the node, the supervisor and the FQ strategy load. Each move is byte-identical, with old names bound to the moved objects; identity tests pin it; the AF5 NO-side suites and the ladder, halt and supervisor suites run unedited before merge; and a test-side monkeypatch grep found none. Residual: a future test that patches an old alias would no longer reach the moved code. The pre-move grep rule (§3.1.2) catches that at build.
- **R-39 #16 depends on AUT-4 (r12, AF1; r13, AG1).** #16 cannot be recomputed inside an adapter-free closure (§0 (20)), so it consumes AUT-4's C4 `eval_replay_path` verdict. Until AUT-4's `eval_offline` producer is live (earliest 2026-11-23), #16 is `NO_INPUT`, so r11's R-7 "#16 pages now" holds only from then. The required class "forecast and feature drift" stays covered by #9, #10 and #11 (§3.2), and the live proof depends on none of them (R-8). The gap cannot go silent, for three reasons (§3.4.4):
  - once the producer is live, or from `AUT4_REPLAY_EXPECTED_BY` = 2026-11-30 even if it never goes live, a missing verdict pages WARN and then CRITICAL after 3 days;
  - a stale or foreign verdict is a #25 CRITICAL;
  - a missing C4 directory after go-live is INTEGRITY.

  Residual: if AUT-4's ETA slips, #16 pages from 2026-11-30 until AUT-4 lands or the constant is moved in a reviewed commit, which errs toward the page.
- **R-40 `READY=1` coupling (r14).** A `Type=notify` unit with no READY sender fails every start with `Result=timeout`. `Restart=always` retries it and #22 pages, but capture stops. Three guards prevent it. AUT-1 activates its unit lines and its call site together. V-W3 sizes `TimeoutStartSec`. And `test_watchdog_member_execstart_reaches_a_notify_sender` refuses `Type=notify` on a member whose entry module does not reach the gate.
- **R-41 False watchdog on a quiet or empty feed (r14).** The gate pings for an empty listing while discovery reloads stay fresh. W is AUT-1's (600 s in r9), inside AUT-6's [120 s, 600 s] bound, and V-W2 checks it against the measured 7-day gap. It is never below 120 s, which is above the 60 s Nautilus idle-timeout reconnect. A gap above 300 s stops WP4 (V-W2). Residual: a quiet but subscribed market that goes silent for longer than W restarts the recorder. Each such restart is paged (#21), so it is never silent.
- **R-42 `OnFailure=` semantics (r14).** The target runs on every watchdog failure (§0.1 (25)), not only at exhaustion. The notifier therefore branches on `SubState` and never double-pages a pending restart. Two failures inside one notifier run may be coalesced by systemd (a stated reading); the health pass counts both.
- **R-43 The recorder has no native cap (r14, O-8).** `StartLimitIntervalSec=0` stays, so a persistent stall restarts the recorder about every W + 480 s, and each kill is paged CRITICAL (AUT-1's stop hook, #21 fallback), and AUT-1's storm veto applies at 3 kills in a trading day. Accepted over the alternative: a finite start limit re-opens the 2026-09-09 6.7 h tape loss. If O-8 is ruled the other way, WP4 adds `StartLimitBurst=3`/`StartLimitIntervalSec=86400`, and exhaustion pages through `OnFailure=` but leaves capture dead until a build-side `reset-failed`.
- **R-44 The rotate under `Type=notify` (r14).** The 09:00Z rotate's blocking `try-restart` now completes at `READY=1`. V-W4 checks the rotate's bound; if it is too short, the rotate unit fails and #22 pages it, while the recorder still starts.
- **R-23 `journal_blind` false UNKNOWN (r5, F2).** A unit failed so long ago that its entry was vacuumed makes passes UNKNOWN until it is `reset-failed` (a one-time, journaled build-side step, as at WP3 activation). That is loud by design: an unexplained failed unit must not be silently accepted.

## 9. Binding-constraint compliance

- **Nautilus immutability:** nothing in Nautilus is touched; #15 calls the native `order_book_depth10` query; the off-loop sink is a stdlib thread, the `src/breezy/ingest/nws_actor.py` pattern.
- **Caps:** no AUT-6 module reads or writes the operator-reserved controls (ARCH `test_autonomy_never_reads_or_writes_operator_controls`). No value is assigned.
- **allow_short:** untouched (stays `False`); #15 uses `leg_prices` read-only, which encodes "every order is a BUY of one leg".
- **NO-SEND:** `test_execution_egress_firewall_guard` unchanged; alert egress stays the single `alerts.env` key (`test_autonomy_alert_egress_not_widened`); Path B, if needed, is one reviewed, host-pinned key that widens nothing else. **(r8, E-7)** The bwrap'd demand stage keeps network only for outbox delivery. It never sees `~/.config/breezy` credentials (`--tmpfs ~/.config`) and has no import path to the venue adapters (`test_demand_stage_has_no_import_path_to_venue_adapters`), so NO-SEND is unchanged. **(r11, E-7a)** The same holds for every AUT-6 row: credentials hidden, network for delivery only, and no import path to the venue adapters. The evaluate stage's only adapter import, `leg_prices`, moves unchanged to `domain` (WP6), and `test_evaluate_stage_has_no_import_path_to_venue_adapters` pins it. **(r12, AF1)** The halt decode, the lock-holder parser and the ladder numerics also move to adapter-free modules, and the measured evaluate and daily closures hold no `breezy.adapters` module (§0 (21)). `test_aut6_entry_points_import_no_adapter_and_no_crh_package` pins every entry point.
- **Master enablement and permit:** `permit_lapsed` and #6 read an expiry integer or log line only (AST test); no AUT-6 code imports the permit issuer, the enablement env or the live-orders gate; **(r14)** the watchdog is armed only on `WATCHDOG_DAEMON_UNITS`, never on the trade node, the supervisor, the engine or the dead-man (`test_watchdog_units_are_literal_and_exclude_trade_supervisor_engine`), and no autonomy code restarts any unit (`test_no_autonomy_module_calls_systemctl_restart`).
- **PREREG via ruling:** action classes, cause classes and `n_min` come from the AUT-5 ruling (or the pinned `DEFAULT_RESTRICTIVE_CLASS`, restrictive only); the catalogue is a proposal; the INTEGRITY demand write is restrictive only and ARCH-ratified (P6-12). AUT-6 verdicts charge no α (ALPHA); no AUT-6 detector reads an outcome row of the frozen holdout (HOLDOUT, C4.1).
- **Safety tests never weakened:** existing alert, egress, firewall, settlement and contract tests pass unedited; `tests/unit/test_alerts_env_deploy.py` gets an additive entry only; the one named expected skip is bounded and self-expiring; `scripts/analysis/discovery_set_equality.py` keeps its API and its tests; r3's `test_no_study_window_intersects_stop_launch` is superseded by ARCH's stricter `test_no_unit_overlaps_launch_window`, never relaxed. **(r11)** Two planned fallback tests, never written, are withdrawn with the fallback (AE2) and replaced by the stricter INTEGRITY test; the `leg_prices` move leaves its existing tests unedited; the shared `breezy-study-failed@` notifier is not changed. **(r12)** The four AF1 moves leave every existing test unedited, including the supervisor, halt, ladder and NO-side suites, and none of them edits `decision.py`. **(r14)** The tests deleted in r14 belonged only to the deleted executor, probe and mode machinery (§R14, D-list); none was ever written. None of them is a safety, settlement, contract, NO-SEND or egress test. ARCH's `test_self_heal_unit_allowlist_is_literal_and_excludes_trade`, `test_self_heal_cap_survives_process_restart` and `test_restart_window_resets_before_launch` are replaced through §ERRATA-REQUEST ER-2, never dropped silently. The recorder's incident-backed `StartLimitIntervalSec=0` is kept (O-8).
